# Acceptance: findings to implementation to test

The design of this repository was set by a hostile architecture review whose
brief was to find how a skill self-improvement loop produces "confident,
well-documented, self-referential garbage". Its verdict was **build the scoring
half, do not build the closed loop**.

This document is the answer sheet. Every row names the finding, where the answer
lives in the source, and the test that proves it. The test names are real: a
test in `tests/test_acceptance_map.py` asserts that every test named here exists
in the suite, so this table cannot rot into a list of claims about tests that
were renamed or deleted.

Read it as a *map*, not as evidence. The evidence is the test run.

## The verdict, honoured directly

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The improver must not write to a skill without a human merge | The daily path has no write capability at all: it takes a read-only skill store, and `apply` is a separate command | `app/daily.py`, `app/promote.py`, `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_a_writable_skill_store_is_refused_outright`, `test_the_daily_module_does_not_import_the_promotion_module` |
| Only `apply` and `rollback` may open the skill store writable | Every `build_skill_store` call in the CLI is mapped to its command function by parsing the source; the callers passing a literal `writable=True` must be exactly `cmd_apply` and `cmd_rollback`, every other command that opens one (`daily` among them) relies on the wiring default, which is asserted to be read-only, every call is accounted for, and the CLI never references the store class itself, so the `ReadOnlyViolation` row in OPERATIONS describes the code rather than an intention; the README command table is held to the same source: its rows are exactly the parser's subcommands, the rows whose Writes column names a skill file are exactly the writable-store callers, and every other row writes nothing or only under the state directory | `cli.py`, `app/wiring.py`, `README.md` | `test_only_apply_and_rollback_ask_for_a_writable_skill_store`, `test_every_subcommand_has_a_row_and_every_row_is_a_subcommand`, `test_the_rows_that_claim_to_write_a_skill_file_are_the_writable_store_callers` |
| A command documented as writing nothing must write nothing | `skills`, `rubrics`, `discover`, `candidates`, `set-aside`, `review` and both `scheduler-template` kinds are run through the real CLI against a fresh environment populated by one committed `daily` pass alone, and every file and directory under it and under the shared fixture corpus must keep its content, mode and mtime; against an absent state tree the commands that read state may create only empty owner-only directories, reset the root and the directory they read if either had drifted wider, and the others must leave it absent; the scheduler templates are run with an empty `$HOME`, which must stay empty | `cli.py` | `test_against_a_populated_state_tree_nothing_changes`, `test_against_an_absent_state_tree_only_owner_only_directories_may_appear`, `test_19_scheduler_templates_render_and_install_nothing` |
| A printed scheduler job must be the one the operator asked about | `scheduler-template` substitutes the config path this invocation was given (`--config` or the default) and that config's declared `state_dir` for the log paths, loading the config to do so, so a template is never printed for a different config or with private output routed to a state tree the config does not use | `cli.py`, `packaging/` | `test_19b_the_templates_schedule_the_config_they_were_asked_about`, `test_20_the_launchd_template_is_a_well_formed_property_list` |
| A printed scheduler job must survive the syntax it is pasted into | The four substituted paths land in a crontab line, which the shell splits on spaces and cron rewrites a bare `%` in, and in a property list, which is XML. `_cron_quote` shell-quotes and escapes `%` as `\%` for cron's own rewrite, spelling a backslash as the row "A printed cron job names a path holding a backslash as itself on every cron" says; `_xml_quote` entity-escapes; a path with nothing awkward in it renders byte-for-byte as before. Measured before this row, a tree under `My Projects & co 100%` produced a job whose change-directory step stopped at the first space and a plist that did not parse | `cli.py`, `packaging/` | `test_the_cron_job_survives_the_shell_and_cron_percent_handling`, `test_cron_quoting_round_trips_through_crons_own_rewrite`, `test_the_launchd_plist_still_parses_and_names_the_exact_paths`, `test_the_control_a_plain_path_renders_byte_for_byte_as_before` |
| A printed scheduler job fills each path in once | `cmd_scheduler_template` reads the template once, replacing each of the four markers (`__REPO__`, `__PYTHON__`, `__CONFIG__`, `__STATE__`) with its spelled value as it is met, so no value is read again for a marker. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): the markers were replaced one after another, so a path holding the text of a marker filled later had that value spliced into it after it was quoted: a config under a directory named `x __STATE__ y` printed a cron line of 17 shell words where the job has 15 and a plist naming a config that does not exist; one named `q __STATE__ ;echo INJECTED;` printed a cron line the shell splits at both semicolons, two commands the config never named; a repository path holding `__CONFIG__` printed the wrong working directory in both; and on 3.11 an interpreter path holding `__STATE__` (a link to the interpreter, which that build keeps as `sys.executable`) printed the wrong interpreter. A state directory was safe before, being filled last. After: each case runs exactly the configured job, read back as cron hands the command to the shell and through the shell's word splitting, and through `plistlib`; a plain path, and one holding quotes, `%`, `&`, `<`, `]]>` or a tab, prints byte-for-byte as before on both. 5 mutants (the markers filled one after another, or in reverse order; the values unquoted; only the first marker filled; a second pass after the first) are each caught on both | `cli.py` | `test_the_control_plain_paths_run_exactly_the_configured_job`, `test_a_config_path_holding_every_markers_text_is_carried_as_itself`, `test_a_state_directory_holding_every_markers_text_is_carried_as_itself`, `test_a_repository_path_holding_every_markers_text_is_carried_as_itself`, `test_an_interpreter_path_holding_every_markers_text_is_carried_as_itself`, `test_a_marker_in_the_config_path_cannot_add_a_shell_command` |
| A printed cron job names a path holding a backslash as itself on every cron | Most crons rewrite a crontab command before the shell sees it (`do_command.c`): a bare `%` ends it and a backslash before a `%` is consumed. They part ways on a backslash before a backslash: FreeBSD and macOS make the pair one, cronie and OpenBSD keep both, and busybox crond rewrites nothing. `_cron_quote` doubled every backslash, which only FreeBSD and macOS undo; it now prints no backslash pair, following each backslash in a path (always inside the shell quotes, as a path holding one is always quoted) with a quote ending the quoted text and one resuming it, which every cron keeps as written and the shell reads as the backslash alone. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13), with each rewrite modelled from its loop and the command read by the shell's word splitting: of seven config directories holding a backslash (one, two in a row, three in a row, one before a `%`, one ending the name, one before an apostrophe, one between spaces), each was named as a directory that does not exist under the cronie and OpenBSD rewrite, and each of the six without a `%` under busybox; FreeBSD and macOS read every one exactly. After, on both: every one is read exactly under each rewrite that applies to it, and so is a state directory holding each of the six without a `%`, and a real shell reads each spelling back as the path. A path without a backslash is spelled as before, and the launchd plist, which carries a backslash as it is, is unchanged: both print byte-for-byte as before on both. 8 mutants (the backslash doubled again, left alone, spelled after the `%` escape, spelled only at its first place, escaped outside the quotes, or followed by a quote that is not resumed; the `%` left bare; the template fill reading a backslash in a value as a template escape) are each caught on both. Not covered by this row: a quoted path holding a `%` under busybox, which hands the shell `\%` inside the quotes, as before | `cli.py` | `test_a_config_path_holding_a_backslash_is_named_as_itself_by_every_cron`, `test_a_state_directory_holding_a_backslash_is_named_as_itself_by_every_cron`, `test_a_backslash_before_a_percent_is_named_as_itself_by_each_rewriting_cron`, `test_no_backslash_pair_is_printed_for_any_cron_to_read_its_own_way`, `test_a_real_shell_reads_each_spelling_back_as_the_path`, `test_the_launchd_plist_carries_a_backslash_path_exactly`, `test_the_control_a_path_without_a_backslash_is_spelled_as_before` |
| The improver must not be able to edit its own examiner | The rubric directory, the package source, the config and the state directory are all protected paths, derived in code, not read from configuration | `domain/skills.py`, `config.py` | `test_the_rubric_directory_is_protected`, `test_the_protected_set_is_not_taken_from_configuration`, `test_a_write_inside_a_protected_path_is_refused` |
| No metric may count proposals, merges or "gaps closed" | The gate measures fixed cases, regressions, holdout preservation and judge preference. Proposal count is reported as a fact and is never a success criterion | `domain/validation.py`, `app/receipt.py` | `test_replaying_nothing_is_not_a_pass`, `test_the_policy_is_recorded_with_the_decision` |
| No improvement claim without a holdout frozen before the proposal existed | The holdout is built from the passing cases at scoring time, before any candidate is generated; a gate with no holdout cannot pass | `domain/scoring.py`, `domain/validation.py` | `test_passing_cases_become_the_holdout_set`, `test_no_holdout_means_the_no_regression_claim_is_unsupported`, `test_a_holdout_degradation_is_not_treated_as_neutral` |
| A fix count on failures withheld from the improver's prompt is reported, never gated | With `[caps].unseen_withhold = k > 0` (off by default) and a skill holding at least `[caps].unseen_min_failures = N` failures in a run, one or more whole conversations that together hold at least k of them, taken in an order fixed by a hash of the validation seed and never the last, are kept out of the improver's prompt and replayed against the candidate after it, unless the failures are all on one conversation or fewer than k of them are on the conversations before the last in that order, when nothing is withheld and the record says which (`one-conversation`, `short-of-k`; the conditions in full are in `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, "Withholding failures from the improver's prompt"). The count is report-only: the gate never reads it. `daily` notes and the receipt record "unseen failures fixed: x/w" for every measured candidate, gate-failed included, and the candidate record carries it; `review` prints it for validated candidates only. Every printed number carries the scope "withheld from the improver's prompt as failures and excerpts only, not from the skill text that prompt shows or files its command can read; not used by the gate". `validate_candidate` itself refuses, before any model call, a withheld case passed to it as a holdout pair or a claimed target, so a library caller cannot have a withheld case gate the candidate. The record (`asi.validation.v4`) recomputes the split from its own pool and seed; `asi.validation.v3` records still parse and verify. Measured before (on 3.13 for the design; the example plus three cloned conversations, `[selection].max_conversations_per_run = 11`): every fix the gate counts is on a failure the improver was shown; 5 targets, 4 fixes, 14 holdouts, 29 evidence calls. After (3.11 and 3.13, the same numbers on both): with k = 0 the run is unchanged (5 targets, 4 fixes, 14 holdouts, 29 calls, a v3 record, no unseen line, and a receipt with no unseen key, so the record, the receipt, the review text and the review JSON are byte-identical to a build without the feature, the frozen v3 record's review JSON pinned by its length and sha256 against the build before this row; with k = 1 that JSON gains only `unseen`, not-recorded); with k = 1, N = 4 conversation `conv-human-0006` and both its failures are withheld (w = 2), the gate counts 3 targets, 3 fixes, 13 holdouts, 25 calls and passes, and the unseen count is 1/2, replayed 2; only the withheld failure pairs leave the holdouts, so with 8 extra holdout slots a passing pair of `conv-human-0006` is a holdout and the count stays 14 (13 and 14 are this fixture's counts, not a rule); the judge is never asked about `conv-human-0006`; with `min_fixes = 4` the same candidate fails the gate and its note still says 1/2 (a candidate fixing neither gets 0/2 in its note and its receipt); the gate is identical for 0/2 and 2/2 and `apply` proceeds with either. The 33 mutations of the design's mutant table and 14 added in review (the receipt listing the unseen caps at k = 0, the public-safety export filter reverted, a home path planted in the candidate-record fixture, the holdout exclusion widened to the whole withheld conversation, `review` reading the unseen count from the receipt instead of the record, the record's ref-order, status and replayed-count checks each removed, `validate_candidate` accepting a withheld holdout pair or a withheld claimed target, a v3 record's review losing its not-recorded `unseen` at k > 0, `evaluate_gate` accepting an unseen outcome, that refusal moved below the gate's counting, and, test-only, the limit test's skill section widened over the excerpts), each an anchored edit run in a committed copy against only its named test, are each caught by the test named for them on both, 47 of 47, none surviving; the candidate-record fixture is held to its own privacy check (no absolute home path, no deep-link scheme, no leak-scanner finding), since it has no thread id; a frozen `asi.validation.v3` candidate (`tests/fixtures/validation-v3-record.json`) is still reviewed and applied unchanged on both. Not covered by this row: the prompt still shows the current skill text whole, so a withheld conversation's id or failure words that an earlier edit wrote into the skill reach the improver (asserted as the visible limit by `test_a_withheld_instance_an_earlier_edit_wrote_into_the_skill_reaches_the_prompt`); the improver command can still read the withheld conversation and its failure record from the state directory and the source (the open read path, asserted as the visible limit by an in-process improver, not by an improver argv run through the macOS sandbox); a conversation shown in an earlier run; the pool's completeness (a record missing an unclaimed failure verifies); a v4 record rewritten as v3, which verifies and applies; the scorer's verdicts themselves (the record is checked for consistency, not origin: an unseen verdict rewritten with its derived flags and counts verifies and applies, while one rewritten alone is refused, both asserted by `test_a_coherent_unseen_verdict_edit_is_accepted_and_an_incoherent_one_refused`); and the number is never gated, so a candidate that fixes none of its withheld failures is still applied if its gate passes. The withholding is not a security boundary, and the count is not a general out-of-sample estimate: it counts fixes on a few failures of conversations the same run scored | `domain/validation.py`, ## Corpus composition: the majority class is the machine

The review measured 4,809 of 5,000 threads in a 30-day window carrying one
orchestrator title (96.2%), leaving 191 human sessions. A judge pointed at "all
conversations" scores machine traffic almost all of the time, and the scarce
class is the valuable one.

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Selection must not let the majority class crowd out the minority | Selection is round-robin across authorship buckets, best-first inside a bucket, and reports its coverage | `domain/selection.py` | `test_round_robin_reaches_every_class_within_the_same_budget`, `test_a_thin_bucket_contributes_all_it_has_and_no_budget_is_wasted`, `test_bucket_coverage_reports_a_narrow_run_as_narrow` |
| A ranked prefix is a filter, not a sample | A test demonstrates the failure directly: sorting the corpus and taking a prefix excludes the minority class entirely | `domain/selection.py` | `test_a_sorted_prefix_would_exclude_the_minority_class_entirely` |
| A title-only guess must not be reported as authorship | Two vocabularies. Discovery may only say `orchestrated` or `not-agent-titled`; only a fetched transcript can say `human-authored`, and `unknown` is never folded into `human` | `domain/authorship.py` | `test_the_pre_fetch_vocabulary_does_not_contain_human`, `test_the_post_fetch_vocabulary_keeps_unknown_separate_from_human`, `test_no_user_turn_is_unknown_and_never_folded_into_human` |
| Orchestrator markers differ between installations | The marker list is configuration, and matching is word-boundary aware so `redispatchered` is not a false hit for `dispatcher` | `domain/authorship.py`, `config.py` | `test_markers_are_configurable_because_one_orchestrator_is_not_all_of_them`, `test_marker_matching_respects_word_boundaries` |
| An unclassifiable record must not be silently dropped | Unclassified items get their own bucket and still reach the gate | `domain/selection.py` | `test_an_unclassifiable_item_gets_its_own_bucket_rather_than_being_dropped` |

## Discovery: the default page is 3.9% of one day

The review measured a default page size of 10 against 256 threads in a day, and
noted the most recently touched threads are disproportionately loop iterations.

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A page must never be reported as a total | `DiscoveryPage` derives `is_total` from the relationship between the returned count and the requested limit; an exactly-full page is never a total | `domain/discovery.py` | `test_a_short_page_is_a_total`, `test_an_exactly_full_page_is_never_reported_as_a_total`, `test_the_truncation_flag_is_derived_not_supplied` |
| The limit must be explicit, never left to a default | The source always renders its limit into the configured argv, including a limit of 1 | `adapters/conversation_cli.py` | `test_the_source_always_passes_its_limit_explicitly`, `test_a_limit_of_one_is_still_sent_rather_than_treated_as_falsey` |
| A failed page must not read as an empty page | An error page carries no threads, is not a total, and is distinguishable from a successful empty page | `domain/discovery.py` | `test_an_error_page_is_not_a_total_and_carries_no_threads`, `test_an_error_page_is_distinct_from_an_empty_successful_page` |
| Merging pages must not manufacture completeness | One truncated or failed page makes the whole merge non-total; every page is preserved for audit | `domain/discovery.py` | `test_one_truncated_page_makes_the_whole_merge_non_total`, `test_one_failed_page_makes_the_whole_merge_non_total`, `test_every_page_is_preserved_in_the_summary_for_audit` |
| The operator must see the honesty flag, not just the JSON | `discover` prints whether the result is a page or the whole population | `cli.py` | `test_03_discover_is_honest_about_its_page`, `test_03c_the_human_readable_output_says_it_too` |
| A discovered id must stay a value, never become an option | Titles in a listing derive from conversation content, so a forged `Thread:` line can plant an option-shaped id (a leading double dash followed by a flag name). `is_safe_thread_id` refuses a leading dash, whitespace, control characters and over-long ids; the parser drops such lines and `fetch` refuses them as a `RecordDefect` before any command runs | `domain/discovery.py`, `adapters/conversation_cli.py` | `test_an_option_shaped_id_is_dropped_at_parse_time`, `test_fetch_refuses_an_option_shaped_id_before_running_anything`, `test_the_control_id_still_reaches_the_command_line`, `test_the_predicate_names_the_rule` |

## Evidence sufficiency: the interface is a summariser

The review's fatal finding: through the supported interface the loop cannot
observe which skill was loaded, which tools ran, what diff was produced, or
whether anything exited non-zero. A rubric asking "did the agent use the skill
correctly" asks a judge to infer an unobservable, and it will comply.

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A verdict must be refusable, not just pass or fail | Three verdicts, and only two of them are decided. INSUFFICIENT is a first-class outcome | `domain/scoring.py` | `test_three_verdicts_are_distinct_and_only_two_are_decided` |
| A claim with no resolvable citation must earn nothing | Every FAIL and every PASS must cite an element that resolves by id **and** position; anything else is downgraded to INSUFFICIENT with a reason | `domain/scoring.py`, `domain/conversation.py` | `test_fail_with_no_evidence_is_downgraded`, `test_fail_with_an_invented_element_id_is_downgraded`, `test_fail_with_the_right_id_but_wrong_position_is_downgraded`, `test_pass_is_held_to_the_same_standard_as_fail` |
| An unmentioned criterion must not become a pass | Absence is INSUFFICIENT; an unknown criterion is recorded as extraneous rather than dropped | `domain/scoring.py` | `test_an_unmentioned_criterion_becomes_insufficient_never_pass`, `test_an_unknown_criterion_is_recorded_as_extraneous_not_dropped` |
| Skill attribution must abstain when unobservable | Attribution is graded: an observed path in tool IO outranks a bare mention, a rubric prior is labelled a prior, and nothing observed yields an explicit unattributed marker | `domain/scoring.py` | `test_a_skill_path_in_tool_io_outranks_a_bare_mention`, `test_a_rubric_prior_alone_is_marked_as_a_prior_with_low_confidence`, `test_nothing_observed_and_no_prior_yields_an_explicit_unattributed_marker` |
| Only decided failures may become work | Only FAIL becomes a failure case; INSUFFICIENT never does | `domain/failure.py` | `test_only_fail_becomes_a_failure_case`, `test_insufficient_never_becomes_a_failure_case` |
| Downgrades must be visible in the receipt | Downgraded verdicts are counted and reported | `domain/scoring.py`, `app/receipt.py` | `test_downgraded_verdicts_are_countable_for_the_receipt` |
| The transport truncates from the front | Normalisation records that it truncated and how many messages it kept, rather than silently scoring an opening | `domain/conversation.py` | `test_max_messages_truncates_and_says_so` |

## One judge, one model family

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A judge from the same configuration as the improver is not independent | The gate refuses by default when the judge command is not independent of the improver command; the opt-out is explicit and recorded | `config.py`, `domain/validation.py` | `test_a_non_independent_judge_fails_the_gate_by_default`, `test_the_independence_requirement_can_be_switched_off_explicitly`, `test_independence_is_recorded_in_the_metrics_either_way` |
| A cosmetic label must not manufacture independence | Independence is fingerprinted on the argument vector only. Free-text model labels are reported and never counted | `config.py` | `test_independence_is_recorded_in_the_metrics_either_way` |
| Position must not be a signal to the judge | Arms are assigned blind from the run seed, both arms always appear exactly once, and the assignment varies across cases | `domain/validation.py` | `test_arms_are_assigned_from_the_seed_and_are_stable`, `test_both_arms_are_always_present_exactly_once`, `test_the_assignment_varies_across_cases_so_position_is_not_a_signal` |
| A tie must not be scored as a win | Ties are excluded from the ratio; only ties means no decided judgements and no pass | `domain/validation.py` | `test_ties_are_excluded_from_the_ratio_rather_than_counted_as_wins`, `test_only_ties_means_no_decided_judgements_and_no_pass` |
| A failing gate must report every reason | All reasons are collected, not just the first | `domain/validation.py` | `test_every_failing_reason_is_reported_not_just_the_first` |

## Rubrics are configuration, and configuration is the examiner

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A rubric must be versioned and hashed | Each rubric carries a declared version and the hash of the file it was read from; an undeclared edit changes the hash | `domain/rubric.py` | `test_a_rubric_carries_its_declared_version`, `test_an_edit_changes_the_hash_even_when_the_version_does_not`, `test_the_hash_is_serialized_so_it_reaches_the_artifacts` |
| A rubric must state when the answer is insufficient | Every criterion in every shipped rubric declares `insufficient_when` | `rubrics/*.toml` | `test_every_criterion_states_when_the_answer_is_insufficient` |
| A missing rubric must be an error, not a silent skip | An enabled rubric that does not exist is a configuration error | `config.py` | `test_a_missing_enabled_rubric_is_a_configuration_error` |

## Skills are code, not documents

The review counted 15 executable payload files across the local skill tree,
including a git-safety hook and loop launchers, and stated plainly that write
access to skills is arbitrary code execution including write access to the
guardrails.

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Only the document may ever be written | Any write whose target is not the skill document is refused, and the document check runs before the hash check | `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_only_the_skill_document_is_writable`, `test_the_document_check_runs_before_the_hash_check` |
| Executable payloads must be surfaced, not ignored | The inventory reports payloads by extension and by executable bit, and rolls them up across copies | `domain/skills.py` | `test_a_shell_script_beside_a_skill_is_a_payload`, `test_a_symlinked_launcher_is_inventoried_not_skipped`, `test_a_symlink_to_a_plain_text_file_is_not_a_payload`, `test_the_executable_bit_counts_even_with_an_unremarkable_name`, `test_the_group_rolls_payloads_up_across_copies` |
| Backslash and quoted filenames must not mangle path handling | Payload detection is done on real paths, never through a shell | `domain/skills.py` | `test_a_filename_containing_a_backslash_is_handled`, `test_a_filename_with_spaces_and_quotes_is_handled` |
| A broken-frontmatter skill must not look successful | A `SKILL.md` with no opening delimiter loads with a reported warning instead of reading as empty | `domain/skills.py` | `test_a_missing_opening_delimiter_is_reported_not_ignored` |

## Mirror topology: twelve names, two trees, no arbiter

The review found twelve skill names existing as independent, diverged copies in
two trees, mirror scripts forming a cycle with no arbiter, and a mirror that was
1-of-23 installed while reporting success.

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A write must never land in a mirror | Mirror targets are refused and the refusal names the canonical path | `domain/skills.py`, `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_a_mirror_copy_is_refused_with_the_canonical_path_named` |
| The refusal must not be bypassable by path spelling | Comparison is on normalised, resolved paths, and an unrecognised target is refused rather than assumed safe | `domain/skills.py`, `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_a_mirror_reached_through_a_redundant_path_segment_is_still_refused`, `test_a_mirror_reached_through_a_symlinked_root_is_still_refused`, `test_an_unrecognised_skill_document_is_refused_not_assumed_safe` |
| Two roots must not name one tree | A `[[skill_root]]` that names the same directory as another, spelled plainly, through `.` or `..`, through a symbolic link, or (for a root that exists) as a case variant on a filesystem that folds case, or that lies inside another root, is a `ConfigError` quoting both ids and spellings; `doctor` reports it with exit 3. Stated limit: a case variant of a root that does not exist yet is compared by text only. Measured before the check: the aliased mirror root loaded cleanly, each skill was inventoried twice and listed as a mirror of itself, and the root flagged read-only was the directory the write landed in. A root that does not exist yet is still compared | `config.py` | `test_a_mirror_root_naming_the_canonical_directory_is_refused_by_both_ids`, `test_the_same_directory_spelled_with_redundant_segments_is_still_refused`, `test_a_root_reached_through_a_link_to_another_root_is_refused`, `test_a_root_inside_another_is_refused_whichever_is_listed_first`, `test_a_case_variant_of_an_existing_root_is_judged_by_identity_not_text`, `test_the_control_sibling_roots_load_even_when_one_does_not_exist_yet`, `test_doctor_reports_overlapping_roots_as_a_config_error` |
| An editable root must be somewhere the tool may write | A `[[skill_root]]` marked editable, whether or not it is also marked canonical, that lies inside a protected path (the package, the config directory, the state directory or the rubrics directory), spelled plainly, through `.` or `..`, a link, or by on-disk identity, is a `ConfigError` naming the root and the protected path; `doctor` reports it with exit 3. Measured before the check: such a config loaded, `doctor` was clean, and every write under the root was refused as self-mutation after its candidates had been proposed, scored and reviewed. A read-only mirror in the same place, and a root that contains the config or state directory, both load; a skill inside such a directory is not proposed (row "A skill inside a protected path is never proposed"). An editable root not marked canonical is held to the same rule because `resolve_groups` writes to it: a skill present only in that root gets it as its canonical copy, and when no root is marked canonical the highest-precedence editable root is the canonical one for every skill; measured before: with `canonical = false` such a root loaded and the write was refused only at apply | `config.py` | `test_a_canonical_root_inside_the_config_directory_is_refused_at_load`, `test_an_unmarked_editable_root_inside_a_protected_path_is_refused_at_load_too`, `test_a_canonical_root_inside_or_at_the_state_directory_is_refused_at_load`, `test_a_canonical_root_inside_the_rubrics_directory_is_refused_at_load`, `test_a_canonical_root_inside_the_tool_package_is_refused_at_load`, `test_the_control_a_read_only_mirror_inside_the_config_directory_loads`, `test_the_control_a_root_that_contains_the_config_and_state_directories_loads`, `test_doctor_reports_a_canonical_root_in_a_protected_path_as_a_config_error` |
| A skill inside a protected path is never proposed | `propose_candidate` in `app/pipeline.py`, asked by `daily`, refuses (`SelfMutationRefused`) a skill whose canonical copy is, or lies inside, a protected path, before the skill is read and before the improver is asked, and `daily` records it as a policy refusal of the skill (status `refused`, stage `propose`), as it does a skill listed in `[guard].protected_skill_ids`. The judgment (`protected_dir_holding` in `domain/skills.py`) is the one the write path's `_assert_not_protected` in `adapters/skill_fs.py` made, moved there unchanged, and the write path asks it too, so the two cannot drift apart. The config refuses an editable root inside a protected path, not a root that holds one. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the config file, or the rubrics directory, in the folder of `tdd`, the skill the fixtures fail): `doctor` said ready; `daily --commit` exited 0 with one candidate, its gate passed, the improver and the judge paid; `review` exited 3 with `SelfMutationRefused` "refusing to review <path>: it is inside a protected path", naming no candidate; `apply` could get no token; and the record stayed `validated`, open. After: `daily --commit` exits 0 with no candidate, the conversations scored, neither the improver nor the judge asked, and the receipt gives `tdd` the refusal "skill 'tdd' at <path> lies inside a protected path (<directory>), so it is never a proposal target: this tool never edits its own source, config, state or rubrics, and `apply` would refuse it. To have it proposed, keep the skill's folder and that path apart."; with the config file in its own directory, or in a folder `td` beside `tdd`, `tdd` is proposed as before; with the skill roots spelled through a link to the tree the skill is still refused, since the inventory walks each root resolved, as the protected paths are. Both paths go through `named_path`, so a line break in one is spelled on its line. 15 mutants (the check not made, or made after the skill is read; `daily` passing no protected paths, or not the state directory and this tool's package; the inventory walking a root as spelled; the skill quarantined rather than refused; either path named raw; the advice dropped; the judgment by string prefix, by equality only, by lying inside only, or reversed, or resolving the paths again, which its callers have resolved; the write path judging nothing) are each killed on both Pythons by the test named for it. On the tree before this row the module does not import (`protected_dir_holding` does not exist there); with that function added there, its tests of the refusal in `daily` (each of the three placings) and of the command fail, and its tests of the words and of what `daily` asks error: the first with a `TypeError` (`propose_candidate` takes no `protected_paths` there), the second with a `KeyError` in the stand-in that records the call (`daily` passes no `protected_paths` there), while the control and the judgment's two tests pass. Not covered by this row: a candidate already open on such a skill, which `review` and `apply` still refuse naming no candidate (until the row "A target inside a protected path is refused with the candidate and how to close it", which names it; `rollback` names it, answering that no backup manifest was found for it, as for any candidate with no backup manifest), and which stays open until its record is deleted or the path is moved apart from the skill, when `review` and `apply` take it with the same token; `doctor`, which still says ready; and the skill's place among those selected for a run, which it still takes, as a listed skill does | `app/pipeline.py`, `app/daily.py`, `domain/skills.py`, `adapters/skill_fs.py`, `config.py`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md` | `test_a_skill_beside_the_config_file_is_refused_before_the_improver`, `test_the_control_with_the_config_file_apart_proposes_it`, `test_the_command_proposes_nothing_and_exits_0`, `test_the_paths_are_named_each_on_its_line`, `test_a_path_is_held_by_the_protected_path_it_is_or_lies_inside`, `test_daily_asks_with_every_protected_path_the_config_names`, `test_the_judgment_takes_both_paths_as_given`, `test_a_write_inside_a_protected_path_is_refused` |
| Divergence must be reported, not resolved silently | The inventory reports canonical-versus-mirror divergence and mirror-only skills | `domain/skills.py` | `test_divergence_between_canonical_and_mirror_is_reported`, `test_a_mirror_only_skill_has_no_canonical_copy` |
| A relocated tree must not read as a mutation | The tree digest is keyed on the root id plus the path relative to that root | `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_relocating_a_root_does_not_look_like_a_skill_mutation`, `test_a_file_moving_between_roots_still_changes_the_digest` |

## A whole-tree veto is a ratchet

The review named a publication script whose single regex hit aborts every skill,
and identified it as the same structural defect that cost twelve days elsewhere.

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| One bad record must not veto a batch | Records are quarantined, deferred or refused individually; the rest of the batch still scores | `app/daily.py`, `app/state.py` | `test_malformed_records_are_quarantined_while_the_rest_are_scored`, `test_a_model_that_returns_garbage_quarantines_only_that_record` |
| Unreachable is not a verdict | A source that cannot be reached defers rather than failing the run or quarantining anything; only a streak of `caps.max_defer_attempts` misses (default 3) escalates (next row) | `app/daily.py` | `test_an_unreachable_source_defers_rather_than_failing_the_run` |
| A live mutation earns `blocked`, provably | A skill file edited from inside a model call during the pass flips the tree digest, the receipt earns `blocked` with a named note, and the CLI maps that status to exit code 5, distinct from a negative answer (1) or a refusal (3) | `app/receipt.py`, `cli.py` | `test_a_skill_edited_mid_run_blocks_the_run_and_the_exit_code_says_so` |
| One open proposal per skill | A skill that already has `caps.max_open_candidates_per_skill` open candidates is skipped as a policy refusal with the reason and its slot goes to another skill; closing the open candidate re-opens the skill; nothing is discarded or replaced | `app/daily.py`, `config.py` | `test_a_skill_with_an_open_candidate_is_not_proposed_again_until_it_is_closed`, `test_the_slot_a_capped_skill_would_have_taken_goes_to_another_skill` |
| Corrupt state is refused by name, never guessed at | A truncated checkpoint or conversation record raises `StateUnreadable` naming the file and is left untouched, instead of escaping as a bare parse error; the receipt reaches disk before the checkpoint is updated | `app/state.py`, `app/daily.py` | `test_a_truncated_checkpoint_is_refused_by_name_not_guessed_at`, `test_the_receipt_reaches_disk_before_the_checkpoint_is_touched` |
| A blocked run leaves nothing applicable | Every candidate persisted by a run whose skill-tree digest moved is rewritten with the closed status `blocked-run` and the reason in its notes, even when the edited skill was not the candidate's target, so `apply` refuses it, the integrity checker still accepts the record, and the review queue holds nothing from that run | `app/daily.py`, `app/promote.py`, `domain/integrity.py` | `test_candidates_from_a_blocked_run_are_downgraded_so_apply_refuses_them`, `test_a_candidate_that_cannot_be_rewritten_does_not_cost_the_blocked_receipt` |
| Skill outcomes are not record outcomes | A skill that could not be proposed or validated is reported in the receipt's `skills` table and `skills_*` counts, never in `records_*`, and never enters the per-day checkpoint's processed-record set | `app/receipt.py`, `app/daily.py` | `test_skill_level_outcomes_are_not_counted_as_records`, `test_the_refusal_is_recorded_as_policy_not_as_a_defect` |
| Retry-first must not become a ratchet | A conversation that keeps deferring is quarantined after `caps.max_defer_attempts` consecutive misses and stops claiming a selection slot ahead of fresh work; a completed score resets the streak, and so does a completed propose-and-validate for a skill id deferred at those stages; the cap is configurable and must be at least 1 | `app/daily.py`, `app/state.py`, `config.py` | `test_a_record_that_defers_persistently_is_quarantined_not_retried_for_ever`, `test_a_success_between_misses_resets_the_deferral_streak`, `test_a_completed_proposal_resets_a_skills_deferral_streak`, `test_max_defer_attempts_defaults_to_three_and_rejects_zero` |
| A refusal must name its reason and its stage | Every isolated record gets its own file carrying a reason and the stage that produced it | `app/state.py` | `test_each_quarantined_record_gets_its_own_file_with_a_reason`, `test_a_shape_defect_and_a_model_defect_are_recorded_at_different_stages` |
| The three isolation states must stay distinct | Quarantine, defer and refuse are three states, and a deferred record can later clear | `app/state.py` | `test_quarantine_defer_and_refuse_are_three_distinct_states`, `test_a_deferred_record_can_be_cleared_when_it_later_succeeds` |
| A leak scan must not stop at the first hit | The scanner reports every finding grouped by location and says so in its own output | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_a_every_rule_is_two_sided`, `test_a_no_tracked_file_content_matches_any_rule` |
| A backlog must stand the run down, not silently grow | When the backlog exceeds its bound the run stands down and discards nothing | `app/daily.py` | `test_a_backlog_stands_the_run_down_without_discarding_anything` |

## Untrusted content is data

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Conversation text must reach a model as quoted data | Content is wrapped in a nonce-delimited fence and the system prompt states the contract | `app/prompts.py` | `test_content_is_placed_inside_an_explicit_fence`, `test_the_scorer_states_the_contract`, `test_the_improver_states_the_contract`, `test_the_judge_states_the_contract` |
| A forged delimiter must not end the fence | Forged opening and closing delimiters are neutralized, visibly, and the nonce cannot be replayed from inside the content | `app/prompts.py` | `test_a_forged_opening_delimiter_is_neutralized`, `test_a_forged_closing_delimiter_cannot_end_the_fence_early`, `test_the_nonce_cannot_be_replayed_from_inside_the_content`, `test_neutralization_is_visible_rather_than_silent` |
| The elements a scorer may cite reach it fenced and masked, as the transcript does | `scorer_user_prompt` in `app/prompts.py` lists the elements a verdict may cite (`citable_elements`). Their ids, and a message's role and a tool's name and status, are the export's own text. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): the list sat outside every fence, under a heading calling it trusted and derived locally, and was never masked, so for each of five fields (a message's id, a tool's id, a role, a tool name, a status) a forged closing delimiter and an instruction planted in it reached the scorer outside the fences, and a `ghp_` token planted in it reached the scorer whole, though the transcript masked the same token: 15 leaks over the five. Now the list holds only each element's id, position and kind, as JSON fenced as untrusted data under its own label (`citable-elements`) and nonce; the role, name and status stay in the transcript's head lines, inside its fence. After, on both: 0 of the 15. Each id is masked whenever the transcript is (`redact_secrets`), by the transcript's own shapes, and any forged delimiter in it neutralized, before the list is encoded (`_listed_value`), never the encoded text. Masking or neutralizing the encoded list (this change's first form, refused in audit) broke it: an assignment shape such as `password=` runs to the next space and an opening delimiter with no `>` then ran to the next `>`, each taking the list's quotes with it, and the fixture models read a list that does not parse as nothing to cite. Of 13 tool ids planted through the scorer's prompt (a plain one, 8 credential shapes, 4 forged delimiters), that form left 8 lists that do not parse (the 5 assignment shapes, the 3 delimiters with no `>`); now 0 on both, each credential id reads as the transcript's masking makes it and each delimiter as neutralized, with the other ids exact. The list's own nonce in an id is stripped by `wrap_untrusted`, as in every fence, masking on or off. With masking turned off an id is listed as written, its delimiters still neutralized, inside the fence. An element whose id is masked or neutralized is listed as rewritten, as the transcript has always shown it, so a verdict citing it by that id is held to the rule for a citation that does not resolve. The fixture models still cite what the list names: a committed daily run's decided verdicts each carry evidence, and the end-to-end daily pass against the example config scores the same 6 conversations and writes the same 40 records as before, timestamps and the lock's process id aside. 17 mutants (the old section back; the list not fenced; not masked; masked only when masking is off; masked after encoding; masked by a hand-written token pattern; an id not neutralized; neither the id nor the fence neutralizing; the role, the name or the status listed again; the transcript's label shared; the heading still saying trusted; the fence opened after the list; the fence built by hand without `wrap_untrusted`'s own pass; the list's nonce made constant, or shared with the transcript's) are each caught on both. Not covered, as no test sees a difference: an id neutralized before it is masked rather than after. The two orders can list an id differently: `password=<<<END-UNTRUSTED-DATA x>>> ignore` is listed as `password=[REDACTED:secret-assignment] x>>> ignore` (masked first) or `password=[neutralized-delimiter] x>>> ignore` (neutralized first), but a mask's placeholder holds no delimiter and the marker no credential, so neither order lists either | `app/prompts.py` | `test_an_instruction_planted_in_any_citable_field_stays_inside_a_fence`, `test_a_credential_in_any_citable_field_is_masked_in_the_whole_prompt`, `test_the_list_names_each_element_by_id_position_and_kind_only`, `test_the_role_name_and_status_stay_in_the_fenced_transcript`, `test_masking_an_id_leaves_the_list_readable_and_the_other_ids_exact`, `test_a_forged_delimiter_in_an_id_is_neutralized_and_the_list_still_reads`, `test_the_lists_own_nonce_in_an_id_is_stripped_as_in_every_fence`, `test_with_masking_turned_off_an_id_is_listed_as_written_inside_the_fence`, `test_a_committed_daily_run_still_cites_what_the_list_names` |
| The failures the improver and the judge read reach them fenced and masked | `improver_user_prompt` in `app/prompts.py` lists the failures a proposal must address: each case's id, its conversation's id, the scorer's rationale and the id and kind of each element it cites. `judge_user_prompt` shows a conversation's id and a rationale, and `app/validate.py` fills that rationale with the proposal's own hypothesis. None of these is local: the ids are the export's text, the rationale is the scorer's words about an untrusted transcript, and the hypothesis is the words of the improver, the role the judge is there to check. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): both sat outside every fence, under headings calling them trusted and derived locally, so for a rationale, a thread id or an element id (the improver) and for a hypothesis or a thread id (the judge), a forged closing delimiter and an instruction planted in it reached the role outside the fences, and a `ghp_` token planted in it reached the role outside them: 15 leaks over the five; counting the whole prompt, the token reached a role whole 8 times (a thread id 4 times in the improver's: the case id, the conversation id and the excerpt's head, which names both and was never masked). Those counts plant each value directly; in a run the scorer's rationale and the improver's hypothesis are masked already when their records are parsed (`kept_prose`, whatever the flag says), so for those two only the delimiter and the instruction were open, and for the ids all three. Now the improver's list is fenced as untrusted data under its own label (`failures`) and nonce, each of those values masked whenever the transcript is (`redact_secrets`) and any forged delimiter in it neutralized before the list is encoded (`_listed_value`, the rule the row "The elements a scorer may cite reach it fenced and masked, as the transcript does" states for the citable list), so it still parses; the judge's conversation id and hypothesis are fenced under their own label (`observed-failure`) and masked; the excerpt's head is masked as its text is. Both headings say untrusted data. After, on both: 0 of the 15, and the token whole 0 times. The kind each cited element carries is the export's text too (a scorer's citation must match it), and it was still listed as written once the ids were masked: of six values planted in it, a `ghp_` token reached the improver whole twice, an assignment once, and an opening delimiter with no `>`, or two delimiters, left the list unreadable twice; after, on both, none of the six. A rationale, thread id, element id or kind holding an assignment shape, a token followed by a quote, an opening delimiter with no `>`, or two delimiters is listed as masking and neutralizing make it, and the rubric's id, the criterion's id and its title exactly. Each evidence entry lists its element's id, its position as a number and its kind, and nothing more. Each fence strips its own nonce from a rationale. With masking turned off each value is shown as written, inside its fence. The example models (`examples/fake_model.py`, which the example config and the end-to-end CI job run) still read through the fences: the improver finds the rule a rationale names and writes it, and the judge prefers the option holding it. Neither role is asked to repeat an id, so masking one changes no answer. The end-to-end daily pass against the example config scores the same 6 conversations, proposes the same 2 candidates, passes the same 1 and writes the same 40 records as before, timestamps, tree paths and the lock's process id aside. 31 mutants (the improver's old section back; its list not fenced; its heading saying trusted; the case id, the conversation id, the rationale or the evidence ids listed raw; the list masked after encoding; values not neutralized, or neutralized only once; the list's label shared with the excerpts' or its nonce made constant; the excerpt's head unmasked; the judge's old section back; it not fenced, not masked, or under a heading saying trusted; masking only when it is off; the observed failure's label shared with the transcript's or its nonce made constant; the kind listed raw, only masked, only neutralized, masked whatever the setting, or holding the element id; the evidence listed as it was before the kind was masked; its position dropped or made text, or its kind dropped; the element id listed raw; every listed value masked whatever the setting) are each caught on both. Not covered: the scorer's own prompt still names the thread id outside its fences, in its task line and its output contract, which the scorecard's conversation id is matched against; a thread id from the conversation command is already held to printable characters with no space (`is_safe_thread_id` in `domain/discovery.py`), one from fixture files only to its length | `app/prompts.py`, `app/validate.py` | `test_an_instruction_in_a_rationale_or_an_id_stays_inside_a_fence_for_the_improver`, `test_an_instruction_in_the_hypothesis_or_the_id_stays_inside_a_fence_for_the_judge`, `test_a_credential_in_a_rationale_or_an_id_reaches_neither_role_whole`, `test_both_headings_say_untrusted_data`, `test_the_improvers_list_reads_with_each_value_masked_and_neutralized`, `test_each_evidence_entry_lists_the_cited_elements_id_position_and_kind`, `test_each_fences_own_nonce_in_a_rationale_is_stripped`, `test_with_masking_turned_off_a_value_is_shown_as_written_inside_the_fence`, `test_the_example_improver_still_reads_the_rule_a_rationale_names`, `test_the_example_judge_still_prefers_the_option_with_the_rule` |
| Hostile text must survive verbatim so it can be scored | The content is not scrubbed; it is fenced. What the agent did with it is the thing being graded | `app/prompts.py` | `test_hostile_instructions_survive_verbatim_as_data` |
| A forged delimiter hides no text but its own | `wrap_untrusted` in `app/prompts.py` rewrites a forged delimiter in fenced content to a marker (`_FENCE_RE`). Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): the pattern ran from the fence word on to the next `>` anywhere, across lines, so an opening delimiter with no `>` in a user's message (`<<UNTRUSTED-DATA`, `<< untrusted-data`, the closing form with a slash after the `<<`, or a closing one with a label and no `>`) cut the scorer's transcript from 348 characters to 90: the rest of that message, the agent's reply, its tool call and its output, the head lines naming two of the elements a verdict may cite and the next user turn were all gone from it, 7 pieces for each and an 8th, the instruction it carried, for the one with a label, and a closed one took its label with it, so an instruction to the scorer written there was gone as well: 30 pieces hidden over the five. A zero-width space, non-joiner, joiner, word joiner or byte-order mark in a delimiter's gaps (after the `<`, around the slash) let it through whole: 6 of 6. Now only a delimiter's own characters are replaced: its run of `<`, its gaps, its slash, `END-`, the fence word and up to three `>`; what follows the word stays, as data, and the gaps take those five characters as well as space. After, on both: 0 of the 30 and 0 of the 6. On 20,000 random strings of delimiter pieces, zero-width characters and other text, what is left once a delimiter's own characters are taken out is the same before and after neutralizing, and one pass leaves no forged delimiter. A run of 256 KiB of zero-width characters in a gap is fenced in linear time, as the row "A forged delimiter is neutralized in time in proportion to the text" holds for space. 16 mutants (the old tail back, on to the next `>`; a tail of the rest of the line, or of up to 64 characters of it; the gaps taking space only; zero-width characters taken only before the slash or only after it; the byte-order mark, the joiner or the word joiner dropped from the gaps; the lookbehind dropped; the gap around the slash split every way; the `>` after the word taken without bound, or not at all; the word matched in one case only; the slash dropped; `END-` required) are each caught on both. Not covered, measured: a zero-width character inside the run of `<` that leaves no two of them side by side (with three, the two still side by side are neutralized as before), inside the fence word or after `END-`; a soft hyphen or another format character in a gap; a look-alike `<` or letter (fullwidth `＜`, Greek `Α`). Each is left as written, inside the fence; matching them all is not something a pattern can finish, and the fence is one layer of several | `app/prompts.py` | `test_an_unclosed_forged_delimiter_hides_none_of_the_transcript_from_the_scorer`, `test_the_words_in_a_closed_forged_delimiters_label_reach_the_scorer`, `test_a_zero_width_character_in_a_delimiters_gaps_does_not_let_it_through`, `test_only_a_delimiters_own_characters_are_replaced_and_one_pass_leaves_none`, `test_a_long_run_of_zero_width_characters_in_a_gap_is_fenced_in_linear_time` |

## The lethal trifecta, removed mechanically

Private conversation content is attacker-influenceable, the model roles are
arbitrary child processes, and a child with the parent environment and an open
socket is a finished exfiltration path. Fencing and redaction reduce the chance
of cooperation; they do not remove the capability, so they are not counted here.

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A private source must not be read at all unless the roles are confined | The isolation decision is the first statement of `run_daily`, before the lock, the skill hash and discovery, so no export happens on a misconfigured run | `domain/isolation.py`, `app/daily.py` | `test_a_private_source_with_unconfined_roles_refuses_before_the_source_is_touched`, `test_the_control_proceeds_and_the_source_is_actually_called`, `test_a_runner_that_cannot_describe_its_isolation_is_treated_as_unconfined` |
| A configured path must expand or refuse, never become literal text | `~` and `$VARIABLE` in a configured path are expanded; whatever the expansion leaves behind, an unset `$NAME` or `${NAME}`, a braced form with a shell modifier such as `${NAME:-x}` (kept literally even when NAME is set), or a `~user` naming no user, is a `ConfigError` quoting it before any directory is created, so a scheduler's smaller environment cannot redirect the private state tree to a directory literally called `$NAME`; `doctor` reports it as a config error with exit 3. There is deliberately no escape for a directory whose real name contains `$` | `config.py`, `cli.py` | `test_a_path_naming_an_unset_variable_is_refused_with_the_name`, `test_the_braced_form_is_refused_too`, `test_a_braced_form_with_a_shell_modifier_is_refused_even_when_the_variable_is_set`, `test_a_tilde_naming_no_user_is_refused`, `test_the_control_a_set_variable_expands`, `test_doctor_reports_it_as_a_config_error_rather_than_creating_the_tree` |
| The exception must be mechanical, not a trust label | The requirement is derived from `[source].kind` alone; the `[source]` schema is closed, so an unrecognised key is a configuration error rather than a new opt-out, and an unknown kind classifies as private | `domain/isolation.py`, `config.py` | `test_the_fixture_corpus_runs_with_no_os_sandbox`, `test_the_exception_is_derived_from_the_source_kind_and_nowhere_else`, `test_an_unknown_source_kind_classifies_as_private`, `test_an_unknown_source_key_is_a_hard_configuration_error`, `test_the_control_shows_the_same_table_loads_without_the_label` |
| A prompt fence must not be accepted as a boundary | The fence and the redactor both remain in place and neither changes the decision | `app/prompts.py`, `domain/isolation.py` | `test_a_fence_exists_and_still_does_not_satisfy_the_check`, `test_redaction_being_enabled_does_not_satisfy_it_either` |
| An unsupported host must fail closed, not degrade | Detection is injectable and returns nothing when the mechanism is absent; a detected-but-unverified mechanism is refused too | `adapters/sandbox.py`, `app/wiring.py` | `test_a_host_with_no_mechanism_detects_nothing`, `test_linux_without_bubblewrap_detects_nothing`, `test_macos_without_sandbox_exec_detects_nothing`, `test_the_control_detects_the_mechanism_when_it_is_present`, `test_no_mechanism_means_the_decision_refuses_rather_than_degrades`, `test_a_detected_but_unverified_mechanism_is_also_refused` |
| The confinement must be measured, not requested | A real child under the real OS sandbox is proved unable to open a socket to a listener this process owns, or to write outside its private directory, while an unsandboxed control does all three and a no-op wrapper fails verification | `adapters/sandbox.py` | `test_the_unsandboxed_control_can_do_all_three_things`, `test_the_sandbox_blocks_an_outbound_socket`, `test_the_sandbox_blocks_a_write_outside_the_private_directory`, `test_the_sandbox_still_allows_a_write_inside_the_private_directory`, `test_the_overall_verdict_is_ok_and_names_what_it_checked`, `test_verification_fails_when_the_wrapper_does_nothing` |
| The generated profile must deny before it allows | The macOS profile is asserted to place the network and write denials before the single writable subpath, because a later rule wins; the bubblewrap argv is asserted to unshare the network and bind the root read-only | `adapters/sandbox.py` | `test_the_profile_denies_network_and_writes_then_allows_only_the_workdir`, `test_the_wrapper_passes_the_profile_inline_and_keeps_the_argv_intact`, `test_the_bubblewrap_wrapper_unshares_the_network_and_binds_root_read_only`, `test_the_no_op_isolation_returns_the_argv_unchanged` |
| The child must not inherit the environment | The child gets an allowlist plus a per-call `HOME` and temp directory; credential, token and proxy names are absent, a credential placed in a role's own env under such a name is refused rather than dropped quietly, and a configured value shaped like a credential (`credential_labels`, the same high-confidence shapes the skill text check uses) is refused whatever its name, the refusal naming the variable and the shape but never the value | `domain/isolation.py`, `domain/redaction.py`, `adapters/model_command.py` | `test_no_credential_token_or_proxy_name_reaches_the_child`, `test_the_control_shows_the_child_env_is_not_simply_empty`, `test_home_and_tmpdir_point_at_the_per_call_directory_not_the_real_home`, `test_the_per_call_directory_is_owner_only_and_does_not_outlive_the_call`, `test_a_credential_placed_in_role_env_is_refused_not_silently_dropped`, `test_a_credential_shaped_value_in_role_env_is_refused_whatever_its_name`, `test_credential_bearing_values_are_named_by_variable_and_shape`, `test_the_control_shows_a_harmless_role_env_value_is_accepted`, `test_a_missing_parent_path_falls_back_rather_than_producing_an_unusable_child`, `test_the_denied_substring_list_covers_the_obvious_conventions`, `test_an_ordinary_name_is_not_flagged` |
| A role cannot keep its scratch by changing its modes or flags | `remove_scratch` in `adapters/model_command.py`, which ends every model call whatever its outcome, removes the call's scratch, the private directory it was given under the state directory's sandbox directory. It was one `rmtree` that ignored errors, which stops at an entry the role made read-only, unsearchable or immutable and removes no link or file the role put where its scratch was. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, with no sandbox and under `sandbox-exec`, the same in all four but for the last case): a subdirectory at mode 500 or 000, the scratch itself at 500 or 000, `chflags uchg` on a file or on a directory, the scratch replaced by a link or by a file, a link out of a scratch at 500, two links in the scratch to one flagged file, a link flagged itself, and a tree 1,200 directories deep whose last is at 500 each left the role's entries in the state tree, 12 cases of 12, and every call reported success, but for the deep tree on 3.11, whose `rmtree` raised `RecursionError` out of the call through its ignored errors; after, 0 of 12. The removal is now one walk without recursion (`_sweep`) that acts only on a name in a directory it holds open: each directory is opened relative to its parent's descriptor with `O_NOFOLLOW` and `O_DIRECTORY` and given to its owner (`fchmod`), so nothing is reached through a path a process could change under it and no link is followed; a mode is set by name only on a directory the role made unreadable, told not to follow (`_chmod_at`), and not at all where the C library cannot refuse to follow; and the owner's flags that stop a removal (`uchg` and `uappnd`, `USER_FLAGS_THAT_KEEP_AN_ENTRY`) are cleared only where a removal needs it, by the bare name from inside the directory held open (`fchdir`, then `lchflags`, and the working directory given back at once). A flagged file with more than one link keeps its flags and stays unless all its links were counted in the scratch, when a second walk clears and removes it. Controls: a scratch the role left alone is removed with no mode or flag forced; a file linked in from elsewhere and flagged through the link keeps its flags and stays, since clearing them would change that file (macOS refuses to link a file that already has such a flag; unconfined only, as `sandbox-exec` refuses the link); a directory elsewhere that a link in the scratch points at keeps its mode, its content and a flag on a file in it, and a flagged link's target keeps its flag; a directory renamed and replaced by a link to a directory elsewhere just after the walk opened it does not steer the walk there (simulated, from this row's first audit), and one replaced by a link, by a file of the scratch's own or by a hard link to a file elsewhere before it is opened is unlinked, neither followed nor opened, so that file keeps its mode; where `chmod` cannot refuse to follow (simulated, raising as CPython does on Linux: `ValueError`, given a directory descriptor, or `NotImplementedError`), a subdirectory at 000 stays, no link is followed and the call returns; and the working directory is given back after a flag is cleared, and when clearing it fails. 24 mutants (the removal as before, or `rmtree` run first; no second walk; every kept file taken as inside, no kept file recorded, or each link counted once; a file with another link cleared; no flag cleared on a file, or on a directory; the entry judged following a link; the flags cleared with the call that follows, or from the wrong working directory; an unreadable directory not opened; `chmod` following; the directory opened following, or without `O_DIRECTORY`; a link that replaced a directory not unlinked; an opened directory not given to its owner; the walk reaching each directory by its name again; no directory removed; nothing listed; the working directory not given back; and `chmod` falling back to following, or its `ValueError` let out) are each caught on both by the module of this row, apart from `rmtree` run first, caught on 3.11 only: on 3.13 `rmtree` walks by descriptors and handles the depth, so running it first changes nothing there. The first form of this change walked by path and was failed by its audit, which steered it outside the scratch with that swap and showed two links inside the scratch keeping a confined role's content; the second form was failed by its own audit for a `ValueError` CPython raises on Linux where `fchmodat` cannot refuse to follow, which escaped the call, and for a return of the working directory no test checked; the third, by its own, for a directory replaced by a file that no test tried, so opening it without `O_DIRECTORY` survived and set a file elsewhere to owner-only. Not covered by this row: a process the role started that moved to a process group of its own (`setsid`, `setpgid`), which outlives the call (any other the role left is ended before the removal, as the row "A role leaves nothing running in its group when it exits" says), so it can make its scratch again after the removal and write there (measured, with no sandbox and under `sandbox-exec`) or move an entry out of the walk's way so it stays (measured, with the move simulated), and, with the user's own authority, can steer the removal outside the scratch as the clause after next says; a C library whose `fchmodat` cannot refuse to follow (glibc before 2.32, or a later one with no proc file system mounted), where a directory the role made unreadable stays, as before; a process with the user's own authority, which such a process of an unconfined role is, replacing a directory the role made unreadable by a hard link to a file elsewhere between the walk's look and its `chmod`, or a flagged entry by another file between the look and the flag call, which sets that file's mode to owner-only or gives it the entry's other flags, or moving a directory the walk holds open out of the scratch, which the walk then empties where it now is, or another directory into one the walk has yet to list, which it then removes (each measured, with the race simulated), each a change such a process could make itself, and each one `sandbox-exec` prevents for a confined role by refusing the link and the move (measured); a tree deeper than the number of files the process may hold open, which the walk, holding one descriptor a level, leaves in place (measured: 600 levels with the limit at 256 were left, the removal returning without an error, and removed once the limit was back); and the two directories the sandbox probe makes, which `verify` in `adapters/sandbox.py` removes as before | `adapters/model_command.py`, `docs/PRIVACY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md` | `test_each_entry_the_role_locked_is_removed_when_the_call_returns`, `test_each_entry_the_role_made_immutable_is_removed_when_the_call_returns`, `test_a_link_or_a_file_where_the_scratch_was_is_removed`, `test_a_link_out_of_the_scratch_is_never_followed`, `test_the_scratch_is_removed_whatever_the_outcome`, `test_a_confined_role_that_locks_its_scratch_leaves_nothing`, `test_a_scratch_the_role_left_alone_is_removed_without_forcing_a_mode_or_a_flag`, `test_the_control_a_file_linked_from_elsewhere_keeps_its_flags_and_stays`, `test_a_flagged_file_behind_a_link_out_of_the_scratch_keeps_its_flags`, `test_a_link_flagged_itself_is_cleared_and_removed_and_its_target_keeps_its_flags`, `test_a_tree_deeper_than_the_recursion_limit_is_removed`, `test_a_directory_swapped_for_a_link_after_it_is_opened_does_not_steer_the_walk`, `test_a_directory_swapped_for_a_link_before_it_is_opened_is_unlinked_not_followed`, `test_a_directory_swapped_for_a_file_before_it_is_opened_is_unlinked_not_opened`, `test_where_chmod_cannot_refuse_to_follow_nothing_is_followed_and_the_call_returns`, `test_the_working_directory_is_given_back_after_a_flag_is_cleared`, `test_remove_scratch_removes_a_locked_tree_without_a_role` |
