| No child process can exhaust the parent | Every external command (each model role and the conversation CLI) runs through one bounded reader: the byte cap is enforced while stdout is read, so a flooding command is killed at the cap instead of being buffered whole and measured afterwards; the timeout is a wall-clock deadline over the whole exchange and a timed-out child is killed and reaped; the child leads its own process group (`start_new_session`) and the kill goes to the group, so a wrapper's grandchild dies with it at the deadline or the cap instead of running on (in rounds, and a model role's group at the role's own exit too, as the row "A role leaves nothing running in its group when it exits" says); stdin is fed from a helper thread so an eager child cannot deadlock the parent; non-UTF-8 output is a named failure (`MalformedModelOutput` for a role, `RecordDefect` for one export), not a crash; a conversation command that exits non-zero is `SourceUnavailable` naming the exit code and the last `QUOTED_TAIL_CHARS` characters of its stderr (its stdout only when stderr was empty or blank), so the operator sees the reason and not a partial export; a model role that exits non-zero is a `TransientFailure` quoting the same bounded tail (`QUOTED_TAIL_CHARS`, one constant for both adapters, held below `STDERR_TAIL_BYTES` and equal to the number the privacy doc states); an unparseable reply is quoted by its first `QUOTED_OUTPUT_CHARS` characters only, the other number that doc states | `adapters/bounded_process.py`, `adapters/model_command.py`, `adapters/conversation_cli.py` | `test_a_flooding_role_is_stopped_at_the_cap_before_it_finishes`, `test_the_control_output_under_the_cap_is_parsed`, `test_output_that_is_not_utf8_is_a_malformed_output_not_a_crash`, `test_a_large_request_does_not_deadlock_a_child_that_answers_first`, `test_a_child_that_never_finishes_is_a_transient_failure_and_is_reaped`, `test_a_timed_out_role_takes_its_grandchild_with_it`, `test_a_flooding_wrapper_takes_its_grandchild_with_it_too`, `test_a_flooding_export_is_a_record_defect_stopped_at_the_cap`, `test_a_flooding_discovery_is_stopped_at_the_cap_too`, `test_an_export_that_is_not_utf8_is_a_record_defect_not_a_crash`, `test_a_hanging_command_is_unavailable_and_the_child_is_reaped`, `test_a_failing_export_names_the_exit_code_and_quotes_a_bounded_stderr_tail`, `test_a_failing_export_that_said_nothing_says_so`, `test_a_failing_export_with_only_blank_stderr_is_quoted_from_stdout`, `test_a_role_that_exits_non_zero_is_a_transient_failure_quoting_a_bounded_stderr_tail`, `test_a_role_that_exits_non_zero_in_silence_says_so`, `test_the_stderr_quote_bound_in_the_privacy_doc_is_the_shared_constant`, `test_the_diagnostic_quotes_only_the_documented_head_of_the_output`, `test_the_output_quote_bound_in_the_privacy_doc_is_the_shared_constant`, `test_the_control_export_under_the_cap_is_normalized` |
| A role leaves nothing running in its group when it exits | `run_bounded` in `adapters/bounded_process.py`, asked by the model runner (`end_group_on_exit`), ends the role's process group once the role has exited, whatever its exit code, before the call returns and the role's scratch is removed. The bounded reader killed that group only while the role itself still ran (at the deadline, the cap or a stop signal), so a role that exited on its own left whatever it had started. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, with no sandbox and under `sandbox-exec`, the same in all four): a background `sleep`, the same under `nohup`, a subshell ignoring the hang-up, termination and interrupt signals, a double fork, a loop of 200 background jobs and the background job of a role that then exited 3 each ran on after the call returned, and a job that made the role's scratch again after its removal and wrote there left it in the state tree, 7 cases of 7; after, 0 of 7. The exit is waited for without reaping the role (`_exited_unreaped`: `waitid` with `WNOWAIT` where there is one, on Linux and on macOS from Python 3.13, else a kqueue), so while its group is signalled the role's id still names that group and cannot have passed to another process; the role is reaped only after. While SIGCHLD is ignored in the run, the kernel reaps the role itself as it exits, so no role is started then (row "No child is started while its exit could not be read"), and a role SIGCHLD came to be ignored for as it ran is not signalled (`children_reaped_by_the_kernel`; found by this row's first audit: on 3.11, where macOS waits with a kqueue, that exit was taken for one not yet reaped and the freed id was signalled, in 5 calls of 5, measured with the signal only recorded; after, in none on either Python). A run started with SIGCHLD ignored (a parent can leave it so through `exec`, and Python does not reset it) puts it back to the default before anything else (`_put_sigchld_back` in `cli.py`): macOS does not reap the children of a process started so, yet `getsignal` reports the ignore, so with that check alone the group was left as if the kernel had reaped the role, and what the role started ran on in 5 calls of 5 (measured on both Pythons, though the role was an unreaped zombie each time and its group was ended without that check); Linux keeps the ignore through `exec` and then reaps each child as it exits (read from its kernel/signal.c, not run here), as macOS does with SIGCHLD ignored in the run, where each child's exit then reads as 0 (measured on both Pythons, before the row "No child is started while its exit could not be read": a role's answer that exited 3 taken, 5 of 5, and an export that exited 1 taken, 5 of 5). The commands such a run starts then start with SIGCHLD at the default too (measured). The group is signalled in rounds (`_end_group`) until nothing in it runs, since one signal misses a child forked as it arrives (measured on macOS, both Pythons: with one signal, a shell starting a background job, a double fork or a loop of 200 as the role exited left a process running in the group in 19 to 37 runs of 40; with the rounds, in none of 40); the rounds end when macOS reports that the group can no longer be signalled, or after `GROUP_END_SECONDS` (1 s). On Linux there is one round (`ONE_ROUND_ENDS_A_GROUP`), since Linux gives a group's signal sent during a fork to the new child too, and reports a signal to an exited member not yet reaped as sent, so rounds there could not tell when nothing was left (read from its kernel/fork.c and kernel/signal.c, not measured here; CI runs this module on Linux). The kqueue's names are read through an untyped name (`_kqueue_exited`), since the stubs declare them only for the platforms that have a kqueue: read as they were, the type gate failed on Linux (measured before: `mypy --platform linux` gave 8 errors, one for each name read, and so did CI's on all three Pythons; after, none, and none as on macOS), and CI now checks the types as on macOS too (`mypy --platform darwin`). The kill at the deadline and the cap goes in the same rounds. Controls: a role that closes its output and works on is not cut short, and one still running at the deadline after closing it times out as before; a call that leaves nothing signals its group once; where the exit cannot be waited for without reaping, nothing is signalled, as before, so a role still at work is not ended; and the conversation command, the operator's own tool and not a role, keeps a helper it starts. 31 mutants (the runner not asking, or every command asked; the group not ended, or ended after the role is reaped; a wait that cannot tell taken as an exit; the exit not waited for; one round everywhere, on macOS, or nowhere; an error not ending the rounds; their bound ignored, or changed; a group that cannot be signalled not reported; `_kill` without its fallback, or in one round; `waitid` reaping, a failed `waitid` taken as an exit, or `waitid` not waiting; a kqueue's no-such-process event, or the same error raised, not taken as an exit, any error event taken as one, or its timeout taken as one; the kqueue preferred to `waitid`; no way to wait taken as an exit; an ignored SIGCHLD not checked, every SIGCHLD taken as ignored, or the default taken as ignored; and the run not putting SIGCHLD back, putting it back to ignored, doing so only off the main thread, or a call of `main` doing so too) are each caught on both by the module of this row, apart from three: one round nowhere is the code as written on macOS, and on Linux fails the module's check of the rule (not run here); and `waitid` reaping, or not waiting, is caught on 3.13 only, since 3.11 on macOS has no `waitid` and waits with the kqueue. Not covered by this row: a process that moved to a process group of its own (`setsid`, `setpgid`), which the signal to the role's group does not reach, so it runs on after the call (measured, with no sandbox and under `sandbox-exec`), with what that lets it do to the scratch as the row "A role cannot keep its scratch by changing its modes or flags" says; a platform with neither `waitid` nor a kqueue, where the group is left as before; a caller of `main` or of `run_bounded` in a process started with SIGCHLD ignored, or one that ignores it in the run, where no role is started (the row "No child is started while its exit could not be read" says what that still leaves); SIGCHLD set to be ignored from C code in the run after it started, which `getsignal` does not see (measured: it still reported the default while the kernel reaped each child), so the kqueue's exit is taken for one not yet reaped as before this row's audit; and a process the kernel cannot end at once (in an uninterruptible wait), which is signalled for `GROUP_END_SECONDS` and left dying | `adapters/bounded_process.py`, `adapters/model_command.py`, `docs/PRIVACY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `cli.py` | `test_what_a_role_leaves_running_in_its_group_is_ended_when_it_exits`, `test_a_child_forked_as_the_group_is_signalled_is_reached_too`, `test_a_role_that_exits_non_zero_leaves_nothing_running_either`, `test_a_confined_role_leaves_nothing_running_and_no_scratch`, `test_a_role_that_keeps_working_after_closing_its_output_is_not_cut_short`, `test_a_role_still_running_at_the_deadline_after_closing_its_output_times_out`, `test_the_group_is_ended_before_the_role_is_reaped`, `test_a_call_that_leaves_nothing_signals_its_group_once`, `test_where_the_exit_cannot_be_waited_for_unreaped_nothing_is_signalled`, `test_the_limit_a_process_that_moved_to_a_group_of_its_own_runs_on`, `test_the_control_a_conversation_command_keeps_what_it_starts`, `test_an_exited_child_is_reported_and_left_unreaped`, `test_a_running_child_is_waited_for_until_the_timeout`, `test_the_form_follows_what_the_platform_has`, `test_a_failed_wait_is_not_taken_for_an_exit`, `test_a_kqueue_error_other_than_no_such_process_is_not_taken_for_an_exit`, `test_the_group_is_signalled_again_until_nothing_in_it_runs`, `test_on_linux_one_round_is_all`, `test_the_rounds_are_bounded`, `test_a_group_that_cannot_be_signalled_falls_back_to_the_child`, `test_an_ignore_taken_up_while_a_role_runs_leaves_its_group_unsignalled`, `test_with_sigchld_ignored_no_exit_is_taken_for_one_not_yet_reaped`, `test_a_run_started_with_sigchld_ignored_puts_it_back_to_the_default`, `test_a_run_started_with_sigchld_ignored_leaves_nothing_running_in_the_role_group`, `test_a_run_started_with_sigchld_ignored_reads_a_role_exit_as_it_is`, `test_a_call_of_main_keeps_the_sigchld_its_caller_left` |
| No child is started while its exit could not be read | `run_bounded` in `adapters/bounded_process.py` starts nothing while SIGCHLD is ignored in the process and raises `ExitUnreadable`, an `OSError`, so the model runner reports the role as a command that cannot be started (a transient failure, so the conversation is deferred) and the conversation source reports its command as one it cannot execute (the source is unavailable). With SIGCHLD ignored the kernel reaps each child itself as it exits, and Python's `subprocess` then reads every exit as 0. Measured before, with SIGCHLD ignored in the run (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both): a role's answer that exited 3 was taken, 5 calls of 5; an export that printed and exited 1 was taken as the export, 5 of 5; the kill at the output cap went to an id the kernel had already freed, 5 of 5 (measured with the signal refused unless `ps` still showed the id as this process's exited child); and a background job a role started ran on after the call, 5 of 5. A process started with SIGCHLD ignored that calls the library rather than `asi` (which puts SIGCHLD back as it starts: row "A role leaves nothing running in its group when it exits") read every exit right on macOS, where an ignore inherited through `exec` does not make the kernel reap, but left a role's background job running, 5 of 5, since the ignore is reported all the same. After, on both Pythons, each of these is refused before anything starts, 5 of 5, and nothing is left running; a daily run called as a library with SIGCHLD ignored in the run, which read `status=ok` with 5 conversations scored, now defers all 5, each with the role named as a command that cannot be started. Control: with SIGCHLD at its default a role's exit 3 and an export's exit 1 are reported as before, and an answer is taken. 6 mutants (the refusal removed, made after the child is started, made for model roles only, made at the default instead, made an exception other than an `OSError`, or saying nothing of SIGCHLD) are each caught on both by the module of this row. Not covered by this row: an ignore taken up while a child runs, which only a signal handler or C code could set while the call waits: its exit is not taken for one not yet reaped (row "A role leaves nothing running in its group when it exits"), but a kill at the deadline or the cap may reach an id already freed, and its exit reads as 0; SIGCHLD set to be ignored from C code, which `getsignal` does not see (measured: it still reported the default, and every case above went as before this row, on both Pythons), so the child is started and its exit reads as 0; `doctor` called as a library with SIGCHLD ignored in the run, which said ready (measured) and now names the ignore as a problem (row "Doctor names SIGCHLD ignored in the process it runs in"); and the one child started elsewhere, the isolation probe (`adapters/sandbox.py`), whose verdict is read from what it prints, not from its exit status (the detail it records quotes that status, which then reads 0) | `adapters/bounded_process.py`, `adapters/model_command.py`, `adapters/conversation_cli.py`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` | `test_with_sigchld_ignored_a_role_is_not_started_and_its_answer_is_not_taken`, `test_with_sigchld_ignored_an_export_is_not_started_nor_taken`, `test_with_sigchld_ignored_a_role_past_its_cap_signals_no_id`, `test_with_sigchld_ignored_nothing_a_role_would_start_runs`, `test_run_bounded_refuses_before_it_starts_anything`, `test_a_library_call_started_with_sigchld_ignored_is_refused`, `test_the_control_with_sigchld_at_its_default_a_child_runs_as_before` |

Not covered by this row: SIGCHLD set to be ignored from C code, which `getsignal` does not see (measured on both Pythons: `doctor` says ready, and `daily` reads `status=ok` with 5 scored, its children started as the row "No child is started while its exit could not be read" says, their exits read as 0); and an ignore taken up after `doctor` has run, which it cannot see (measured on both Pythons: `doctor` said ready at the default, then with SIGCHLD ignored `daily` read `status=partial` with 0 scored and 5 deferred, each role refused as that row says) | `cli.py`, `adapters/bounded_process.py`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` | `test_a_caller_of_main_that_ignores_sigchld_is_told_it_is_not_ready`, `test_the_json_report_lists_it_among_the_problems`, `test_it_is_named_after_a_daily_has_made_its_state_tree`, `test_it_is_named_with_a_role_unconfigured_as_well`, `test_at_the_default_it_is_not_named`, `test_asi_started_with_sigchld_ignored_puts_it_back_and_is_ready` |
| A quoted failure must not persist the credential it is about | The stderr tail a role or the conversation CLI leaves, the head of a non-JSON model reply and the conversation command line itself are redacted with the transcript's shapes before they are cut to the documented length and become a `reason`, so a key that straddles the cut is masked whole rather than sliced into an unrecognised fragment; harmless text is cut exactly as before. The same masking covers a model role's argv wherever it is persisted (its command id in scorecards, candidates and receipts; its dump in the config report) and the confinement probe's quoted output in the receipt's isolation record, while the argv that actually runs is untouched. A value a model put inside its JSON is quoted the same way: `quoted_value` in `domain/redaction.py` (the `repr`, redacted whole, then cut at `QUOTED_VALUE_CHARS` with the number of characters not quoted) is the one way a refusal quotes a scorecard's schema or `conversation_id`, a criterion id the rubric does not know or that was scored twice, a judgement's schema or preferred label, a proposal's schema, the improver's `target_skill_id` or the frontmatter name its replacement would introduce, and the two the scorecard itself keeps: a verdict string the rubric does not know (`Verdict.parse` in `domain/rubric.py`, whose message becomes a note and a `downgrade_reason`) and an evidence `element_id` that does not resolve in the conversation, which the downgrade reason names bounded, with its `position` bounded too (a JSON integer has no bound; five refs at 4300-digit positions once made one reason of 12,985 characters), and the scorecard stores nowhere else, since a citation that does not resolve is the model's claim rather than evidence and the stored refs feed the improver prompt; a value that fits is quoted whole. Measured before: these were interpolated with `!r`, so a scorer putting four megabytes, or a pasted key, into `conversation_id` would have put it in the quarantine reason, the receipt, the daily text summary and the scheduler log | `adapters/bounded_process.py`, `adapters/conversation_cli.py`, `adapters/model_command.py`, `adapters/sandbox.py`, `domain/redaction.py`, `domain/rubric.py`, `domain/scoring.py`, `domain/validation.py`, `domain/candidate.py`, `app/pipeline.py` | `test_a_failing_export_that_prints_a_credential_has_it_redacted_in_the_reason`, `test_a_credential_in_the_configured_command_line_is_redacted_from_the_reason`, `test_a_role_that_exits_printing_a_credential_has_it_redacted_in_the_reason`, `test_the_quoted_head_is_redacted_before_it_lands_in_a_quarantine_record`, `test_quoting_redacts_the_whole_capture_before_it_cuts`, `test_a_credential_in_a_role_argv_is_redacted_from_its_command_id_and_dump`, `test_a_probe_that_prints_a_credential_has_it_redacted_from_the_detail`, `test_a_short_value_is_its_repr`, `test_a_long_value_is_cut_and_the_cut_is_counted`, `test_a_credential_is_redacted_whole_before_the_cut`, `test_a_refusal_quotes_the_models_value_bounded_and_redacted`, `test_the_control_a_short_value_is_quoted_whole`, `test_an_unresolved_element_id_is_quoted_bounded_redacted_and_not_stored`, `test_an_unresolved_position_is_quoted_bounded_like_its_element_id`, `test_the_control_a_small_unresolved_position_is_named_whole`, `test_exactly_as_many_unresolved_refs_as_are_shown_says_no_more`, `test_a_ref_that_resolves_is_stored_beside_one_that_does_not`, `test_an_unknown_verdict_is_quoted_bounded_and_redacted`, `test_a_huge_or_secret_preferred_label_is_quoted_bounded_and_redacted`, `test_the_control_a_short_wrong_label_is_quoted_whole`, `test_a_huge_schema_value_is_cut_and_a_secret_one_redacted`, `test_the_control_a_short_wrong_schema_is_quoted_whole` |
| There must be no way to opt out for a private source | No flag, config key or environment variable relaxes the requirement, and the refusal says why a hosted model is not an alternative | `domain/isolation.py` | `test_no_opt_out_or_override_flag_exists_anywhere_in_the_package`, `test_the_control_finds_a_flag_that_really_does_exist`, `test_the_refusal_message_says_why_a_hosted_model_is_not_an_option` |

## State holds verbatim private transcripts

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A permissive umask must not decide the mode | Every directory is created `0700` and every artifact written `0600`, set explicitly; the test runs under `umask(0)` and proves the umask really was permissive | `app/state.py`, `jsonio.py` | `test_the_state_root_is_0700_even_under_a_permissive_umask`, `test_every_declared_subdirectory_is_0700`, `test_the_readme_is_0600`, `test_the_umask_really_was_permissive_during_the_test`, `test_the_state_tree_declares_a_sandbox_subdirectory_and_it_is_owner_only` |
| Raw exports and derived artifacts are equally sensitive | Raw exports, conversations, scorecards, failures, receipts, backups and the lock are all owner-only | `app/state.py`, `adapters/skill_fs.py`, `app/lock.py` | `test_a_raw_export_is_0600`, `test_conversations_scorecards_failures_and_receipts_are_all_0600`, `test_a_backup_directory_is_0700`, `test_the_run_lock_is_0600_even_when_it_already_existed_wider` |
| The run lock must be a file this tool owns | The lock leaf is opened without following a symlink and judged with `fstat` before its mode is set or a byte is read or written, so a planted fifo or device is a named refusal rather than a hung run, a wrong `chmod`, or a write to somewhere else | `app/lock.py` | `test_a_symlinked_lock_leaf_is_refused_without_touching_its_target`, `test_a_fifo_at_the_lock_path_is_refused_without_being_waited_on` |
| The atomic writer must not widen a mode | The mode is applied to the temp file before the rename, so no window exists at a wider mode and nothing is left behind | `jsonio.py` | `test_the_atomic_writer_defaults_to_0600_and_not_to_the_umask`, `test_the_atomic_writer_honours_an_explicit_mode`, `test_write_json_also_lands_0600`, `test_no_temp_file_is_left_behind_at_a_wider_mode` |
| A state directory that is really a symlink redirects the whole private tree | The root and every mandatory subdirectory must be a real directory, measured with `lstat` *before* anything follows, chmods, creates through, reads through or writes through it; a dynamically named directory such as a backup stamp is proven to be a real direct child of an already-verified mandatory directory; `doctor` reports the layout as a problem and repairs nothing, so the outside target's mode and contents are unchanged; an aliased ancestor of the root is refused on the lazy subdirectory access every non-`daily` command takes, exactly as on `ensure()`; the ancestor walk follows the path as written, so a `..` after a link is not collapsed away before the link is looked at, and an absent component does not end the walk while a later `..` can step back over it onto a link; configuration resolves only the ancestors of `[run].state_dir` and keeps its last component as written, so a link planted at the configured state directory reaches the store as a link, `doctor` reports it before anything (its sandbox scratch root included) touches the tree, and `daily` refuses | `app/state.py`, `cli.py`, `config.py` | `test_a_state_root_that_is_a_symlink_is_refused_before_anything_is_created`, `test_a_lazy_subdirectory_access_refuses_a_symlinked_ancestor_too`, `test_a_dotted_path_through_a_symlinked_ancestor_is_refused_not_collapsed`, `test_a_dotted_path_that_steps_back_over_an_absent_component_still_meets_the_link`, `test_the_control_a_dotted_path_through_real_directories_is_accepted`, `test_a_symlinked_state_root_receives_no_layout_and_no_artifact`, `test_a_mandatory_subdirectory_that_is_a_symlink_is_refused`, `test_a_write_through_a_symlinked_mandatory_subdirectory_is_refused`, `test_a_repair_walk_does_not_chmod_through_a_redirected_layout`, `test_a_mandatory_state_directory_that_is_a_regular_file_is_refused`, `test_a_precreated_backup_directory_symlink_is_refused`, `test_a_backup_directory_is_created_as_a_direct_child_of_backups`, `test_a_backup_manifest_is_not_read_through_a_symlinked_backup_directory`, `test_the_control_shows_a_healthy_layout_reports_no_problems`, `test_an_arbitrary_leaf_symlink_is_still_skipped_rather_than_refused`, `test_doctor_reports_a_symlinked_state_subdirectory_without_chmodding_it`, `test_a_configured_state_root_that_is_a_symlink_is_refused_through_the_cli` |
| An already-wide tree is the case a fresh-run check misses | `ensure()` creates, repairs and then verifies; a repair that cannot take effect is a refusal rather than a warning, and a symlink is skipped rather than followed; what the walk skipped is named (`foreign_entries()`, `doctor`'s `left_alone` and a `WARN`) rather than silently passed, and a leaf that is not a regular file is neither counted as a record nor opened for long (a fifo is refused, not waited on) | `app/state.py`, `cli.py` | `test_a_pre_existing_world_readable_tree_is_detected`, `test_it_is_repaired_and_the_repair_is_reported`, `test_ensure_repairs_a_pre_existing_tree_rather_than_refusing_it`, `test_a_repair_that_cannot_take_effect_is_a_refusal_not_a_warning`, `test_the_control_shows_a_healthy_tree_does_not_refuse`, `test_a_symlink_in_the_tree_is_skipped_rather_than_followed`, `test_every_entry_the_mode_walk_skips_is_named_rather_than_silently_passed`, `test_a_symlinked_leaf_is_not_counted_as_a_record`, `test_a_fifo_in_the_tree_is_named_left_alone_and_never_opened_for_long`, `test_doctor_names_a_link_inside_a_confined_tree_as_a_warning_not_a_problem` |

## Promotion is the only write, and it is deliberately awkward

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| An apply step added to a scheduler template must inherit the automation marker | **Measured before (3.11 and 3.13):** neither CLI-rendered template set a marker; daily then a separate noninteractive apply both exited 0 and the fixture skill changed. **After:** launchd sets `EnvironmentVariables` and cron sets `ASI_AUTOMATED_RUN=1` before the jobs; cronie, Vixie and OpenBSD honor that line for later entries. BusyBox does not, so every template command also exports the marker for its whole shell command, including an appended apply. Apply with a valid token and acknowledgement refuses with the automation message, leaving the skill, candidate, backups and receipt unchanged; `candidates` and `doctor`, including the weekly cron commands, still work. An unmarked noninteractive apply still succeeds: apply does not authenticate its caller; do not schedule apply. **Mutants:** 15 changes (cron environment absent, false, misnamed or after the job; launchd environment absent, false, misnamed or under the wrong key; daily export absent or false; either weekly export absent; weekly candidates marker assigned but not exported; approval's check removed; automation always inactive) are caught by their named tests on both Pythons; the unchanged control passes. **Not covered:** installation or execution by real scheduler daemons, a separate unprefixed BusyBox line, deliberate marker removal or override, or caller authentication | `packaging/cron/crontab.template`, `packaging/launchd/com.local.agent-skill-improver.plist.template`, `domain/approval.py` | `test_an_apply_on_a_later_cron_line_inherits_the_marker_and_is_refused`, `test_an_apply_added_to_the_launchd_job_inherits_the_marker_and_is_refused`, `test_an_apply_appended_to_the_cron_command_is_refused_even_on_busybox`, `test_candidates_and_doctor_accept_each_templates_marker`, `test_the_weekly_cron_commands_export_the_marker_and_still_work`, `test_a_separate_unmarked_apply_after_daily_still_succeeds_without_a_terminal` |
| Apply requires review interlocks, not proof of a human caller | An approval token, a typed acknowledgement, a matching base hash, and no active automation marker; apply does not authenticate its caller and an unmarked process with a token and acknowledgement can still apply; do not schedule apply. A marked apply or rollback names its own command and says the marker is an interlock, not caller authentication. **Wording mutants:** four changes (reverting either hint, routing rollback through the apply hint, or advising against scheduling apply in the rollback hint) are caught by their named refusal tests on both Pythons; the unmutated control passes. | `domain/approval.py`, `app/promote.py` | `test_08_apply_without_a_token_is_refused`, `test_09_apply_without_the_acknowledgement_is_refused`, `test_10_apply_with_a_wrong_token_is_refused`, `test_11_apply_under_the_automation_marker_is_refused`, `test_12_the_skill_file_is_still_untouched_after_four_refusals`, `test_a_marked_cli_apply_refuses_with_the_review_interlock_limit`, `test_an_apply_belt_refusal_names_apply_and_not_caller_authentication`, `test_a_marked_rollback_names_rollback_and_never_advises_apply` |
| An approval must not survive a change to what it approved | The token is a **deterministic review interlock, not a security boundary**: it is derived from the candidate id, the base hash and the patch hash with no secret, so anyone who can run this tool can derive one -- what it buys is that an approval cannot outlive an edit to the base or the patch | `domain/approval.py` | `test_the_token_is_bound_to_the_candidate_the_base_and_the_patch`, `test_changing_the_base_hash_invalidates_the_token`, `test_changing_the_patch_invalidates_the_token` |
| A token must not be recoverable from the artifacts | The token is never written into the receipt | `app/promote.py` | `test_the_token_is_not_written_into_the_receipt` |
| A stale base must be refused | If the file changed since the candidate was built, apply refuses even with a valid token | `app/promote.py` | `test_18_a_stale_base_is_refused_even_with_a_valid_token`, `test_apply_refuses_a_live_base_that_moved_under_the_candidate` |
| A stale base must be named as a stale base, on review as on apply | `review` checks the record against itself first, then the live file, then the record against the live file, in the same order as `apply`, so an edited skill is reported as `BaseHashMismatch`, the skill named as changed, rather than as a tampered record, the record named as edited (both now give the one remedy the row "A refusal that sends you back to `daily` says what proposing again takes" describes); a record that fails its own base-independent recomputation is still named tampered first even when the file also moved | `app/promote.py` | `test_review_names_an_edited_skill_as_a_base_mismatch_not_as_tampering`, `test_review_still_names_a_tampered_record_first_when_the_skill_also_moved` |
| A token must bind the bytes that were reviewed, not a field beside them | Every derived field is recomputed before review, apply and validation: the patch hash from the normalized `new_text`, the approval challenge from the id and both hashes, the diff and its counts from the *live* base, the canonical target identity, and the status against the recorded gate. The token itself is derived from the recomputed hash. Editing only `patch.new_text` used to leave the token valid and put unreviewed text into a skill file | `domain/integrity.py`, `domain/candidate.py`, `app/promote.py`, `app/validate.py` | `test_04_apply_with_the_original_token_is_refused`, `test_05_the_skill_file_is_byte_identical_after_the_refusal`, `test_editing_new_text_alone_is_detected_although_every_other_field_is_intact`, `test_the_token_of_a_record_whose_text_changed_is_not_the_reviewed_token`, `test_an_edited_unified_diff_is_detected_against_the_live_base`, `test_a_rewritten_approval_challenge_is_detected`, `test_a_skill_id_that_disagrees_with_the_path_is_reported`, `test_a_validated_status_over_a_failing_gate_is_reported`, `test_08_control_the_untampered_record_reviews_to_the_same_token`, `test_09_control_apply_still_succeeds_on_the_record_that_was_reviewed` |
| A validation must be evidence about the failures the candidate names | Every target outcome is bound to a candidate failure ref by the whole `(rubric_id, conversation_id, criterion_id)` triple: the record's claim set must equal the candidate's own `rubric_failures`, no target outcome may name a triple the candidate does not claim, and an outcome must be attributed to a rubric that really grades its criterion. Substituting a rubric that is live with a matching version and source hash used to pass unnoticed, because the binding was only `conversation::criterion` | `domain/validation.py`, `domain/integrity.py` | `test_substituting_a_live_matching_rubric_on_the_outcome_is_refused`, `test_substituting_the_rubric_on_the_claim_set_too_is_still_refused`, `test_an_outcome_whose_named_rubric_cannot_grade_it_is_refused`, `test_two_rubrics_may_grade_the_same_criterion_id_without_colliding`, `test_a_target_outcome_re_attributed_to_the_other_live_rubric_is_refused`, `test_re_attributing_the_claim_set_as_well_is_still_refused`, `test_a_clean_record_reports_no_mismatch_with_the_live_rubrics` |
| A promotion-eligible candidate must carry exact target coverage | Adding or omitting a claimed failure invalidates review and apply, and a claimed target with no replay outcome fails the gate where it is produced rather than becoming a nominally validated record that refuses itself at review time | `domain/integrity.py`, `app/validate.py` | `test_an_added_candidate_failure_ref_invalidates_the_record`, `test_an_omitted_candidate_failure_ref_invalidates_the_record`, `test_adding_a_ref_to_both_sides_without_an_outcome_fails_the_recomputed_gate`, `test_an_added_candidate_failure_ref_is_refused`, `test_an_omitted_candidate_failure_ref_is_refused`, `test_a_failed_target_replay_fails_the_gate_and_writes_a_readable_record`, `test_a_gate_failed_record_with_an_unreplayed_claim_is_honest_not_tampered` |
| A count no verifier can recompute is not evidence | `evidence_model_calls` is derived exactly from the persisted outcomes and judgements and must equal that derivation; calls that were attempted and produced nothing usable are reported as a note instead of inflating the field. The previous lower-bound check accepted any overcount | `domain/validation.py`, `app/validate.py` | `test_one_extra_call_is_refused`, `test_one_missing_call_is_refused`, `test_the_call_count_is_the_evidence_count_and_the_attempts_are_a_note`, `test_one_extra_evidence_model_call_is_refused`, `test_a_second_criterion_on_a_scored_pair_costs_no_further_call`, `test_each_distinct_conversation_rubric_pair_costs_two_calls` |
| A record the writer emits must parse under its own parser | Rubric bindings and judgements are derived from the outcomes that persisted rather than from every attempted case, so a failed or partial replay still writes a record the strict parser accepts, and no judgement exists without the outcome it is about | `app/validate.py`, `domain/validation.py` | `test_a_partial_multi_rubric_replay_binds_only_the_rubric_that_produced_evidence`, `test_no_judgement_is_written_for_a_target_that_produced_no_outcome`, `test_a_judgement_about_a_case_with_no_target_outcome_is_refused`, `test_a_judgement_about_an_unreplayed_case_is_refused`, `test_a_full_replay_passes_the_gate_and_binds_exactly_what_it_claims`, `test_a_target_naming_a_rubric_that_cannot_grade_it_is_never_substituted`, `test_zz_control_the_untampered_record_still_reviews_and_applies_with_two_rubrics` |
| A refusal must leave nothing behind | The recomputation and the live-base check both run before the backup directory is created and before the write, so a refused apply changes no skill file, no candidate status, no backup and no receipt | `app/promote.py` | `test_06_no_backup_and_no_receipt_were_written_by_the_refusal`, `test_07_the_candidate_status_was_not_advanced_by_the_refusal`, `test_apply_refuses_a_tampered_candidate_before_the_store_is_written`, `test_apply_creates_no_backup_directory_when_it_refuses` |
| A state read must fail closed, not traceback | A candidate record that is truncated, of the wrong schema, not an object, missing a field, or claiming an id other than the one it was looked up by is a named `SafetyRefusal`; a listing skips such a record rather than failing the command | `app/state.py` | `test_a_record_that_claims_a_different_id_is_refused`, `test_a_truncated_record_is_a_named_refusal_not_a_json_error`, `test_a_record_missing_a_required_field_is_a_named_refusal`, `test_listing_skips_an_unreadable_record_rather_than_failing_the_command`, `test_a_healthy_record_reads_back_by_its_own_id` |
| Every apply must be reversible | A backup is written before the edit, `rollback` restores it, and the restored hash is verified against the recorded base | `app/promote.py`, `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_14_a_backup_exists_and_holds_the_previous_text`, `test_16_rollback_restores_the_original_file`, `test_17_the_restored_file_matches_the_recorded_base_hash` |
| The recovery command must not be able to lose the file | Rollback verifies the manifest shape and the backup's own hash **before** it copies, so a truncated, edited or unreadable backup is a refusal with the live file still intact rather than a corrupt file and nothing left to restore from; a manifest that is not a regular file (a symlink, a fifo) is refused before it is opened, so it is neither read through nor waited on | `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_a_backup_that_no_longer_matches_the_manifest_is_refused`, `test_the_live_file_is_untouched_after_a_refused_rollback`, `test_a_missing_backup_is_refused_and_says_the_file_is_unchanged`, `test_a_manifest_missing_a_required_field_is_refused_by_name`, `test_an_unreadable_manifest_is_a_refusal_not_a_crash`, `test_a_manifest_that_is_a_fifo_is_refused_rather_than_waited_on`, `test_a_manifest_that_is_a_symlink_is_refused_before_it_is_read`, `test_a_manifest_pointing_at_a_payload_file_is_refused_on_its_filename`, `test_the_control_rollback_restores_the_previous_text` |
| One pathological record must not abort the pass | `json.loads` raises `RecursionError`, not `JSONDecodeError`, on deep nesting; every untrusted parse (export, fixture file, model reply, state leaf, backup manifest) goes through `loads_untrusted`, which folds that into `ValueError` so the caller's existing classification (`RecordDefect`, `MalformedModelOutput`, `StateUnreadable`, `BackupUnusable`, skip) applies: the healthy records are still scored, a nested candidate record is refused by name and skipped from the listing, and `rollback` refuses without touching the skill | `jsonio.py`, `adapters/conversation_cli.py`, `adapters/fixture_source.py`, `adapters/transcript_export.py`, `adapters/model_command.py`, `app/state.py`, `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_a_pathologically_nested_export_quarantines_only_that_record`, `test_pathologically_nested_output_is_malformed_not_a_crash`, `test_a_pathologically_nested_record_is_a_named_refusal_not_a_crash`, `test_listing_skips_a_pathologically_nested_record_rather_than_failing_the_command`, `test_a_pathologically_nested_manifest_is_a_refusal_not_a_crash` |
| A credential written by the model never becomes a candidate | `skill_text_defects` adds a defect when the replacement contains a high-confidence credential shape; the record is quarantined before a candidate, backup or judge prompt exists, and the receipt names the shape without repeating the value | `app/pipeline.py` | `test_a_token_written_into_the_replacement_is_a_defect`, `test_a_key_block_written_into_the_replacement_is_a_defect`, `test_placeholder_prose_in_the_replacement_is_not_a_defect`, `test_a_faithful_small_edit_has_no_defects` |
| What a reviewer sees must be what applies | `asi review` prints a candidate's prose and diff raw, and the new text becomes the skill an agent loads. `unreviewable_characters` names every `Cc` code point other than newline, carriage return and tab, every bidi embedding, override or isolate control (U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069), the invisible tag block (U+E0000 to U+E007F) and the line and paragraph separators (U+2028, U+2029), as `U+XXXX`; `candidate_defects` refuses prose that carries any of them and a replacement or diff that introduces one absent from the live base text, so the record is quarantined at the propose stage with the code point named and never the raw byte echoed. A code point the operator's own skill already carries is tolerated, so the rule is about what a proposal introduces | `domain/candidate.py`, `app/pipeline.py` | `test_a_terminal_escape_in_the_hypothesis_is_quarantined_not_proposed`, `test_a_bidi_override_introduced_by_the_new_text_is_quarantined`, `test_a_control_character_already_in_the_live_skill_does_not_block_improving_it`, `test_tabs_and_newlines_are_ordinary_text`, `test_control_characters_are_named_by_code_point`, `test_bidi_overrides_and_isolates_are_unreviewable`, `test_tag_characters_and_line_separators_are_unreviewable`, `test_ordinary_whitespace_and_joiners_are_not`, `test_each_code_point_is_reported_once_in_text_order`, `test_characters_already_present_in_a_baseline_are_tolerated` |
| The terminal shows a hostile byte, it never obeys it | A terminal executes control characters, and error messages quote model stderr tails, file names and configured paths verbatim. `escape_for_terminal` turns every `Cc` code point other than newline and tab, every bidi control, every invisible tag character and every lone surrogate (a file name that is not valid UTF-8, which a strict stream would refuse to encode) into the Python escape it is; `_say` and `_err` are the only places `cli.py` writes text for a person to stdout or stderr and `_say_json` the only place it writes JSON, a static test refuses every other mention of a standard stream in `cli.py` (not only `.write` calls: `.buffer`, aliases, module-level, `print` and a raw `sys.stdout.write(dumps(...))` too, each proven caught by a control), the internal-error traceback goes through the same escape, and the argument parser class escapes argparse's own messages (which repeat argv raw). `--json` output has its own choke point, `_say_json`, which writes every bidi control, tag character, line or paragraph separator, DEL, C1 control and lone surrogate the encoder leaves raw as the JSON `\uXXXX` escape it is, so the bytes are safe to read and `json.loads` gives the same object | `cli.py` | `test_the_escape_shows_what_a_terminal_would_obey`, `test_a_lone_surrogate_is_shown_rather_than_crashing_the_write`, `test_newlines_tabs_and_ordinary_unicode_pass_through`, `test_a_refusal_quoting_hostile_text_reaches_stderr_escaped`, `test_text_for_stdout_is_escaped_at_its_choke_point`, `test_a_not_found_message_and_an_internal_error_are_escaped_too`, `test_a_hostile_configured_path_is_shown_not_obeyed`, `test_json_output_is_safe_to_read_and_unchanged_to_parse`, `test_json_for_the_terminal_goes_through_its_choke_point`, `test_argparse_messages_that_repeat_argv_are_escaped_too`, `test_every_text_write_in_the_cli_goes_through_the_escape`, `test_the_static_check_catches_each_way_of_sidestepping_the_escape` |

| The model's prose must not be able to write the report that judges it | `review` prints the hypothesis, intended change, risks and rollback note as the model wrote them (masked, and clipped past the limits the row "An improver reply leaves a bounded candidate" states), line breaks kept, but indented: every line of that prose opens with a `  | ` margin (a blank line is a bare `  |`) or, for the first line of a risk, a `  - ` bullet (`quoted`); candidate id, skill id, path, gate reasons, evidence references, notes and mirror paths are kept to one line (`one_line`). The two `---`/`+++` labels of the diff are the other text a reviewer did not write, copied from the path by `difflib`, so `make_patch` spells a line break inside them (`one_line_label`), and because the integrity check `review_report` runs recomputes the diff through the same function, a stored diff whose label obeys a break is a mismatch, not a rendering. Only the report's own text stands at column 0: the `candidate`, `skill`, `file`, `base sha256` and `gate` field lines, the section headers, the prefixed lines of the diff and its `\ No newline at end of file` marker, `note:` lines and the sentence that introduces the apply command (the command itself is indented two spaces beneath it), so neither prose nor a directory name can imitate a `DIFF` header or the apply instruction, and the reviewer still sees everything the candidate keeps of what the model and the file system said. | A document discovery steps over must be reported, not silent | `FilesystemSkillStore` records every `SKILL.md` it saw and did not take as a `PassedOverDocument` (root id, path, reason: a link whose target does not exist or cannot be reached, a link while the store does not follow links, a link that resolves outside its root, a document deeper than `max_depth`, an entry that is not a regular file, named by kind: a directory, fifo, socket, device or otherwise; a directory the walk could not list, with the error; an entry it cannot examine and a link whose target it cannot reach, with the system's words, since the row "An entry named `SKILL.md` that discovery cannot examine is passed over with why") and exposes them as `passed_over()`, refreshed with the snapshots. Entries are enumerated with `os.walk`, not `rglob`, because on Python 3.11 a glob drops an entry whose `exists()` is false and a dangling link was never seen at all. `doctor` prints one `WARN` line per document (a warning, not a problem: a stepped-over document does not stop a run, and `doctor`'s exit code means only that the configuration is not runnable), counts them on its `skills` line and lists them as `skill_documents_passed_over` in JSON; `skills` prints one `passed over (<root>)` line each, counts them in its summary and lists them as `passed_over` in JSON. The fifo is judged by stat and never opened. Measured before: with a dangling link, an unfollowed link, a fifo and a too-deep document present, `doctor` said `verdict: ready` with no mention and `skills` listed exactly what a clean tree lists, so a skipped skill read as an absent one. | A candidate record that does not read must be named, never dropped | `StateStore.scan_candidates` returns every `.json` entry in the state tree's candidates directory split into the regular files that parse and `UnreadableRecord`s (path, reason); `list_candidates` is the readable half and nothing else changes for it. `candidates` prints one `UNREADABLE` line per record and an `unreadable` list in JSON; `doctor` makes each a `PROBLEM` (exit 1, `candidate_records_unreadable` in JSON; a directory, a link or a fifo at a name a record could have is one too, and keeps the warning it had, the row "A directory, link or fifo at a candidate record's name is counted, so the queue guard holds"; stated limit: a file without the `.json` suffix is neither a record nor reported, and a directory, a link or a fifo at a name no record can have keeps only the warning), read only from a sound tree that already has the directory; `daily` counts them as `candidate_records_unreadable` and, when there is at least one, stands down (`queue guard`) before discovery, because an unreadable record may be an open candidate for any skill and neither the backlog cap nor the per-skill cap can be applied to a queue that cannot be counted (the narrower response, scoring the day and refusing only proposals, was considered; the stand-down is chosen because it is the simpler claim to verify and the day is retried once the record is repaired). | A directory the payload inventory can list but not search is an unknown, not an absence | `_payload_files` in `adapters/skill_fs.py` now examines each entry the walk listed before judging it. A directory this process may list but not search (mode 0o444) gives its names and refuses every look at an entry, and the walk's `onerror` hears of nothing, so each entry was stepped over in silence. Now an entry gone since the listing (`FileNotFoundError`) is nothing there; any other refusal names the entry's directory once in `unlistable_dirs`, as "listed, but an entry in it could not be examined" with the system's words for the first refusal in name order, and an entry whose name marks it a payload is counted by that name when the listing itself gives it as a file or a link (`DirEntry.is_symlink` or `is_file(follow_symlinks=False)` on a second listing of its directory, which answer from the type the directory read gives, without a `stat`), as the examined path counts such an entry. Where the listing gives no type (a file system without `d_type`, on which `os.walk` files a directory among the files), or the directory cannot be listed again, the entry is not counted, so the count stays a floor: v1 of this change took any entry `os.walk` did not list as a directory for a file, and a directory named like a payload was then counted. An executable in such a directory whose name does not mark it a payload is not counted, and the named directory makes the count a floor. The discovery warning and the `ELEVATED REVIEW RISK` note say a directory "could not be listed or fully examined", and `doctor`'s `WARN` says "could not list or fully examine". Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both, with `tdd`'s scripts at 0o444 holding hook.sh and an executable tool): `skills --json` gave no payload, no directory and the inventory complete, `doctor` counted no payload and warned of nothing, and `daily --commit` proposed one candidate with no risk note; with scripts/sub/inner.sh as well, only scripts/sub was named, and the two files in scripts were lost without scripts being named. After: hook.sh counted, scripts named, the inventory incomplete, `doctor` counting one skill with payloads and giving the `WARN`, and the candidate carrying both notes; nested, both directories named, scripts/sub first. At 000 and 755 nothing changes but the words. 19 mutants (the examination dropped; an entry gone taken for a refusal; a payload name not counted, or counted without asking its type, or every refused entry counted; a link not counted; an entry of no known type counted; a refused second listing raised, or a name missing from it counted; the directory not named, or each entry named instead; the words as the whole error, as `strerror` alone, or without "listed"; the last refusal kept; the named directories not reported; the warning, `WARN` or note in its old words) are each caught on both. On the source before this change the new module and the two modules whose words changed give 13 failures on both Pythons and both temporary directories: 10 of the new module's 12 tests (the control and the mode check pass), and the words in 2 tests of the others, one of them in two subtests. Not covered by this row: Linux, where the same modes are expected to act alike, unverified until CI; a file system without `d_type`, simulated here by a listing whose entries need a `stat` for their type, not run on one; what such an unnamed executable is, which no one can see without searching the directory | `adapters/skill_fs.py`, `cli.py`, `app/pipeline.py`, `app/daily.py`, `domain/skills.py`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, `docs/PRIVACY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md` | `test_such_a_directory_is_named_once_and_a_payload_name_is_counted`, `test_the_control_the_directory_searchable_again_is_a_complete_inventory`, `test_skills_gives_the_payload_by_name_and_the_inventory_as_incomplete`, `test_doctor_counts_the_payload_and_warns_the_count_is_a_floor`, `test_daily_proposes_with_both_risk_notes_and_review_shows_them`, `test_the_directory_is_left_as_it_was`, `test_another_error_names_the_directory_once_in_the_systems_words`, `test_of_two_errors_in_one_directory_the_first_in_order_is_kept`, `test_an_entry_gone_since_the_listing_is_nothing_there`, `test_a_link_named_like_a_payload_is_counted_as_the_examined_path_counts_it`, `test_where_the_listing_gives_no_type_no_name_is_counted`, `test_a_directory_shut_after_the_walk_listed_it_counts_no_name` |

Measured with a skill named by 24 short words (its directory and its frontmatter name) and the run "skill '<name>'" (25 words) in the cited message, copied by the improver (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both): before this row, 1 candidate proposed and passed the gate, the run in its record under state/candidates/; after, 0 candidates, the reason is exactly the fixed words, the skill id and the digest, and the one 25-word run of the shown excerpt that the base does not hold standing in stdout, the receipt, the checkpoint or the quarantine record is that one, the word skill and the skill's name, which the reason and the summary's line for the skill print as they name the skill (none in stderr); the check is strict, so a copy of that stdout with the run going on to the excerpt's next word added fails it, though the added run holds the name's words. The raw run the fence rewrote was never shown to the improver, so a replacement holding it is not refused and is proposed. 40 mutants (the run length 26, or 24; runs the base holds not subtracted, or any run the base shares clearing the whole replacement; runs taken from the whole conversation rather than the cited excerpts; the check, or the prompt, rendering its own excerpts; the check before the structural checks, or before the size check; the words quoted in the reason; the conversation id unquoted in the reason; the reason quoting any id; the naming helper quoting the id, quoting an id of fewer than 25 words as before, or adding the id after the digest; the digest taken of the quoted id, of the id stripped and case-folded, or cut to 16 characters; the clause on the judge and the replay dropped; words split on spaces only, or on ASCII whitespace only; a case-folded comparison; punctuation dropped; only the first excerpt checked; the first excerpt named whichever excerpt held the run; the excerpts joined into one text; only the first window of each text; an excerpt's last word, or its head, left out; the check reading the excerpts before the fence rewrote them, neutralizing delimiters but keeping the nonce, stripping the nonce but keeping delimiters, also reading the raw excerpts, or reading the shown excerpts joined into one; `shown_excerpts` stripping another fence's nonce; the prompt fencing the raw excerpts; the reason quoting the excerpt's word after the skill's name; the JSON summary printing the conversation ids as values; and, in the module itself, the id check widened to values inside the allowed keys, or the skill-name check widened to any copied run holding a word of the name) are each killed on both Pythons by the test named for it. Subtracting the base's runs from each excerpt rather than from the replacement gives the same answer for every input, so it is not a mutant a test can kill. | Two roots that tie on precedence are an ambiguity, not an alphabetical choice | `resolve_groups` in `domain/skills.py`, after selecting a root by tier (marked canonical and editable, else merely editable) and lowest precedence, requires that no other editable root of the same tier at the same precedence hold a copy of the skill; when one does, the group has no canonical copy, `ambiguous_copies` names every tied copy, and the reason names the roots, the tier and the precedence and says what to change (distinct precedences, the lower number winning, or `canonical = false` or `editable = false` on all but one). The remedy is worded per tier (in the unmarked tier, marking one root `canonical = true` is offered and `canonical = false` is not, since it would change nothing); a tied root that also holds two copies has that named in the same reason. A root of the other tier, or one that is not editable, at the same precedence does not tie, and distinct precedences pick by number whatever the ids. `skills` shows the `why:` line, `doctor` warns naming the roots and their copies, `daily` quarantines the skill at propose, and the write path in `adapters/skill_fs.py` refuses either tied copy as a skill with no canonical copy, quoting the resolver's reason, rather than as a mirror of an unknown source. | An empty value never leaves a flag to swallow its neighbour, and the window is never empty | `render_argv` in `adapters/conversation_cli.py`, when it drops an argument that is one placeholder rendering empty, drops with it a literal flag (an argument beginning with a dash and carrying no placeholder, judged on the template, not the rendered value) that immediately precedes it; `--`, a bare dash and a negative number are kept. The rule has a stated limit, measured in a test of its own: the renderer cannot tell a valueless flag from one that takes a value, so `--json` written directly before a placeholder that renders empty is dropped too, and the example config says to write such flags elsewhere. A placeholder preceded by a literal that is not a flag drops nothing else. | A discovery value is sent as written, though it names a placeholder | `render_argv` in `adapters/conversation_cli.py` fills the discovery command's placeholders (`{since}`, `{limit}`, `{query}`, `{workspace}`) from the window, the page size, the configured query and the workspace (`discover --since` and `--workspace` among them). | A blocked run blocks the next one until somebody says they looked | `run_daily` reads the newest daily receipt (`StateStore.scan_receipts`) before the lock, before any record is written under the state tree and before a conversation is read (the command has preflighted the source, exported nothing; the layout and owner-only mode repair every run performs come first, so a receipt whose mode drifted is repaired and then read, never refused as unreadable); if its `mutation_detected` field is true (the field, not the status: a receipt without it does not block), the run is refused with `BlockedRunUnreviewed` unless `mutation_reviewed` names that run's day, and the command maps that refusal to `RC_BLOCKED` so the page repeats every morning instead of filing as an ordinary refusal. The run that names the day records the statement in its receipt notes and becomes the newest receipt, so the schedule resumes without a change to the cron line; a dry run proceeds on the statement but writes no receipt, so the block stands. The flag waives one run only: a different day is refused naming the right one, a day when nothing is blocked is a `ConfigError`, and a value that is not a day is refused by argparse before any config is read. The gate fails closed: a receipt that cannot be read (torn, or a mode this process cannot open, which `scan_receipts` lists rather than raises) refuses the run as `StateUnreadable` instead of falling through to the previous readable receipt, and a day or path read back from the tree is spelled onto one line (`one_line_label`) before it reaches the message. | A directory where a record goes is refused by name, and doctor says it is there | Every state record write, the apply and rollback receipts included (`write_action_receipt`), goes through `StateStore._persist` and ends in `os.replace`, which swaps a link or a fifo out of the way (the documented contract: replaced, never followed, a link to a directory included) but cannot swap a directory out. `_persist` refuses a directory at the record's path by name (`SafetyRefusal`: the path, that a write cannot replace it, remove it if you did not put it there) before the write, for every record kind alike, and leaves it as found with no temporary file beside it; a dry run withholds the write and does not ask. No handler in `run_daily` turns a refused record write into a refusal of the skill (the `SafetyRefusal` handler around `propose_candidate` wraps reads only; the one handler that spans a record write is the blocked-run downgrade, `_downgrade_candidates_of_blocked_run`, which reports the failure in the receipt on purpose, since the blocked receipt is the artifact worth saving); `clear_deferred` removes only a regular file of this tool's own, so a directory at a deferral path is left alone there too. A promotion writes its receipt last, since the receipt records the write, so `apply_candidate` and `rollback_candidate` ask `artifact_path_problem` about `action_receipt_path` first (`_refuse_unwritable_receipt`) and refuse before the backup and the skill write when anything but a regular file sits there. `doctor` lists every directory sitting directly in a record directory (`RECORD_SUBDIRS`: every state directory but `backups`, one directory per candidate, `sandbox`, per-call scratch, and `locks`, the one lock leaf `lock_entry_problem` judges) as `misplaced_directories` under `state_permissions`, with a `WARN` naming the first, singular or plural, that skips one already raised to a `PROBLEM` as today's receipt or checkpoint; a link to a directory is a foreign entry, not a misplaced directory, a record directory this process cannot list is passed over, and nothing is listed through a broken layout. Measured before: a directory at one conversation record's path escaped as `IsADirectoryError` from the middle of the pass, exit 70 with the day's receipt never written, while `doctor` said ready; a directory at the apply receipt path let the apply change the skill, mark the candidate applied and exit 70 with no receipt of it | `app/state.py`, `app/promote.py`, `app/daily.py`, `cli.py` | `test_a_directory_at_the_record_path_is_refused_by_name_and_left_as_found`, `test_every_record_kind_is_refused_the_same_way`, `test_the_apply_and_rollback_receipts_are_records_too`, `test_a_dry_run_withholds_the_write_and_does_not_ask`, `test_a_link_or_a_fifo_at_the_record_path_is_replaced_not_followed`, `test_a_directory_at_a_deferral_path_is_not_a_record_to_clear`, `test_the_record_directories_are_every_subdir_but_backups_sandbox_and_locks`, `test_a_record_directory_this_process_cannot_list_is_passed_over`, `test_a_directory_under_a_record_directory_is_listed_and_others_are_not`, `test_nothing_is_listed_through_a_broken_layout_or_an_absent_root`, `test_doctor_warns_and_the_run_refuses_by_name_instead_of_exiting_seventy`, `test_todays_receipt_path_is_a_problem_once_not_a_problem_and_a_warning`, `test_apply_then_rollback_each_refuse_before_the_skill_write` |

When something else stands at that name (a directory, empty or not, a link to a directory or to nothing, or a fifo), it is now refused by name as `StateUnreadable`, exit 3: the message says what the entry is, judged as `doctor` judges a foreign entry, with `lstat` first (`_foreign_reason`: `is a symlink`, `is not a regular file or directory`, and otherwise `is a directory`), that this tool writes each candidate record as a regular file, and that the entry is left alone and `doctor` warns of it, the path named on one line as the store's other refusals name one (`_shown`). With nothing at the name it is still `FileNotFoundError`, exit 4 (`not found: no candidate '<id>' at <path>`), and a link to a regular file is still refused by `_read_state_json` (`the leaf is a symlink`). Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both), with a real validated candidate and its approval token: a directory, a directory holding the record, a link to nothing, a link to a directory and a fifo at its record path each made `review`, `apply --approve <token> --i-reviewed-this` and `rollback` print `not found: no candidate '<id>' at <path>` and exit 4 (15 of 15), as if nothing stood there, while `doctor` warned of each one (a directory as one that sits where only records are written, a link or a fifo as an entry it leaves alone). After, on both: each of the 15 exits 3, its first stderr line naming the entry for what it is, the entry left as found and the skill unchanged; with nothing at the name each command exits 4 with the line it printed before; a link to a regular file is refused as before, exit 3; the record put back is reviewed, exit 0; and a state directory whose name holds a line break is named with the break spelled `\n`. 13 mutants (the branch removed; it raising `FileNotFoundError`; it asking only whether the path exists, so a link to nothing is missed, only whether it is a link, only whether it is a link or a directory, or whether it is a link that exists; one word for every kind of entry, or a link to a directory called a directory; the `doctor` clause dropped; the path named unspelled, or the id named in its place; a link to a regular file refused by this message rather than read; and a link or a fifo removed before the refusal) are each caught on both by the module of this row. Not covered by this row: `candidates`, `doctor`'s verdict and `daily`, which read the candidates directory through `scan_candidates`; with a directory at a validated candidate's record path they listed nothing, stayed ready and proposed a new candidate the next day (measured on both, before this change and after it, which did not touch them), and the row "A directory, link or fifo at a candidate record's name is counted, so the queue guard holds" covers them | `app/state.py`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md` | `test_each_entry_that_is_not_a_regular_file_is_refused_for_what_it_is`, `test_a_state_directory_with_a_line_break_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_the_control_a_missing_record_is_still_not_found`, `test_the_control_a_link_to_a_regular_file_is_refused_as_before`, `test_review_apply_and_rollback_refuse_each_entry_by_name`, `test_doctor_warns_of_each_entry_the_refusal_says_it_does`, `test_the_control_a_missing_record_is_exit_4_and_the_record_reads` |
| A directory, link or fifo at a candidate record's name is counted, so the queue guard holds | `scan_candidates` in `app/state.py`, how `candidates`, `doctor` and `daily` read the candidates directory, now lists anything but a regular file at a name a record could have (`<candidate id>.json`, `_names_a_candidate_record`) as an `UnreadableRecord`, judged with `lstat` and never opened or followed. Its reason names the entry in the words `read_candidate` uses (`_what_stands_at_a_record_name`: `is a symlink`, `is not a regular file or directory`, and otherwise `is a directory`), says that this tool writes each candidate record as a regular file, so the entry is not one it wrote and may stand where an open candidate's record was, and that it is left alone, the path named on one line (`_shown`). So `candidates` prints it as `UNREADABLE`, `doctor` makes it a `PROBLEM` (exit 1) beside the warning it already gave, and `daily` stands down before discovery (`queue guard`), as for a record that does not parse (the row "A candidate record that does not read must be named, never dropped"). Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both), after a committed run wrote one validated candidate for `tdd`: a directory, empty or holding the record, a link to the record moved elsewhere, a link to nothing, a link to a directory and a fifo at its record's name were each passed over, so `candidates --all` listed 0 candidates and 0 unreadable, `doctor` stayed ready (exit 0) with one warning, and the next day's committed run proposed a second `tdd` candidate (6 of 6), where with the record in place it proposed none. After, on both: each of the 6 is listed as unreadable in the text and the JSON, `doctor` exits 1 with that one problem and still one warning naming the entry, and the next day's run stands down, writes no record and leaves the entry as found; a state directory whose name holds a line break is named with the break spelled `\n`; and as controls, the same as before: with the record in place the next run proposes none for the skill; a name without the `.json` suffix is no record; a record that does not parse keeps its own reason; a regular file at a name no record can have (the record copied under the name a Finder duplicate gets, and a `notes.json` holding `{}`) is read and, binding to no id, listed as unreadable; and an entry at a name no record can have is only the foreign entry it is: the link Emacs makes beside a file it is editing (`.#<record>.json`, which `*.json` matches and no candidate id does), so a record open in an editor does not stand the run down, a directory named `notes.json`, a fifo whose name is no id, and the link named `planted.json` that the store's and `doctor`'s tests of a link left alone plant. Counting every `*.json` name, this change's first form, would have stood the run down for that editor's link. 15 mutants (the entry passed over, as before; only directories counted; links passed over, or fifos; a link followed to its target; a fifo read as a record; one word for every entry; a link to a directory called a directory; the open-candidate clause dropped; the path named unspelled; every name in the directory counted, not only `*.json`; `daily` standing down only for regular files; the name rule dropped; a name taken for a record's on its `CAND-` prefix alone; and the name rule moved above the regular-file branch, so such a file was passed over, which this row's audit found untested) are each caught on both. | A run that refuses still reports the modes it reset first | Wiring the runner opens the sandbox scratch root (a reset there is pending in the store until `ensure()` reports it) and `ensure()` repairs the whole tree before every refusal `run_daily` makes after its layout check (the blocked-run gate, an unreadable receipt, a non-file where the day's receipt or checkpoint goes), and a refused run writes no receipt. `cmd_daily` catches `AsiError` from the runner wiring onward (so a model command that is not found, an isolation refusal or a source that fails preflight report too), writes one stderr line through `_report_repairs_of_refused_run` naming the count and the reset paths (`StateStore.repairs_so_far`: what `ensure()` reported plus any reset still pending, the first five, each spelled onto one line by `one_line`) and that no receipt reports the repair, then re-raises, so the exit code and the refusal line are unchanged and follow it; a refusal that reset nothing adds no line, a run that proceeds reports the repair in its receipt as before, and an exception that is not a refusal (exit 70, a bug report) is left to its traceback.
