| Every command that opens the state tree names the modes it reset | `StateStore.dir` resets a drifted mode on the directory it opens and leaves the reset pending (`repairs_so_far` lists it) for `ensure()` to report; only `daily` calls `ensure()` and only `doctor` reports its own walk. Every store a command opens goes through `_open_state` in `cli.py`, which registers it, and `main` calls `_report_unreported_repairs` before it returns and before it writes any refusal, not-found or internal-error line: each registered store whose `repairs_reported` flag is unset gets one stderr line naming the count and the reset paths (through `one_line`) and that the repair changed no content, then the flag is set. `daily` sets the flag when its receipt reports the repair or when `_report_repairs_of_refused_run` has, and `doctor`'s own walk (`repair_permissions`) drains the pending list into its report, so neither says it twice; a clean tree adds no line, the note never touches stdout (a `--json` listing stays JSON), a directory a command did not open is neither reset nor named, and the registry is cleared when `main` returns, so an in-process second call never inherits the first call's store. Measured before: with the state directory and its candidates directory at 0755, `candidates`, `set-aside`, `review`, `discover`, `apply` and `rollback` each put the directory back to 0700 and none of them said so, on success or on refusal | `cli.py`, `app/state.py` | `test_a_listing_a_review_and_a_refused_promotion_each_name_the_reset`, `test_only_the_directories_a_command_opens_are_reset_and_named`, `test_a_clean_tree_adds_no_line_and_a_json_listing_stays_json`, `test_daily_and_doctor_report_once_in_their_own_output_not_twice`, `test_an_in_process_second_call_does_not_inherit_the_first_calls_store` |
| The run that says a mutation was reviewed must not destroy the record of it | The receipt path is per day, so a committed run of a day whose receipt recorded a mutation (the one started with `--mutation-reviewed` naming that day after a morning review, or a later re-run of that day once a newer run has cleared the block) writes its receipt where the blocked one sits. `run_daily` keeps that receipt first, inside the lock and before anything else is written, deciding from the file at the path it will write (`StateStore.committed_receipt_at`, which reads that path itself, so the newest receipt being another day's, or the name being spelled differently on a case-insensitive filesystem, changes nothing; a file there that does not read as a receipt is a `StateUnreadable` refusal by name): `StateStore.keep_blocked_receipt` moves it (`os.replace`, never a copy) to `StateStore.kept_blocked_receipt_path`, `_blocked--` then the keyed day and the finish stamp (a leading underscore, which `safe_name` never leaves on a keyed day, so a run day literally named blocked still has a receipt that counts), suffixed when anything already holds that name so no earlier kept receipt is replaced; the new receipt's note names the kept path and the command's report prints it. A kept receipt is an archive, never a run: `scan_receipts` passes every `_blocked--*.json` over by name (`is_kept_receipt_name`) and lists it under `kept`, so a kept receipt stamped ahead of the clock or sorting ahead on a tie can never re-block the schedule, and `doctor` warns with their count and paths (`kept_blocked_receipts` under `--json`) so they do not accumulate unseen. A path that holds no receipt recording a mutation is overwritten as it always was; a dry run moves nothing; a store that withholds writes refuses the move as a `SafetyRefusal`, and a move the tree refuses (an `OSError`) is the same named refusal with both paths, never a traceback. Measured before: the reviewed run overwrote the blocked receipt, and `skill_tree.changes`, the only record of what wrote to the tree, was gone, destroyed by the run that said it was reviewed | `app/daily.py`, `app/state.py`, `cli.py` | `test_the_blocked_receipt_is_kept_byte_for_byte_and_the_new_receipt_says_where`, `test_an_older_blocked_receipt_at_the_run_day_is_kept_whichever_run_is_newest`, `test_the_same_entry_under_another_spelling_is_kept_not_clobbered`, `test_a_kept_receipt_is_never_read_as_a_run_again`, `test_a_day_named_blocked_still_blocks_its_receipt_is_not_a_kept_one`, `test_a_run_that_dies_after_the_keep_leaves_exactly_one_copy`, `test_the_store_reads_what_sits_at_the_days_path_and_refuses_what_does_not_read`, `test_a_blocked_receipt_the_run_would_not_overwrite_is_left_where_it_is`, `test_an_ordinary_same_day_re_run_still_overwrites_and_keeps_nothing`, `test_a_dry_run_of_the_same_day_moves_nothing`, `test_a_kept_name_already_taken_is_not_replaced`, `test_the_store_refuses_to_move_a_receipt_while_withholding_writes`, `test_a_receipt_without_a_finish_time_is_kept_as_unfinished`, `test_a_move_the_tree_refuses_is_a_named_refusal_and_the_receipt_stays`, `test_the_reviewed_run_of_the_blocked_day_keeps_its_receipt_and_doctor_counts_it` |
| A promotion and a run never overlap | `apply_candidate` and `rollback_candidate` hold the run lock (`RunLock` at `StateStore.lock_path`, the one `run_daily` takes) from the live read of the skill (for rollback, from the choice of the backup manifest) through the skill write to the candidate record and the action receipt (`_holding_the_run_lock`), with an owner line naming the promotion, so a run started meanwhile is refused `LockHeld` naming it; a promotion that finds the lock held is refused `LockHeld` naming the action, the candidate and the holder, before anything is read from or written to the tree, the backups or the records, and the attempt leaves the lock free; `rollback` refuses inside an automated run (`AutomationRefused`) as `apply` always has, before the lock. The holder line is read back from the lock file, so it is spelled onto one line (`one_line_label`) before it reaches the message. Measured before: `apply` took no lock, an apply during a scheduled run went through, the run finished `blocked` with the applied document in `skill_tree.changes`, its candidates were closed as blocked-run, and the next morning's run was refused until the operator reviewed a mutation the tool had made itself | `app/promote.py`, `app/lock.py` | `test_apply_and_rollback_refuse_while_a_run_holds_the_lock_and_write_nothing`, `test_the_lock_names_the_promotion_and_a_run_meanwhile_is_refused_naming_it`, `test_a_lock_held_refusal_is_raised_with_the_action_and_the_lock_released`, `test_rollback_inside_an_automated_run_is_refused_as_automation_before_the_lock`, `test_the_backup_manifest_is_chosen_under_the_lock_not_before_it` |
| A run lock that fails to release leaves the outcome as it is | `RunLock` in `app/lock.py`, which `daily`, `apply` and `rollback` hold, keeps an `OSError` its release reports (`release_error`, the unlock's if both fail, started again at None by each release) and never raises it: `__exit__` forgets the descriptor first, so a second release closes nothing, unlocks it, and closes it in a `finally`, after an unlock that failed in any way, so anything else raised there (a stop signal's exception) is raised as it is with the lock let go; and each close of the descriptor before a refusal while the lock is taken (`_close_quietly`) reports nothing, so the refusal is what is raised. The close ends the lock whatever it reports (Linux's close(2) frees the descriptor even when it returns an error, and so does macOS's, read from Linux's manual and macOS's (XNU) source, not measured here), and the `asi` program exits at the end of each command, though a caller of `cli.main` may go on. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the unlock or the close made to report EIO, the descriptor closed all the same): an `apply` refused at the write (the skill's text changed after its own check) exited 70, "internal error: this is a bug in asi", its `BaseHashMismatch` lost as the outcome (the exit 3 and the first line; it was left only inside the traceback), the record `validated` and no backup; an `apply` that applied the candidate (record `applied`, skill written, one backup) exited 70; so did a `rollback` that rolled it back (record `rolled-back`, the skill as before) and a committed `daily` that wrote its receipt and candidate; `apply` and `daily` refused `LockHeld` while another holder had the lock exited 70, the holder's name lost, when the close of their own descriptor reported the error; and each of the six ways taking the lock fails (the descriptor's stat, a fifo at the path, the mode not set, the mode not 0600, the lock held, the owner line not written) raised the close's EIO in place of its own refusal or error. The lock was free after each: the next command in the same process took it. After, each exits as it does with the release working (3 with the `BaseHashMismatch`; 0; 0; 0; 3 naming the holder) and says nothing of the release, each of the six raises its own refusal or error, the lock is free, and the same token applies once the text is put back. Found by this row's first audit: with the close after the unlock rather than in a `finally`, a SIGTERM during the unlock in `cli.main` (exit 143) left the descriptor open, and the next `daily` in the same process refused `LockHeld` (3.11 and 3.13), where before this change it took the lock and exited 0; after, the descriptor is closed and it exits 0. 17 mutants (the close raising, or catching every error; the unlock's error raised, not kept, or an error that is no `OSError` kept in its place; no close after a failed unlock, or the close not in a `finally`; the close's error not kept, or kept in place of the unlock's; the error not started again at a release; the descriptor not forgotten; and each of the six closes before a refusal raising) are each killed on both Pythons by the test named for it. Not covered by this row: an error writing the owner line is raised as it is (exit 70, as before); the release error is kept on the lock but not told to the operator, since nothing is left held to act on; and a stop signal whose handler runs after the unlock and before the close is called, which leaves the descriptor open in a caller of `cli.main` that goes on | `app/lock.py` | `test_an_apply_refused_at_the_write_keeps_its_refusal`, `test_an_apply_that_applies_exits_0`, `test_a_rollback_that_rolls_back_exits_0`, `test_a_committed_daily_exits_as_it_would`, `test_a_refusal_while_another_holds_the_lock_names_the_holder`, `test_a_stop_at_the_unlock_ends_the_run_and_lets_the_lock_go`, `test_each_refusal_is_raised_and_not_the_close`, `test_the_release_error_is_kept_and_the_lock_is_free`, `test_a_failed_unlock_still_closes_the_descriptor`, `test_a_release_after_a_failed_one_keeps_nothing`, `test_with_the_unlock_and_the_close_both_failing_the_unlock_is_kept`, `test_a_release_that_reports_nothing_keeps_nothing`, `test_a_second_release_closes_nothing`, `test_an_error_that_is_no_os_error_is_raised_and_the_lock_let_go` |
| A wrapper that answers wrongly fails verification, it does not crash it | `verify` never raises for a failed verification: the probe reply is read through `_probe_reply`, which accepts only an object carrying exactly the string fields `PROBE_KEYS` names, the ones `PROBE_SOURCE` writes, so a wrapper (`bwrap` from PATH, `sandbox-exec` at its fixed path) that prints a non-object literal, JSON nested past the recursion limit, an object with non-string fields or without the three keys (which used to be read as the sandbox failing every check), or bytes that are not UTF-8 (the probe is read with `errors="replace"`) is one more shape of the probe not having run, and the mechanism is refused as unverified rather than aborting the run as an internal error | `adapters/sandbox.py` | `test_a_probe_reply_that_is_valid_json_but_not_an_object_did_not_run`, `test_a_probe_reply_nested_past_the_recursion_limit_did_not_run`, `test_a_probe_reply_whose_fields_are_not_strings_did_not_run`, `test_a_probe_reply_without_the_probe_keys_did_not_run`, `test_a_probe_reply_that_is_not_utf8_did_not_run`, `test_the_control_shows_the_reply_the_real_probe_writes_is_accepted`, `test_a_wrapper_that_prints_a_non_object_fails_verification_instead_of_raising` |
| A number that is not a finite double is an unreadable record, not an aborted run | `json.loads` accepts the non-standard tokens NaN, Infinity and -Infinity by default and parses a legal literal that does not round to a finite double (1e400) to infinity; one such position reached `int()` in the normalizer and aborted a committed run as an internal error with no receipt. `loads_untrusted` now reads every float literal through `_finite_float` and the three tokens through `_refuse_constant` (integer literals are untouched), so the text is a `ValueError` the caller already classifies: the fixtures source passes the export over by name with the number in the reason, the healthy export beside it is scored, a model reply carrying one is `MalformedModelOutput`, a state leaf carrying one is `StateUnreadable`; `_as_int` and `_opt_int` in the normalizer tolerate a non-finite float handed to them directly, and `dumps` refuses to write one (`allow_nan=False`) so a writer bug fails at write time rather than as a leaf no reader can read | `jsonio.py`, `adapters/transcript_export.py` | `test_the_non_standard_tokens_are_a_value_error`, `test_a_literal_past_the_double_range_is_a_value_error`, `test_the_control_shows_ordinary_numbers_still_read_as_before`, `test_a_refused_number_is_a_plain_value_error_like_deep_nesting`, `test_a_non_finite_position_sorts_as_the_default_rather_than_raising`, `test_a_non_finite_optional_int_is_absent_rather_than_raising`, `test_the_run_completes_and_scores_the_healthy_record`, `test_the_spoiled_export_is_named_in_the_receipt_with_its_number`, `test_a_model_reply_with_a_non_finite_confidence_is_malformed_not_a_crash`, `test_a_state_leaf_with_a_non_finite_number_is_a_named_refusal`, `test_the_writer_refuses_a_non_finite_float_rather_than_emitting_a_token_no_reader_takes`, `test_the_control_shows_the_writer_still_writes_ordinary_floats` |
| A candidate record of the wrong shape is refused by name, never a traceback | `Candidate.from_dict` reads every nested field through `_object` and `_objects`, which refuse a `patch`, `target`, `validation`, `validation.gate`, `sources` entry or `rubric_failures` entry that is not the object or array the schema says, naming the field and the type found (the three object fields were the unclassified `AttributeError`; the two arrays already failed as a classified `TypeError` and gain the field's name); the reader in `app/state.py` also wraps `AttributeError` so a shape this list does not yet cover is still one `StateUnreadable`; the listing in `cli.py` reads a null gate as not passed. | A scorer's evidence or confidence field of the wrong shape is a recorded defect, not an aborted run | `_build_score` reads a criterion's `evidence` through `_evidence_list`, which hands back the array the contract asks for, an empty list for null, and for any other shape (a number, a boolean, a string, an object) appends one malformed ref naming the type found and hands back nothing, so a decided verdict on a criterion that requires evidence is downgraded to INSUFFICIENT the way a malformed ref always sends it; `_confidence` reads the stored `confidence` as a float only when it is a number a float can hold, and as none otherwise; the persisted reason spells out at most `MAX_SHOWN_BAD_REFS` refs and counts the rest. | A backup manifest edited into nonsense is refused by name, and never restores a skill its owner cannot read | `_read_backup_manifest` requires the four fields `rollback` uses as non-empty text, so a number or an object there is refused naming the field instead of being coerced to text and refused later under another name; `rollback` makes every pre-write check in `_judge_rollback` and turns an `OSError`, a `ValueError` or (3.11, a symlink loop) a `RuntimeError` the platform raises while judging the recorded paths into `BackupUnusable` saying no file was touched; `_restorable_mode` never restores a permission the live file lacks: it honours a recorded mode only in the shape `write_canonical` records (`_RECORDED_MODE`), narrowed to the live file's bits and only if the owner can read the restored file back, otherwise keeps the live file's mode, and adds the owner read bit to whichever it writes. | A file name that is not UTF-8 is written into state as its JSON escape, not an aborted run | `dumps` writes each lone surrogate as its JSON escape after encoding, so every state write can be encoded, and `tree_digest` digests a path as the bytes the file system holds for it; a lone surrogate is how a name that is not UTF-8 reaches Python from a directory listing, and only a JSON string can hold one, where the escape is the same string to any parser unless a high half sits directly before a low half, which a parser reads as the one character the pair spells and a directory listing never gives (only low halves, U+DC80 to U+DCFF); `loads_untrusted` reads a lone one back as U+FFFD. Other text is written as it is. Measured before: a fixtures entry with such a name, passed over because it was not an export, made a committed daily run fall with `UnicodeEncodeError` from the receipt write, with no receipt, and a skill directory with such a name made the first tree reading of every daily run fall the same way. | Rollback restores only what its own apply wrote | `rollback_candidate` hands `rollback` the write this candidate's apply made: its document, its base hash and the hash of its replacement; `_assert_records_the_write` compares the manifest's document (both resolved, as `earlier_write` compares them) and its two hashes to those before `_judge_rollback` reads the backup, so a manifest that records any other write is `BackupUnusable` with no file touched and the candidate still `applied`, and the refusal names the manifest and both writes, its values quoted masked and bounded. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): a manifest edited to name another skill's document, with a backup beside it hashing to its `previous_sha256`, wrote that backup into the other skill; one keeping its own document but naming a planted backup wrote the planted text into it; one whose `new_sha256` was changed to the hash of a later edit overwrote that edit; each exited 0 and recorded the candidate rolled back. After: each exits 3, every file unchanged, and a healthy apply and rollback prints, writes and records what it did before. 11 mutants (no binding passed; the document unchecked, or compared unresolved; the hashes unchecked, only the base or only the applied hash compared, or the two swapped; every mismatch let through; the record checked only after the backup is judged; the applied hash quoted raw; the document named raw) are each caught on both. | A write is verified by reading it back, so it is one its owner can read, and a failed read-back is never reported as nothing touched | `write_canonical` writes the applied text, and the restore when verification fails, at the skill's mode plus the owner read bit and records that mode in the manifest; the read-back after the apply and after a rollback goes through `_read_back`, which turns a document that cannot be opened, is not a regular file or is not UTF-8 into a description, so apply restores the original and says so (a link, a fifo or other bytes put at the name before the read-back are replaced by the original, the link's target untouched; a directory put there, which no rename can replace with a file, or a skill directory that no longer takes a new file, is named with where the copy taken before the write is, never an internal error; the restore writes the bytes read before the write, never a second read of the copy) and rollback says the backup was written. | Masking takes time in proportion to the text, whatever the text holds | `redact` and `credential_labels` find each shape through `_matches`. For the `jwt` shape it looks for a start (`eyJ` where `_LEAD` lets a shape begin) and matches the pattern there; a start with no token after it fails where the run of token characters after it ends, and so does every later start inside that run, so the scan resumes past the run. Every other shape is its own pattern's search. The matches found, and the text left, are the ones the patterns alone give. | A NUL in a config string, or an env name no environment holds, is refused at load, by its field | `load_config` refuses a NUL in any key or string of the config, at any depth (`_refuse_nul`), naming the field (`run.state_dir`, `source.workspaces[1]`, `model.scorer.argv[4]`) and never its value, since an environment entry may be a credential (a name that is long, holds anything but key characters or holds something redaction masks, such as a token used as an env name, is quoted, masked and cut, `_named`); `role_command_from_mapping` refuses a `[model.*].env` name that is empty or holds `=`, quoted and cut (`quoted_value`). | A configured path the platform cannot hold is refused at load, by name | `_within_name_limits` in `config.py` refuses a path with a name past NAME_MAX bytes or a length past PATH_MAX less its terminator (read with `os.pathconf`: 255 and 1024 on macOS, so 1023 is the longest), for every path `_expand` returns, named by its field or its skill root, for the file of each `[rubrics].enabled` id, for the `--config` path, and for `[run].state_dir` with `STATE_PATH_RESERVE` bytes kept free below it, an upper bound on what a record adds (a subdirectory and two names of up to NAME_MAX each; a backup file in its candidate's directory has the most levels); `[run].base_dir`, a rubric's file and the state directory's ancestors are measured again once resolved, since a link may lead somewhere longer; the path, a root id and a rubric id are quoted bounded and masked (`quoted_value`). A path that could never exist is refused even where a run would have gone on without it (a mirror or other read-only skill root, which a run reads only when it is there, or a `base_dir` that no relative path uses), where before such a config ran. Stated limits: the reserve is an upper bound, so some state directories longer than the widest allowed are refused though their records would fit; the limits are read at the root of the file system, so a volume with smaller ones is not measured. The tests size each path from the limits read (the 1000-byte directory below is PATH_MAX less 24, the 849-byte `state_dir` PATH_MAX less 175, and the link under `base_dir` leads to a path of PATH_MAX and one bytes), so each case meets its limit where PATH_MAX is another number than macOS's 1024: measured before, on the Linux CI jobs (3.11, 3.12 and 3.13, PATH_MAX 4096), the three tests sized for 1024 failed, the link under `base_dir` and the linked 1000-byte `[rubrics].dir` loading with no `ConfigError`, and the 849-byte `state_dir` passing `doctor` (exit 0) and a committed `daily` writing below it; after, on macOS (3.11 and 3.13), each case is met as before, and with PATH_MAX taken as 900 as well. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): a 300-byte name in `[run].state_dir`, `[rubrics].dir`, `[source].fixtures_dir` or the `--config` path made `doctor` and `daily` fall with `OSError: [Errno 63] File name too long`, exit 70; in `[run].base_dir`, `doctor` fell the same way and `daily` refused with a `SafetyRefusal`; a 300-byte `[rubrics].enabled` id made `doctor` fall too, and so did a `[rubrics].dir` linked to a 1000-byte directory; and a `state_dir` of 849 bytes passed `doctor`, then made a committed `daily` fall the same way mid-run after it had written records, with no receipt | `config.py` | `test_a_name_past_name_max_in_any_configured_path_is_a_config_error`, `test_a_name_past_name_max_in_a_skill_root_path_is_a_config_error`, `test_a_name_is_measured_in_bytes_not_characters`, `test_a_name_of_exactly_name_max_bytes_loads_and_one_more_is_refused`, `test_a_rubric_id_too_long_for_a_file_name_is_a_config_error`, `test_a_path_past_path_max_is_a_config_error`, `test_a_path_length_is_measured_in_bytes_not_characters`, `test_a_long_path_is_quoted_bounded_and_masked_in_the_refusal`, `test_a_root_or_rubric_id_in_the_refusal_is_quoted_bounded_and_masked`, `test_the_reserve_is_measured_with_the_ancestors_resolved`, `test_a_base_dir_that_resolves_too_long_is_named_as_itself`, `test_a_linked_rubrics_directory_that_resolves_too_long_is_a_config_error`, `test_a_state_dir_that_leaves_no_room_for_the_deepest_record_is_refused`, `test_the_control_the_widest_state_dir_allowed_runs_a_committed_daily`, `test_doctor_and_daily_name_a_long_path_as_a_config_error_never_a_fall`, `test_a_config_path_past_the_limits_is_a_config_error`, `test_the_reserve_formula_follows_the_state_subdirectories` |

| A malformed value typed on the command line is masked and bounded in the refusal that names it | A value typed on the command line reaches its refusal through `quoted_value` (its `repr`, masked by the redaction shapes, cut at `QUOTED_VALUE_CHARS` with the count left unquoted): the run day in `validate_run_day` (`--day`, `--mutation-reviewed`), and the hyphenated day its hint repeats, the candidate id in `StateStore.candidate_path` (what `review`, `apply` and `rollback` look up), the page size in `_positive_int_argument`, the value argparse calls an invalid choice (`_EscapingParser._check_value`, a subcommand, a `set-aside --kind` or a scheduler flavor, with the choices still listed) and the words it does not recognize (`main` parses known arguments and quotes at most `QUOTED_EXTRA_ARGUMENTS` of the rest, counting the others); argparse's own messages that repeat the typed word whole, an ambiguous option (`daily --co=<value>`) and an explicit argument to a flag that takes none (`daily --commit=<value>`), are masked and, past `ARGPARSE_MESSAGE_CHARS`, cut in the middle with a count of the characters left out, keeping the end where argparse names the matches (`_EscapingParser.error`, `_bounded_argparse_message`); a page size spelled as int() reads it but with more digits than Python reads from text is told the digit limit instead of that it is not a whole number; a short value with nothing to mask reads as before, except an unrecognized word, which argparse printed bare and which is now quoted like the others. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): a 100,000-character `--day`, `--mutation-reviewed`, candidate id, `--limit`, subcommand, `set-aside --kind`, scheduler flavor, unknown option, ambiguous option or explicit argument to a flag each came back whole on stderr, about 100 KB; a credential pasted where `asi review` takes a candidate id, typed as a `--day` or after `--commit=`, was printed as typed; and a `--limit` of 5,000 nines was refused as not a whole number. Not covered by this row: a well-formed value, which a refusal or a not-found message may repeat bounded by its own pattern or the path limits but not masked (a `--mutation-reviewed` day that names no blocked run, a `CAND-` id with no record, a `--config` path with no file) | `cli.py`, `domain/candidate.py`, `app/state.py` | `test_a_long_run_day_is_quoted_bounded_and_its_reason_still_follows`, `test_a_long_candidate_id_is_quoted_bounded_by_review_apply_and_rollback`, `test_a_credential_typed_as_a_candidate_id_is_masked_in_the_refusal`, `test_a_page_size_with_more_digits_than_python_reads_is_told_so`, `test_a_long_page_size_that_is_not_a_page_size_is_quoted_bounded`, `test_an_unknown_choice_is_quoted_bounded_and_the_choices_still_listed`, `test_unrecognized_arguments_are_quoted_bounded_and_the_rest_counted`, `test_a_short_value_is_quoted_whole`, `test_a_credential_typed_as_a_run_day_is_masked_in_the_hint_too`, `test_a_value_that_is_not_a_number_at_all_is_not_told_the_digit_limit`, `test_an_ambiguous_option_or_an_explicit_argument_to_a_flag_is_masked_and_bounded`, `test_the_longest_message_this_module_builds_is_not_cut`, `test_a_credential_in_a_message_long_enough_to_cut_is_masked_whole`, `test_a_message_as_long_as_the_limit_is_printed_whole_and_one_more_is_cut` |
| The leak scan's hostname and jwt rules, and its line numbers, take time in proportion to the text | `scan_text` in the scanner finds each rule's matches through `_matches`: a rule with a `start` and a `run` (the `jwt` rule: `eyJ` at a word boundary, then token characters) is matched at each start and, when a start has no match, resumes where the run after it ends, as the redactor's jwt shape does; the `corp-hostname` rule starts only where a run of host characters starts (a lookbehind), since a match from inside a run also matches from its start; a finding's line is bisected from the line-break offsets, listed once per text; `selftest` checks that a rule with a start has a run and that its start finds its own positive sample. The matches found are the ones the patterns alone give. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): 32 KiB of letters and dashes took 3 to 4 s in the hostname rule (256 KiB was still running at three minutes) and 256 KiB of `-eyJ` starts 12 to 17 s in the jwt rule, each doubling four times as long; a tracked file may be up to 2 MB and the history patch is read whole, so by that growth a 2 MB file of such text would take hours in the hostname rule and minutes in the jwt rule (extrapolated, not run); and numbering 40,000 findings in 1.5 MiB took about 10 s, also four times as long per doubling. Not covered by this row: the scanner's other rules, which this change did not measure | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_a_long_run_of_host_characters_is_scanned_in_linear_time`, `test_text_full_of_jwt_starts_is_scanned_in_linear_time`, `test_many_findings_are_numbered_in_linear_time`, `test_the_control_each_rule_finds_what_its_old_pattern_found`, `test_the_control_a_finding_is_on_the_line_counted_from_the_start`, `test_selftest_refuses_a_start_without_a_run_and_a_start_that_skips_its_sample` |
| A rubric version below 1 is refused when the rubric loads | `Rubric.from_mapping` refuses a `[rubric].version` that is not a whole number of at least 1, the bound `RubricBinding` holds every stored candidate to, so `doctor` and `daily` name the rubric file and write nothing. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): with `version = 0` or `-5`, `doctor` said ready, `daily --commit` exited 0 with a validated candidate, `candidates` listed it with 0 unreadable records, and `review` exited 3 with `CandidateTampered`, saying the record had changed since it was written, which it had not | `domain/rubric.py` | `test_a_rubric_version_below_one_is_refused_at_load_by_its_file`, `test_doctor_and_daily_refuse_a_version_below_one_before_anything_is_written`, `test_the_control_a_version_of_one_or_more_is_reviewed_as_written` |
| A stop signal ends the model command with the run | `main` runs each command inside `_stop_signals_unwind`, which turns SIGTERM and SIGHUP (`STOP_SIGNALS`), when still at their default action, into `Stopped`, a `BaseException` raised in the main thread, so the `finally` in `run_bounded` ends the model or source command's process group, the lock is released and a half-written file removed; the command exits 128 plus the signal number with `stopped by SIGTERM` on stderr. The handlers stay taken over while a failure or the stop is reported, a second stop signal during the unwind is let pass, and the process's own command (`entry`, behind `asi` and `python -m agent_skill_improver`) leaves the stop signals ignored after a stop until it exits, so a burst of them still exits as the stop. Ctrl-C stays Python's own `KeyboardInterrupt`, raised at each press, and a stop signal during its unwind is let pass. A stop that arrives after `run_bounded` starts the command and before its read loop begins still ends the command, and a temporary file is named before it is created (`write_bytes_atomic`, `_write_pinned`), so a stop that lands as it is created still has it removed; it is created owner-only (0600), as `tempfile.mkstemp` created it, and a name another writer holds is left alone. A signal the caller ignores stays ignored (`nohup`), `main` puts back the handlers it found, off the main thread nothing is installed, and a command that catches every `Exception` cannot catch it either. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): SIGTERM or SIGHUP during a model call ended the run with no message (exit -15, -1), and the model command went on running, re-parented to process 1 and past its own timeout (alive 6 s after the signal with a 3 s timeout), until it wrote its answer to an output nobody read and ended on the broken pipe; Ctrl-C already ended it with the run. Measured with the first form of this change (3.11 and 3.13), which put the handlers back as the command ended: 40 SIGTERMs 2 ms apart printed the stop, then ended the run by the signal, exit -15, three times of three; and one of twelve SIGTERMs at random moments of an `apply` (3.13) left a `.tmp-*.part` file in the state's candidates directory, created by `tempfile.mkstemp` before the write's cleanup could name it. Not covered by this row: a signal that arrives while Python is still starting, before `main` runs, or after the command has returned, which ends the process by the signal as before (exit -15); a process the model command starts in a session of its own (`setsid`), which no group kill reaches; and an `apply` stopped after it wrote the skill and before it marked the candidate applied (measured once in those twelve), which the row on a stopped `apply` covers | `cli.py`, `adapters/bounded_process.py`, `jsonio.py`, `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_sigterm_or_sighup_mid_model_call_ends_the_model_command_with_the_run`, `test_after_a_stop_the_next_daily_runs_and_no_partial_file_is_left`, `test_the_control_a_hangup_nohup_ignores_still_does_not_stop_the_run`, `test_the_control_ctrl_c_still_ends_the_model_command_with_the_run`, `test_a_second_signal_while_the_first_unwinds_does_not_cut_the_cleanup_short`, `test_a_command_that_catches_every_error_still_does_not_catch_a_stop`, `test_main_restores_the_handlers_it_found_and_runs_off_the_main_thread`, `test_a_burst_of_stop_signals_still_exits_as_the_stop`, `test_the_process_entry_leaves_the_stops_ignored_and_main_puts_them_back`, `test_a_stop_signal_while_ctrl_c_unwinds_is_let_pass`, `test_the_control_each_ctrl_c_still_raises_as_pythons_own_does`, `test_a_stop_before_the_read_loop_still_ends_the_child`, `test_a_stop_while_a_failure_is_reported_is_a_stop_too`, `test_a_stop_as_a_temporary_file_is_created_leaves_no_temporary_file`, `test_the_control_a_temporary_name_already_taken_is_left_alone`, `test_a_temporary_file_is_owner_only_from_the_moment_it_is_created` |
| A skill document whose path is longer than the system opens is passed over, by its length | `_iter_skill_files` in `adapters/skill_fs.py` takes an entry whose `lstat` is refused with `File name too long` as a document passed over, with its length in bytes as the reason, and as a copy of the skill it would be, so that skill's canonical slot is withheld and a proposal against the skill is quarantined, naming the copy and its length; `doctor` names it in a warning and stays ready, and `daily` runs to its receipt. Any other error from that call was raised as before; since the row "An entry named `SKILL.md` that discovery cannot examine is passed over with why" it is passed over in the system's words, not by a length. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): a canonical root reached through a link to a deep directory, so that an entry resolved to 1,024 bytes, made `doctor` and `daily` fall with `OSError: [Errno 63] File name too long`, exit 70; at 1,023 bytes both ran | `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_an_entry_past_the_path_limit_is_passed_over_by_its_length`, `test_daily_runs_and_names_the_canonical_copy_it_passed_over`, `test_the_control_an_entry_at_the_path_limit_is_inventoried`, `test_any_other_refusal_of_that_call_is_not_taken_for_a_length` |
| An entry named `SKILL.md` that discovery cannot examine is passed over with why | `_iter_skill_files` in `adapters/skill_fs.py` judges each entry the walk lists under a document's name with `lstat`, then, for a link, whether what it names exists. An entry whose `lstat` is refused for a reason other than its length (as an entry in a directory this process may list but not search, mode 0o444) is now passed over as "an entry this process cannot examine (<the system's words>)", and a link whose `exists()` is refused (as a link to a file in a directory of mode 000) as "a symlink whose target this process cannot reach (<the system's words>)", an error that carries no words of the system's being named as it is; each is a copy of the skill it would be, as a document this process cannot read is. So `doctor` names each on a `WARN` line and in `skill_documents_passed_over` and says `verdict: ready`; `skills` lists each on a `passed over (<root>)` line; the daily receipt carries each in `skill_tree.skill_documents_passed_over`; a candidate against another skill is reviewed and applied past them; and in place of a skill's own document (its folder made 0o444, or the document made such a link) one withholds that skill's canonical slot, so `daily` quarantines the skill, naming the copy and why, and proposes nothing against its other copy. Neither the entry nor what it names is touched. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, a committed run with one validated candidate, its token taken, then in the canonical root a folder `bad` holding a `SKILL.md` that is a link to a file in a directory of mode 000, or a folder `locked` of mode 0o444 holding a `SKILL.md`): `doctor`, `skills`, `review`, `apply` and `daily` each exited 70, "internal error: this is a bug in asi, not a negative answer", with a `PermissionError` traceback from discovery; with the candidate's own folder made 0o444, or its document made such a link, `doctor`, `skills` and `daily` exited 70 the same way, and `review` and `apply` exited 3 (`BaseHashMismatch`, the skill file "could not be read", and `LinkedTargetRefused`). After, on both: with either entry present the five exit 0 and `doctor` says `verdict: ready`; with the candidate's own folder or document so made, `doctor`, `skills` and `daily` exit 0, `skills` counting the copy passed over, and `review` and `apply` refuse as before. `docs/OPERATIONS.md` lists the two reasons under `skill_tree.skill_documents_passed_over`, and under `LinkedTargetRefused` no longer gives such a link as what stops the target check's later judgments. The long-path row's test of another error of that call, which expected it raised, now expects it passed over in these words, once for each root's copy, as the long-path module checks; and in the row "A target spelled outside every root is refused with the candidate and how to close it", such a link no longer stops the judgments' discovery: past it the spelling is named and, so configured, `review` shows a token, while that row's test of judgments that cannot finish now refuses their reading of the inventory in the link's place. 14 mutants (each error raised as before; the other error of `lstat` taken for a length; each entry passed over as no copy of a skill; each in the whole error's words, and in the system's words alone; each in the other's words; the link whose target cannot be reached taken for a dangling one, and taken as reached; and that row's judgments' error let through) are each caught on both by the module of this row with the long-path module and the module of that row. On the tree before this row, the module's tests of `doctor`, `skills`, `review` and `apply`, `daily`, an entry in place of a skill and the system's words fail, and its test that the entries are left as they were passes; the long-path module's test of another error fails there (it is raised), and so does that row's test of a path through an alias past such an entry, while its test of judgments that cannot finish passes | `adapters/skill_fs.py`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, `tests/test_a_skill_path_longer_than_the_system_opens_is_passed_over.py`, `tests/test_a_target_spelled_outside_every_root_is_refused_with_the_candidate.py` | `test_doctor_names_each_entry_and_why_on_a_warn_line`, `test_skills_lists_each_entry_and_why`, `test_review_and_apply_of_another_skill_take_its_candidate_past_them`, `test_daily_runs_and_its_receipt_names_each_entry_and_why`, `test_the_entries_are_left_as_they_were`, `test_such_an_entry_in_place_of_a_skill_withholds_its_canonical_slot`, `test_another_error_of_either_call_is_passed_over_in_the_systems_words`, `test_a_path_through_an_alias_names_it_past_an_entry_discovery_passes_over` |
| An `apply` stopped after it wrote the skill is finished by running it again | `write_canonical` writes the backup manifest before the skill rather than after it, and removes it where the skill is known to hold its original text again (a write the platform refused, a write that did not verify and was put back). An `apply` that finds the skill no longer at the candidate's base records the apply instead of refusing when the skill holds exactly the candidate's new text and the newest backup manifest of this candidate records that write, from the candidate's base to that text, for the same document (`_earlier_write_of`, `earlier_write`); the record's own integrity is checked as on a first run (`verify_full_integrity`) but not against the base text, which the skill no longer holds and which the earlier run checked before it wrote; the approval token is verified before anything is recorded (a wrong one is `ApprovalRequired`); and the report says `finishes` and the receipt `finishes_an_earlier_run`. Where those conditions do not hold, the apply is refused with `BaseHashMismatch` as before: a manifest that records another base, another text or another document or cannot be read, and an edit made after the stop, even with the manifest edited to record it. The apply's receipt is written before the candidate is marked applied, so a stop between the two leaves a candidate the next apply finishes. A `rollback` of a `validated` candidate whose skill still holds its base is refused as never applied (`_still_at_its_base`), since the manifest written ahead of the skill outlives an apply stopped before its skill write. A stop signal during `apply` or `rollback` adds a line saying to run the same command again (`FINISH_BY_RUNNING_AGAIN`; tested with SIGTERM, and SIGHUP takes the same path), which for `apply` also says that if the stopped run had recorded the apply, the rerun is refused as already `applied` with nothing left to do; a `rollback` stopped after its restore is finished by running it again, as before, by finding the previous text in place. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): an `apply` stopped by SIGTERM as its skill write returned left the skill holding the candidate's text, the candidate `validated`, the copy taken before the write and no manifest, so `rollback` answered that the candidate was never applied (`GateFailed`) and the next `apply` was refused with `BaseHashMismatch`, saying the skill had changed since the proposal, each exit 3; an apply left after its manifest and before its record (made by applying and putting the record back) was refused the same way, while `doctor` said ready and the next `daily` ran `ok`. Measured with the first form of this change (3.13): a `rollback` after an apply stopped before its skill write reported `already_restored`, closed the candidate as `rolled-back` and left its apply refused. Not covered by this row: Ctrl-C, which raises Python's own `KeyboardInterrupt` with no such line, though running the same apply again finishes it the same way (measured on 3.13); `doctor`, which says ready over an apply left this way and does not name it; `review`, which still refuses such a candidate with `BaseHashMismatch`; and a crash (SIGKILL, power loss), which leaves the same files at each of these points but was not run | `adapters/skill_fs.py`, `app/promote.py`, `cli.py` | `test_an_apply_stopped_as_its_skill_write_returned_is_finished_by_running_it_again`, `test_an_apply_stopped_as_it_records_its_write_is_finished_too`, `test_the_second_run_still_needs_the_candidates_token`, `test_the_control_an_edit_after_the_stopped_apply_is_refused_as_before`, `test_an_edit_the_manifest_is_edited_to_record_is_refused_too`, `test_a_manifest_that_does_not_record_this_write_is_not_taken_for_it`, `test_the_control_a_write_the_platform_refuses_leaves_no_manifest`, `test_the_control_a_write_that_does_not_verify_and_is_put_back_leaves_no_manifest`, `test_a_rollback_after_an_apply_stopped_before_its_skill_write_leaves_it_to_apply`, `test_rollback_restores_an_apply_stopped_as_its_skill_write_returned`, `test_a_stopped_rollback_says_to_run_it_again_and_running_it_again_finishes_it`, `test_a_stop_after_the_apply_was_recorded_says_the_rerun_has_nothing_to_do`, `test_the_control_a_stop_of_another_command_says_nothing_of_running_it_again` |
| A forged delimiter is neutralized in time in proportion to the text | `wrap_untrusted` finds a forged delimiter with `_FENCE_RE`, which tries a run of `<` from its first one only (a lookbehind: what follows the run is never a `<`, so a start inside the run fails whenever its first one did) and has one way to split the space around the optional slash, where the old pattern had one for each place in it. Each match the old pattern found begins, at the same place, with a match of this one, and each of this one's lies inside one of the old one's, compared on 20,000 random strings of delimiter pieces and Unicode space (this one's end at the fence word and its `>`, as the row "A forged delimiter hides no text but its own" says). Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): a run of `<` took 0.29 to 0.33 s to fence at 4,000 characters, 1.2 to 1.3 s at 8,000, 4.7 to 5.2 s at 16,000 and 10.4 to 11.6 s at 24,000 (over two runs each), the default `max_skill_text_chars` a skill text is clipped to before it is fenced into a prompt, on which config sets no upper limit; `<<` and then 24,000 spaces took 4.7 to 6.2 s. After, on both, 24,000 of either took about a millisecond. | A state directory this process cannot search is refused in one line, not a crash | `_reach_reason` in `app/state.py` measures the configured path as written, the state directory last, with `lstat`, and names the first real directory that `os.access` says this process may not search, with its mode, the cause and the fix for that cause (`_unsearchable_reason`): a directory of this user's whose owner search bit is clear gets `chmod 700` when it is the state directory and search permission (`chmod u+x`) or another `[run].state_dir` when it is above it; one another user owns gets "ask its owner for search permission" or another `[run].state_dir`; one whose mode grants its owner search is named as refused by an access control list or another policy, to be removed (`chmod` leaves an ACL in place). A link, a file, or an absent or unmeasurable component ends the walk with no verdict and is left to the layout checks, so a state directory that is a symlink is still refused as a symlink (a link above it in the configured path is resolved when the config loads, as before, so through the CLI the walk measures its target); a `..` steps back only over a directory the walk has just measured, so the directory named is the one reached. `layout_problems` asks it first and returns its reason alone, so every check and repair that waits on a sound layout waits on it; `verify_layout` (so `ensure`) and `check_dir` (so `dir`) raise it first, as `ConfigError`, exit 3, before anything is created or re-moded. `doctor` asks `reach_problem` before `Path.exists`, reports the reason as its one state `PROBLEM` (exit 1), prints `state perms ... not measured: this process cannot reach inside the state directory` instead of counts, and its JSON gains `state_permissions.unreachable` (the reason, or null on any other tree) with `unsafe_layout` left empty. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): a state directory at mode 0600 or 0000, or with its parent at either mode, fresh or built, ended `doctor`, `daily --commit`, `candidates`, `set-aside`, `review`, `apply` and `rollback` as internal errors (exit 70) with a `PermissionError` traceback; with the directory two levels up at either mode, `doctor` did too, and the other six refused it as a `SafetyRefusal` that named the directory below it as an ancestor that "cannot be inspected" and said every state directory "must be reachable without following a symlink". Also measured before, on 3.13, for `daily --commit`, `doctor` and `candidates`: exit 70 with a traceback for the state directory or its parent at 0200, the state directory at 0400, a state directory not yet created under a parent at 0600, a deny-search ACL entry on a 0700 state directory or on its 0755 parent, a parent sharing the state directory's name at 0600, and `/private/var/root` (root's, 0750) above it; after, each is the one reason with the cause the entry shows, the ACL and root-owned cases named as such and given no chmod fix. After, on both: each of the 84 runs above gives the one reason naming the directory at fault, on stderr in one line or as `doctor`'s one `PROBLEM`, with no traceback, that directory still at the mode it was given and every entry below it as found, modes included. `discover`, which opens the state directory too, measured the same way (the state directory or its parent at 0600 or 0000, fresh or built, 3.11 and 3.13): exit 70 with a traceback before in all 8 cases, the one reason in one line, exit 3, after; `skills`, `rubrics`, `scheduler-template` and `selftest` do not need it and exit 0 before and after, both Pythons. Stated limits: `os.access` answers for the real user id, and the superuser searches any directory, so the module is skipped when run as root; the ACL test runs on macOS only, and the other-owner wording is tested by reporting this user's directory under another user id; a directory refused for more than one cause is named for the first found, in the order another owner, the owner's search bit, a policy, so after that fix a second refusal may name the next; `permission_problems`, `foreign_entries` and `misplaced_directories` still ask `Path.exists` before the layout, which no command reaches under such a directory | `app/state.py`, `cli.py` | `test_every_state_command_refuses_an_unsearchable_root_in_one_line`, `test_doctor_names_it_as_its_one_state_problem_and_measures_nothing`, `test_a_directory_above_it_that_cannot_be_searched_is_named`, `test_the_control_a_root_it_can_search_but_not_list_still_runs`, `test_the_reason_names_the_first_directory_on_the_way_it_cannot_search`, `test_a_link_on_the_way_is_left_to_the_layout_checks`, `test_the_store_refuses_before_it_creates_or_repairs_anything`, `test_the_reason_names_a_path_with_a_line_break_on_one_line`, `test_the_fix_named_matches_why_search_is_refused`, `test_a_directory_an_acl_shuts_is_named_as_such_not_as_its_mode` |
| A credential glued to an escape, an underscore or a non-ASCII letter is masked | Every shape in `domain/redaction.py` that began at `\b` begins at `_LEAD` instead: not right after an ASCII letter or digit, unless that letter or digit ends an escape (`\n`, `\t`, `\r`, `\b`, `\f` or `\v`; `\x` and two hex digits, `\u` and four, `\U` and eight; `%` and two; or an `m` after a digit, `;` or `[`, as a terminal colour code ends, whether or not the escape character is there). The six vendor-prefix shapes end at `_TAIL`, not right before an ASCII letter or digit, where `\b` had left a token followed by a non-ASCII letter unmasked (any of the six) and one followed by `_` unmasked (the AWS shape and the `gh`-prefixed GitHub one), the Slack shape masking only part of either. So a key after a line break in a `repr` is masked, and with it the refusals `quoted_value` writes (a model's verdict, schema or `conversation_id`, a typed run day or candidate id); so is a key after an escape in JSON text, after `%20` in a URL or after a colour code, a token with `_` or a non-ASCII letter on either side, and an assignment whose key name ends a longer name (`DB_` before the password key). That last reaches past environment files: any snake_case name ending in a key name the assignment shape knows now counts as that key name, so `csrf_token = request.form.get('csrf')` loses its value as `token = request.form.get('csrf')` already did; across this repository's own Python, Markdown and TOML files at HEAD (51,230 lines) the assignment shape masks 39 lines against 33 before, on both Pythons, and it replaces a value rather than driving a refusal. An `&` written `\u0026` or `%26` before a URL parameter has its value masked when either shape knows its name: by the URL parameter shape when that one does (see the row on a credential in a signed, escaped or nested URL), otherwise by the assignment shape when the `=` is written as such (`passwd`, `session_token`), which in JSON text takes the closing quote and what follows with it; a name neither knows (`private_key`), or a name only the assignment shape knows with its `=` written `%3D`, is left as it was. Glued to an ASCII letter or digit in any other way, a shape is still taken for part of a longer word and left alone. Each lead sits behind a lookahead on the characters the shape can begin with, for speed alone, and each of those characters has a glued sample (both cases where the shape ignores case). Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both): of 72 glued samples (7 shapes, each after a `repr` `\n` or `\t`, a JSON `\n` or `\u00e9`, a `%20`, a colour code raw or in JSON, `MY_TOKEN_` or a non-ASCII letter, or before `_prod`; and 2 password assignments, after `DB_` and after a JSON `\n`), 67 were left as they were; `review` given a candidate id holding a line break and a key printed the key; and a key after a line break was quoted whole by `quoted_value` for each of the 7 shapes. Measured after: 0 of 72, the 8 unglued controls masked before and after. 4 MiB of prose is masked in 0.47 s on 3.13 and 0.38 s on 3.11, against 0.41 s and 0.36 s before (2.7 s and 2.5 s without the lookahead). Not covered by this row: the leak scanner's own rules, which still begin at `\b`; a terminal sequence other than a colour code (`ESC[K`) and a key whose own first letter is written as an escape, which are still left as they were; and speed, which is measured here and not tested | `domain/redaction.py` | `test_a_token_glued_to_an_escape_an_underscore_or_a_letter_is_masked`, `test_a_token_followed_by_an_underscore_or_a_non_ascii_letter_is_masked`, `test_an_assignment_whose_name_ends_a_longer_name_or_follows_an_escape_is_masked`, `test_json_text_holding_a_key_after_a_line_break_is_masked`, `test_text_full_of_underscore_and_escape_jwt_starts_is_masked_in_linear_time`, `test_a_snake_case_name_ending_in_a_key_name_is_masked_as_the_key_name_is`, `test_a_url_parameter_after_an_escaped_ampersand_is_masked`, `test_a_model_value_with_a_line_break_before_a_key_is_quoted_masked`, `test_an_unknown_verdict_holding_a_key_after_a_line_break_is_refused_masked`, `test_a_candidate_id_typed_with_a_line_break_before_a_key_is_masked`, `test_the_control_a_shape_glued_to_an_ascii_letter_or_digit_is_left_alone`, `test_the_control_prose_and_longer_names_are_left_alone` |
| A credential in a signed, escaped or nested URL is masked, and only its value | The URL parameter shape in `domain/redaction.py` begins at `_URL_PARAM`: `?` or `&` as written, `&` as JSON text escapes it (`\u0026`), or either percent-encoded, as in a URL carried inside another one's parameter (`%3F`, `%26`); then an optional `X-Amz-` or `X-Goog-` prefix, a name it knows (`security-token` now among them), and `=` or `%3D`. Its value (`_URL_VALUE`, 8 characters or more as before) ends at the next parameter (`&`, `\u0026`, or `%26` before a name and `=` or `%3D`), at a `#`, or at a character a URL cannot hold as written: whitespace, a double quote, or a single quote followed by whitespace, the end of the text or another such character (`"`, `<`, `>`, `[`, `]`, `^`, a backtick, `{`, a vertical bar, `}`). It ends too at a backslash escape, escaped once or more, standing for an ASCII character Python counts as whitespace, a double quote, `#` or `&` (`\"`, `\n`, `\u000A`, `\u0022`, `\u0026`, either case of hex). Anything else is part of the value, escapes included (a backslash before a slash, `\u002B`, `\'`): the assignment shape cannot mask past a placeholder, so a value cut short at a character it can hold would leave the rest of the secret in the text. A run of backslashes is taken whole, so a long one is read once. So a signed S3 or Cloud Storage URL has its `X-Amz-Signature` or `X-Goog-Signature` masked and keeps every other parameter; a parameter after a JSON-escaped or percent-encoded `&` is masked whichever of the shape's names it has; a parameter of a nested URL is masked and its neighbours kept; a URL in double quotes, or in single quotes before a space, a bracket or the end of the text, keeps its closing quote and what follows it; and a session token written with a backslash before every slash, as PHP's `json_encode` writes JSON, is masked whole. The shape still replaces a value and never drives a refusal. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both): of 11 URLs holding a secret (a signed S3 URL's signature and its session token, a Cloud Storage signature, each signature again in JSON text, `sig` and `key` after `\u0026`, `sig` after `%26`, a `token` and a `sig` in a nested URL, and a plain `access_token`), 9 kept it, the two masked being the plain parameter and the session token (that one by the assignment shape); of 5 URLs whose text after the value must survive, 3 lost it to the placeholder (a URL in JSON with a key after it, one in single quotes, one whose next parameter follows `\u0026`) and 1 kept its secret (a nested URL whose next parameter follows `%26`). Measured after: 0 of 11 kept their secret; of the 5, 1 lost text, the single-quoted URL followed by `)`, whose `')` a URL can hold and so goes with the value, byte for byte as before. Against HEAD, with the same results on both Pythons, a secret split in two by each of 71 characters and escapes, under each of 11 names, in each of 8 settings (6,248 texts: a parameter in the middle, one first and last, after `\u0026` in JSON text, in JSON text that writes a backslash before every slash, nested, after `%26`, in single quotes before `)`, and before a space): the first half was left in 2,201 before and is left in none; some part is left in 1,144 against 2,332, and each of the 644 second halves left that HEAD masked follows a stop listed above (a double quote, a single quote before one, a `#` in JSON text, `%26` before a name and `=` or `%3D`, `\"`, `\n`, `\t`, `\u0020`, `\u0022`, `\u0023`, `\u0026`), where by the URL's own reading the value has ended. 4 MiB of prose, and of URL-heavy text, is masked in about a tenth more time than before (best of 5: 0.48 s and 0.55 s on 3.13, 0.39 s and 0.47 s on 3.11, against 0.44 s and 0.50 s, and 0.37 s and 0.43 s, before), and text built to strain the value (one run of 4 Mi backslashes, 4 Mi single quotes, `%26` before long names that have no `=`, unfinished `\u` escapes) in time linear in its length (3.13: each doubling about twice as long). 32 mutants (each start spelling dropped, `\u0026`, `%3F` or `%26`; `%3D` dropped; no vendor prefix, or `X-Amz-` alone; no `security-token`; a value of any length; the old shape back, and the first cut of this value back; the value running past every `%26`, stopping at any `%26`, or stopping at one before a name with no `=` or only before a raw `=`; running past a double quote; a single quote always going with the value or always ending it, or going on before whitespace, the end of the text, a bracket, a double quote, or `<` and `>`; any backslash ending it, every run going on, a run not taken whole, the escape letters read in either case, and each of `\u0026`, `\u0022`, `\u000A`, upper-case hex, `\n` and `\"` going on) are each caught on both by `tests/test_redaction.py` and the modules of this row and of the row on a credential glued to an escape. Not covered by this row: a parameter name the shape does not know (`X-Goog-Credential` among them); an `=` or `&` written any other way (`\u003d`, or percent-encoded twice); a single quote before a character a URL can hold, such as `)` or `,`, which goes with the value; and a secret that itself holds one of the stops above (a raw double quote, `%26` before a name and `=`) | `domain/redaction.py` | `test_a_signed_cloud_store_url_has_its_signature_and_session_token_masked`, `test_the_parameters_beside_the_signature_are_kept`, `test_a_parameter_after_an_ampersand_written_as_a_json_escape_is_masked`, `test_a_parameter_of_a_url_nested_in_another_is_masked_and_its_neighbours_kept`, `test_a_value_ends_where_its_string_or_line_ends`, `test_an_escape_a_url_can_hold_is_part_of_the_value`, `test_a_quote_or_an_encoded_ampersand_a_url_can_hold_is_part_of_the_value`, `test_the_control_ordinary_query_parameters_are_left_alone`, `test_the_control_a_short_value_is_left_alone_and_none_drives_a_refusal` |
| A Stripe secret or restricted key, an npm token or a Hugging Face token is masked and refused | Three vendor shapes join `domain/redaction.py` and `tools/leakscan.py` under the same names, and the redactor counts each as a credential on its own evidence (`HIGH_CONFIDENCE_LABELS`): `stripe-secret-key`, `sk_` or `rk_`, then the mode (`live`, `test` or `prod`; Stripe's documentation names the first two, the gitleaks rule also takes the third) and `_`, then at least 20 letters and digits with no ceiling, since with one, `_TAIL` would leave a longer key unmasked; `npm-token`, `npm_` and exactly 36 letters and digits; `huggingface-token`, `hf_` and exactly 34 letters. A publishable Stripe key (`pk_`) is meant to be shown and is left alone, as is a longer run after `npm_` or `hf_` (`npm_config_cache`, a C or Objective-C name after `hf_`) and an upper-case prefix, which none of the three vendors is documented to issue; so is a placeholder in snake case (`sk_test_your_secret_key_here`, `hf_your_hugging_face_access_token_now`), since none of the three bodies holds `_`. Each begins at `_LEAD` and ends at `_TAIL`, as the other vendor shapes do, behind a lookahead on its first two or three characters rather than its first (`_lead` now takes what follows), because `r`, `s`, `n` and `h` are common letters. So each is masked in a transcript and in what `quoted_value` and `listed_value` show; a skill file holding one is refused (`CredentialInSkill`) before the improver or the judge is called; a replacement that adds one is refused; a configured child environment value holding one is named whatever the variable is called; and the leak scanner reports one in the repository. In an assignment the vendor shape masks the value before the assignment shape reaches it, so `api_key = <key>` reads `api_key = [REDACTED:stripe-secret-key]`, one mask. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both): of 8 samples (`sk_` and `rk_` in each of the three modes, with bodies of 20 to 120 letters and digits, a mixed-case npm token and a Hugging Face token) in prose after a space, `redact`, `quoted_value` and `listed_value` masked 0 (a sample counts as masked when no 12 of its characters in a row are shown, since a cut shows a long value's head), `credential_labels` named 0, so 0 replacements adding one were refused and 0 child environment values holding one were named, the leak scanner reported 0, and a `tdd` skill holding one of the three kinds (one run each) made a candidate, the token in 11 prompts, the improver's and the judge's among them; only an assignment the assignment shape knows (`token=`) masked one. After: 8 of 8 in each, and each such skill is refused with only the scorer called and the token in no prompt. Across the repository's own 161 text files at this change (57,220 lines) neither the redactor nor the scanner finds any of the three. 4 MiB of English prose is masked in 0.67 s on 3.13 and 0.55 s on 3.11, against 0.55 s and 0.47 s before (best of five, on a loaded machine). Not covered by this row: Stripe's organization keys (`sk_org_`), whose format its documentation does not give; a placeholder of the same shape (`hf_` and 34 `x`, or `sk_test_` and a camel-case `YourStripeSecretKeyGoesHere`), which is masked and refused as a key is, as the other vendor shapes treat theirs; the leak scanner's rules, which still begin and end at `\b`, so the scanner misses one followed by `_`, which the redactor masks; and speed, which is measured here and not tested | `domain/redaction.py`, `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_the_samples_are_as_long_as_the_formats_say`, `test_each_is_masked_where_it_stands_and_named_by_its_shape`, `test_an_assignment_holding_one_is_masked_once_by_its_shape`, `test_a_quoted_or_listed_value_holding_one_shows_its_placeholder`, `test_a_replacement_adding_one_is_refused_by_its_shape`, `test_a_configured_child_environment_value_holding_one_is_named`, `test_the_leak_scanner_reports_each_by_its_shape`, `test_a_skill_holding_one_is_refused_before_any_model_role_sees_it`, `test_the_control_what_is_none_of_them_is_left_alone` |
| The parser and the integrity check quote what they found in an edited candidate record, masked, bounded and on one line | The strict parser of a stored validation record (`ValidationRecord.parse` in `domain/validation.py`) and the integrity check run before `review` and `apply` (`verify_full_integrity` in `domain/integrity.py`) name each piece of text they refuse that was read from the record through `quoted_value`, so it is masked, kept on its line and cut at its length, and each list of them through `quoted_values` (new in `domain/redaction.py`), which names the first 5 that way and counts the rest (`and N more`). In the parser: an unknown key, named bare when it reads as a field name and quoting would leave it as it is (so `carries unknown key(s): extra` reads as before), else quoted, the way the config loader names a key it refuses; a digest; a case kind; a case key and what it recomputes to; a judgement schema; a preferred label; a preferred arm with its seed and case key; a count or version below its floor; the record schema; the evidence call count; a duplicate rubric id, ref, outcome or judgement; a case recorded as both kinds; a target case no ref names; a judgement about no outcome; and the missing and unused rubric bindings. In the stored gate's comparison with its recomputation: the value on each side, the recomputed one included, since a case key in it is built from stored text; and a key on one side only, as `path.key` when it is plain, as before, else as `path[...]` with the key quoted. In the integrity check: the recorded patch hash and base hash, by their 12-character prefix as before when each is a digest and quoted when it is not (`_recorded_digest`); the skill file's name and folder, the skill id, the record's candidate id, the case keys claimed and not bound or bound and not claimed, target cases the candidate does not claim or that have no replay, a claimed failure's rubric id and version, a bound rubric's id, and an outcome's case, rubric and criterion. The integrity refusal lists its first 20 problems (`PROBLEMS_SHOWN`) and then `and N more not shown`, its first line keeping the full count. A plain value reads as its `repr` did while that is at most 120 characters, and a plain key of up to 64 is named bare. The numbers the integrity check reads from the record (the patch's added and removed lines, a bound rubric's version) and the parser's preference-ratio bound are printed as before: by then each is a number (the candidate loader reads the counts with `int`, the parser refuses any other type), and the state loader refuses an integer larger than any double, so none holds a line break or a credential. Called directly with an integer of 4,000 digits where it quotes one (for example the evidence call count, a policy count below 0, a rubric binding's version below 1, a claimed failure's rubric version, a value in the stored gate's comparison), either check cuts it; the patch's added and removed lines and a bound rubric's version are printed whole. The state loader refuses such an integer before `review` or `apply` gets that far. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both), with a real validated candidate whose stored record was then edited and `review`, `review --json` and `apply` run on it: an unknown key holding a `ghp_` token, a line break and `config error: forged` printed the token and a second refusal line beginning `config error: forged`, in the record, its gate, the gate's metrics or policy, and a judgement's case (5 places); the same text as the record's digest, candidate id, a case key, a rubric id, a rubric source digest, a fixed case key, the schema, the skill id, or a claimed failure's rubric, criterion or conversation printed the token whole, its line break already escaped by `repr` (11 more); a line break and `config error: forged` before the token, as the recorded patch hash, printed a second line beginning `config erro`, the 12 characters of its prefix keeping the break; a key or a digest of 1,000,000 characters made a refusal of 1,000,578 and 1,000,624 bytes; and 10,000 extra metrics keys made one of 599,359 bytes, 10,000 unknown keys 69,466 and 10,000 extra claimed failures 1,158,857. After, on both: none of the 17 prints the token or a second line, the long key and digest make 732 and 776 bytes, and the three volumes 1,687, 617 and 1,681; `doctor`, which reads none of this, prints the same before and after (899 bytes under the probe's state path). 57 mutants (`quoted_values` unmasked, unbounded or not counting the rest; a key counted plain whatever it holds, whenever the field-name pattern matches it, masked or not, or never; the unknown keys listed raw, named with `str`, or all quoted; a key on one side only always named plain; the problem list unbounded or not counting the rest; a recorded hash always shown by its prefix, or always quoted; and each of the 43 places this row quotes a value or a list shown with `repr` again) are each caught on both by the module of this row. Not covered by this row: the refusal of a candidate whose gate failed or whose skill file no longer matches its base in `app/promote.py` (`GateFailed`, `BaseHashMismatch`), which the row on review and apply quoting the status, the gate reasons and the skill path they refuse covers, with what that row leaves out; a declared id that does not match the file, which the state store names with `repr` (`read_candidate` in `app/state.py`, and the listing `doctor`, `candidates` and the `daily` queue guard print), so a token in it is printed whole on one line (the schema, counts, version and source position it refused the same way are now quoted or named by their field, as the row "The candidate loader quotes the schema it refuses and names a number it cannot read by its field, never by its text" says); what `review` prints of a record that parses (a source's thread id under `--json`, the hypothesis), which a token in still reaches, the mirror paths it prints being those the tree holds, as the row "Review and apply list the mirror copies the tree holds, and the loader reads a record's lists as arrays of strings" says; and a credential of a shape the redaction does not know (`sk_live_`, `npm_` and `hf_` among them), or glued to an ASCII letter or digit before it, which the row on a credential glued to an escape leaves alone, either of which is printed as before | `domain/validation.py`, `domain/integrity.py`, `domain/redaction.py` | `test_each_stored_value_a_parse_refusal_names_is_quoted_masked_on_one_line`, `test_ten_thousand_unknown_keys_are_named_five_and_counted`, `test_a_key_that_is_a_credential_and_nothing_else_is_masked`, `test_a_megabyte_key_or_digest_is_cut`, `test_a_count_or_version_of_four_thousand_digits_is_cut`, `test_a_key_on_one_side_only_is_quoted_and_a_plain_one_named_as_before`, `test_each_stored_value_an_integrity_problem_names_is_quoted`, `test_a_claimed_failure_at_a_version_of_four_thousand_digits_is_cut`, `test_ten_thousand_differences_are_listed_twenty_and_counted`, `test_ten_thousand_claimed_failures_no_ref_binds_are_named_five_and_counted`, `test_review_and_apply_refuse_an_edited_record_without_printing_what_it_holds`, `test_the_control_the_record_as_written_parses_and_verifies`, `test_the_control_a_plain_value_or_key_reads_as_before` |
| Review and apply quote the status, the gate reasons and the skill path they refuse, masked, bounded and on one line | `review` and `apply` (`app/promote.py`) quote what they read from the candidate record when they refuse it. The status a candidate that is not validated is refused for is named through `quoted_value`, so it is masked, kept on its line and cut at its length, and a plain one reads as its `repr` did while that is at most 120 characters (`status is 'gate-failed', not 'validated'`). The skill file's path a base-hash refusal names (the file could not be read, or it now hashes to something else) is named through `named_path` (new in `domain/redaction.py`): bare, as before, when it reads as a path (ASCII letters, digits, the underscore, dot, slash, tilde, plus and hyphen only), is at most 512 characters (`NAMED_PATH_CHARS`) and nothing in it is masked; else quoted, masked and cut at 512. A read that failed is named by the error's number and the system's words for it (`[Errno 2] No such file or directory`), no longer followed by the file name the error repeats, or quoted when it has no number or no words (`why_unreadable`, now in `domain/redaction.py`; row "A refusal of a leaf it cannot open or read names its path once"). A recorded path holding a NUL character, which no file name can, is refused the same way before the read, the path quoted (`holds a NUL character`). `apply` now runs the record-only integrity check before it reads the stored gate, as `review` already did, so a validated record whose stored gate was edited to fail is refused as tampered (`status is 'validated' but the recorded gate did not pass`) before any of its stored reasons is read. The gate-failed refusal that follows names the reasons through `quoted_values`, the first 5 and a count of the rest; no stored record reaches it any more, since that check refuses a validated record whose gate did not pass, and its test stubs the check out. The recorded base hash's 12-character prefix is printed as before: in both commands the integrity check runs before the hash comparison and refuses a record whose base hash differs from its validation's, which the validation parser refuses unless it is a 64-character hex digest. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both apart from the length of a traceback), with a real validated candidate whose stored record was then edited and `review`, `review --json` and `apply` run on it: a status holding a `ghp_` token, a line break and `config error: forged` printed the token, its line break escaped by `repr` (357 bytes from `review`, 166 from `apply`), and a status of 1,000,000 characters made refusals of 1,000,295 and 1,000,104 bytes; with the stored gate edited to fail, `apply` printed a reason holding the token and the line break as written, the token and a second line beginning `config error: forged` (130 bytes), 10,000 reasons of 100 characters made 1,020,067 bytes, a reason that was not a string made `apply` exit 70 with a traceback, and reasons stored as one string were joined a character at a time (250 bytes), while `review` already refused all four as tampered; and with the planted text as the folder in the recorded path (the skill id edited to match) both commands printed the token and a second line, when the file could not be read (533 and 532 bytes) and when a different `SKILL.md` was planted there (462 and 378), with the planted text as the file's name `review` printed both (523; `apply` refuses that name as tampered), and a folder name of 1,000,000 characters made 2,000,404 and 2,000,403 bytes, the path printed twice, once inside the error; and a NUL character in the recorded folder made both commands exit 70 with a traceback (`ValueError: embedded null byte`), as one after the file's name did `review` (`apply` refuses that name as tampered, in 591 bytes before and after). After, on both: none of these prints the token, a second line or a traceback; the status refusals are 340 and 149 bytes and those of the long status 447 and 256; `apply` refuses each of the four edited gates as tampered in 610 to 652 bytes; the planted folder makes 427 and 426 bytes, 448 and 364 with a file planted in it, the planted file name 422 from `review`, the long folder name 894 and 893, and the NUL in the folder 398 and 397 and after the name 398 from `review`.
