| A target that opens the canonical file under another name is refused with how to take it | The target check (`assert_canonical_target` in `adapters/skill_fs.py`) judges a candidate's target by its name, and one file can carry more than one: on a case-insensitive volume a root spelled "skills-canonical" and one spelled "Skills-Canonical" open the same files, as two hard links to one file do. Such a target is still refused as no skill document in any configured root (`MirrorEditRefused`); `_canonical_copy_opened_by`, new there, finds the canonical copy whose file it opens (the same device and inode, by `os.stat`; a `stat` that fails, of the target or of a copy, gives no answer, and only canonical copies are compared), and the refusal then names that copy and its skill, says the check judges a target by its name, and (`canonical_target_refused` with `renamed`) says what lets the candidate be taken where it said "If that file is meant to be the skill's canonical copy, make it that again", which asked for what was so: when the name the candidate holds and the copy's differ only in letter case (`_in_letter_case_only`, new there, which folds case as Python's casefold does, so "Straße" and "STRASSE" are one spelling), "The name the candidate holds and the copy's differ only in letter case. To take it instead, give the file back the spelling the candidate holds, in the config's root or the names on disk"; when they differ by more, as for a hard link outside every root, which already has the candidate's name, "The name the candidate holds and the copy's differ by more than letter case, and a second name does not make a file the canonical copy. To take it instead, have discovery take the file by the name the candidate holds again, as it did when the candidate was written (a root re-pointed since then, pointed back)". Without a candidate (`rollback`, and the write's own check until the row "A refusal at the write names the candidate and leaves no backup directory") the words name the copy and no way to take it. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both, a committed run with one validated candidate, its token taken, then (C1) the config's canonical root spelled `Skills-Canonical`, (C2) the skill's directory renamed `TDD`, or (C3) its "SKILL.md" renamed "skill.md", on a case-insensitive volume, or (C4) the canonical root re-pointed at a copy of the tree, "skills-new", whose "tdd/SKILL.md" is a hard link to the old file, on any volume): `review` and `apply` each exited 3 with "it is not a skill document in any configured root, so this tool cannot tell whether it is canonical. ..." and "If that file is meant to be the skill's canonical copy, make it that again", which in C1, C2 and C4 asked for what was so; `review` changed nothing and `apply` only the run lock, the record stayed `validated` and no backup was made; with the old name given back (C1 to C3), or the root pointed back (C4), `review` exited 0 and `apply` with the same token applied it. After: C1 and C2 name the copy (in C2, of skill 'TDD', the name discovery now gives it) and the spelling given back as the way to take it; C4 names the copy and, as the way to take it, discovery taking the file by the candidate's name again, not that name given back, which the file has; C3, which no longer names a skill document at all, keeps the old words; the exit, what each command changed, the record and the absent backup are as before, and with the root pointed back `review` and `apply` take C4 as before. The rule held over every refusal of the skill store now lets through which of the three fixed ways to take a candidate the words give (`take_it`), counts five calls of `canonical_target_refused`, each in a `raise`, and holds the copy it names (`opened.path`) to `named_path`; the count of paths named rose from 77 to 78. 15 mutants (the check dropped; the new advice not given; the advice for names that differ in letter case only given always, and the one for names that differ by more given always; either one's words not given; the names compared exactly, and compared in lower case; a mirror copy compared too; a skill with no canonical copy not passed by; a failed `stat` of a copy or of the target raised; a match not returned; the skill named as the target's; the copy named as the target) are each caught on both. On the source before this change the new module and the skill-store refusal module give 9 failures and 2 errors on both Pythons and both temporary directories: 8 of the new module's 12 tests, the re-pointed root's in both its commands (the name given back, the control "skill.md", the mirror copies and the target that opens no file pass), and 2 of the other's. The loader clips, strips, drops and refuses nothing it did not before. The module of this row reads each of the five fields over empty, blank, plain and long (100,040 characters) text; text with precomposed and decomposed accents, an astral character and a lone surrogate; text holding C0 and C1 controls, DEL, a zero-width space, a bidi override and a line separator; text that masking twice would change; one example of each of the fifteen shapes `redact` knows, two of them across a line break; and the shortest text each of six shapes masks (12 to 20 characters; an assignment's value is six characters at least, so no shape masks a text shorter than 12); and the notes `apply` (a finishing run's among them), `rollback` and a blocked run write, two of them holding a credential where a path or an operator's name goes; and it compares what the loader keeps with what `redact` alone makes of it. | The candidate listing masks what it reads from a record and cuts what no real one could be | `candidates` lists records that no integrity check has read, so it now shows each one's status, skill id, canonical path and creation time, in the text and in `--json` alike, through `listed_value` (new in `domain/redaction.py`): masked with the shapes `redact` knows, then cut, the cut saying how many characters it left out (`... [N more chars not shown]`); the limit and the count are in characters, not bytes. Unlike `quoted_value` it adds no quotes, so a value that holds no credential shape and is no longer than its limit lists exactly as before, a skill id with spaces or letters outside ASCII included. The limits: 255 characters for the skill id (`LISTED_SKILL_ID_CHARS`; a skill id is a directory name, at most 255 bytes on APFS and ext4 and so at most 255 characters), 4,096 for the path (`LISTED_PATH_CHARS`; `PATH_MAX`, the longest path a system call takes by name, is 1,024 bytes on macOS and 4,096 on Linux), and 120 for the status and the creation time (`QUOTED_VALUE_CHARS`). The text line is built from the row the JSON carries, and `_emit` keeps it on one line as before. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both), with a real validated candidate whose record was then edited and `candidates`, `candidates --all`, `candidates --json` and `candidates --all --json` run on it (the byte counts are those under the probe's temporary directory: the JSON listings name the canonical path, which holds that directory's path unless the case edits it, and the text listings name no path): a `ghp_` token followed by a line break and `config error: forged` in the skill id was printed whole by all four (164, 159, 480 and 479 bytes), in the status by the two under `--all` (180 and 469), and in the canonical path or the creation time by the two JSON listings (419 and 418; 459 and 458); the line break was already spelled `\n` in the text and escaped in the JSON, so no line was forged. A value of 1,000,000 characters in the skill id made 1,000,098, 1,000,093, 1,000,414 and 1,000,413 bytes, in the status 1,000,118 and 1,000,407 under `--all`, in the path 1,000,357 and 1,000,356 from the JSON listings, and in the creation time 1,000,397 and 1,000,396. Not covered by this row: the record's id, listed as written, since it is the name the operator types to `review` it and the loader holds it to the candidate id's alphabet and 204 characters, so it holds no line break and no more than that, but a credential spelled in that alphabet after `CAND-` is printed whole; the counts of added and removed lines, which the loader reads as `int()` reads them, so a count given as text of up to 4,300 digits is listed whole (4,425 bytes from `candidates`, measured on both) and one of 4,301 makes the record unreadable; | An export's thread id is held to the limit a listed id is held to | `_scan` in `adapters/fixture_source.py` passes over an export whose thread id is longer than `MAX_THREAD_ID_LENGTH` (256 characters, in `domain/discovery.py`), the limit the command-line source already holds a listed id to at discovery (the row "A listed entry that cannot be taken as a thread is named on the receipt, not only dropped"); its reason gives the id's length and the limit and quotes the id through `quoted_value` (masked, then cut at 120), `doctor` warns for it, and the exports beside it are read as before. The length is the id's as written, padding included, and the rule runs before exports claiming one id are grouped (the row "Two exports claiming one thread are an ambiguity, not a choice"), so each export claiming one over-long id is passed over for its length and names no other. An id is its record's key in the receipt, the checkpoint and each scorecard and failure case. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both; `daily --commit` over the healthy fixtures and one export more): with a 2,000,000-character id the export was taken (6 ingested), the receipt and `daily --json` were about 4.0 MB each (about 10 KB without it), the checkpoint, the scorecard and the stored conversation about 2.0 MB each, and `doctor` said nothing of it; with a 341-character id beginning with a GitHub token, the token was in the receipt and on stdout as written. After, on both: each is passed over with its reason, and the run ingests 5, finds 2 failure cases and proposes 1, as without it; the receipt is about 11 KB for the 2,000,000-character id, and no state file or output holds the id; the token is masked; `doctor` warns once; a 256-character id, in ASCII or in two-byte characters, is still taken, and a 257-character one is passed over; and a healthy run's receipt, `daily --json` and `doctor` are unchanged but for their times. 10 mutants (no check; the limit reached at 256, or doubled; the length counted in UTF-8 bytes, or after stripping the id; the id quoted raw, or cut but not masked; the length left out of the reason; the export passed over and taken too; or dropped without a reason) are each caught on both. ## The daily path cannot mutate anything

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Prove it structurally, not by inspection | An AST test asserts `app/daily.py` does not import the promotion module and never calls the write method | `app/daily.py` | `test_the_daily_module_does_not_import_the_promotion_module`, `test_the_daily_module_never_calls_the_write_method` |
| Prove it observationally too | A full pass leaves the skill tree digest unchanged and no file content altered | `app/daily.py` | `test_the_skill_tree_digest_is_unchanged_by_a_full_pass`, `test_no_skill_file_content_changes_on_disk` |
| A dry run must persist nothing | `daily` without `--commit` writes no artifact under the state directory: the store withholds every record write in dry-run mode and records what it withheld, measured on the whole tree rather than one directory (an earlier version checked only the candidate directory while conversations, scorecards and quarantine records were being written). What a dry run leaves is the owner-only layout, the run lock and, on a fresh tree, the layout's README.txt, a fixed text that the layout writes whenever it is absent, the one file the test of a dry run from an empty state allows. Measured (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both), after a scout found the operations guide naming only the layout and the lock: a dry run on a fresh tree added the 13 state directories, the lock and README.txt (mode 0600, the same bytes on both) and nothing else; after a committed day, a dry run of the same day changed the lock's bytes alone, and one of the next day the lock's bytes and the modification time of the directory each model call's scratch is made and removed in; nothing outside the state directory changed in any of the three. The guide now names README.txt | `app/state.py`, `app/daily.py`, `cli.py` | `test_04_a_dry_run_persists_nothing`, `test_a_dry_run_from_an_empty_state_writes_no_artifact`, `test_a_dry_run_leaves_existing_state_byte_identical`, `test_the_raw_export_write_is_withheld_too` |
| A dry run may change existing state only by resetting a drifted mode | `ensure()`, which every `daily` run calls before anything else, resets any existing state file or directory whose mode is not owner-only and records which, directories included and a reset the store already made while a subdirectory was being opened for wiring; the receipt counts them (`state_modes_repaired`) and names them, the dry-run note says content is untouched, and the snapshot the dry-run tests compare includes each file's mode so a chmod cannot hide behind byte-identical content | `app/state.py`, `app/daily.py` | `test_a_dry_run_still_resets_a_drifted_mode_without_touching_the_bytes`, `test_a_reset_made_by_dir_before_the_walk_is_still_reported_once`, `test_a_directory_dir_creates_is_not_reported_as_a_repair` |
| A dry run must preview the verdict a commit would reach | Deferral attempts are counted before the write, so the cap decision is identical whether or not the write lands | `app/daily.py` | `test_a_dry_run_reaches_the_defer_verdict_a_commit_would` |
| A dry run must report the backlog on disk, not the proposals it withheld | `open_candidates_after` counts open-status candidate files on disk, so a dry run's own proposals never inflate it; `candidates_proposed` keeps the work the run did, and each candidate's `persisted` flag says whether its file reached disk | `app/daily.py`, `app/receipt.py`, `app/state.py` | `test_a_dry_run_reports_the_backlog_on_disk_not_the_proposals_it_withheld` |
| Promotion must never run through a withholding store | A store in dry-run mode refuses to write a promotion receipt or create a backup directory, so an apply cannot keep the edit and drop its own accounting | `app/state.py` | `test_promotion_refuses_a_store_that_withholds_writes` |
| The receipt legend may drift from the receipt | Every count key a run emits must be in the `counts` block of `docs/OPERATIONS.md` and vice versa, with an extractor control | `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, `app/receipt.py` | `test_every_emitted_count_is_documented_and_every_documented_count_is_emitted`, `test_the_legend_extractor_finds_keys_and_skips_prose_and_continuations` |

## Repeat runs, locks and determinism

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A same-day re-run must not re-score | The day key is the idempotence key | `app/daily.py` | `test_re_running_the_same_day_does_not_re_score`, `test_a_different_day_is_processed_again` |
| Two runs must not overlap | An exclusive lock is held for the pass and released even when the body raises | `app/lock.py` | `test_two_runs_cannot_hold_the_lock_at_once`, `test_the_lock_is_released_after_the_block`, `test_the_lock_is_released_even_when_the_body_raises` |
| Output must be reproducible | Two identical runs produce identical receipts | `app/receipt.py` | `test_two_identical_runs_produce_identical_receipts` |
| The same fully measured rejected replacement for the same failures should not cost another replay | Optional owner-only global FIFO remembers only new committed stable, fully measured replacement losses, at most 64 entries / 128 KiB of fingerprints, never replacement or transcript text; coverage/holdout-size/independence/error failures never teach. Baseline must reproduce every target as FAIL, target replacement and both holdout verdicts must be decided, every required judgement must return, and validation notes veto learning. Any target/holdout INSUFFICIENT vetoes memory; incomplete unseen measurements veto it too, though unseen remains report-only and never decides the gate. Degradation is never a lesson. Same skill, LF-normalized base/replacement, sorted claimed failure ids and examiner context (all enabled rubric byte hashes, gate/prompt, exact serialized caps including active unseen settings, scorer/judge commands and independence) gives an intentional skill-keyed propose refusal linked to the earlier candidate, not a new candidate, gate result or approval token. Missing memory is silent; damaged memory is named and left untouched; optional write failure keeps the candidate, receipt and checkpoint. A repeat does not renew FIFO position or timestamp; no qualifying committed loss leaves memory absent, and dry run withholds its prospective write. **Measured before:** real two-day CLI on CPython 3.11.15 / 3.13.0, each day 5 initial scorer + 8 replay scorer + 1 improver + 2 judge; 32 calls total on each interpreter. **Measured after:** on both interpreters, day1 retains those 16 calls; day2 has 5 initial + 1 improver + 0 replay + 0 judge, ok/0; two-day total 22, saving 10 calls (31.25%), with installed skill, original candidate and repeat memory bytes unchanged and identical improver prompt hashes between the two days. **18 mutants killed by named-test assertions on both CPython versions, with the unmutated named tests passing:** omit skill, base, examiner or claimed cases; strip significant whitespace in the shared digest helper; disable the repeat raise; admit any gate failure; ignore error notes; accept wrong schema; unbounded read; bypass dry-run store; publish a blocked run; fail to catch optional SafetyRefusal; refresh duplicate FIFO position; skip candidate/validation integrity; drop target-baseline FAIL; admit target/holdout INSUFFICIENT; admit unseen INSUFFICIENT. Two kills are unit-only (duplicate insertion and tampered candidate), one uses bounded-read descriptor instrumentation; these are not 18 CLI kills. **Not covered:** with c240 unseen withholding on, the shown failure set is re-drawn per dated candidate, so exact-repeat refusals are rare; savings apply mainly with withholding off. No prompt advice, near duplicates, holdout/withheld-only changed corpus, same-id edited source/backend nondeterminism, age expiry, legacy import, human decline/rollback, original-agent reruns or unreadable candidate repair. | `app/rejections.py`; `app/daily.py`; `app/state.py` | `tests/test_a_rejected_replacement_is_not_replayed_again.py` (`test_the_next_day_refuses_the_exact_replacement_before_replay_or_judge`, `test_a_crlf_repeat_has_the_same_lf_normalized_fingerprint`, `test_changed_skill_base_replacement_or_examiner_is_a_new_trial`, `test_the_same_text_for_different_failures_is_replayed`, `test_a_repeat_uses_one_slot_and_never_asks_for_another_candidate`); `tests/test_a_damaged_rejection_memory_does_not_block_a_proposal.py` (`test_bad_memory_is_named_ignored_and_left_untouched`, `test_the_optional_reader_never_reads_more_than_its_byte_budget`, `test_unwritable_memory_does_not_lose_the_candidate_or_daily_receipt`, `test_an_unreadable_candidate_still_stands_the_daily_run_down`, `test_the_cited_excerpt_guard_precedes_the_repeat_guard`); `tests/test_rejection_memory_is_bounded_and_only_remembers_completed_failures.py` (`test_an_incomplete_or_configuration_failure_is_not_remembered`, `test_memory_keeps_the_newest_sixty_four_distinct_entries`, `test_serialized_bytes_are_bounded_without_truncating_identity`, `test_memory_uses_locally_recomputed_candidate_and_validation_fields`, `test_memory_is_private_and_default_absent_without_a_committed_qualifying_failure`) |

## Public safety

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| No private content, ever | The scanner covers tracked content, tracked paths, full history and commit metadata; its banned literals are assembled from fragments so its own source is scanned like any other file | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_a_no_tracked_file_content_matches_any_rule`, `test_b_no_tracked_path_names_a_private_artifact`, `test_c_history_contains_no_banned_literal` |
| The scanner must be two-sided | Every rule fires on its own positive sample and stays silent on its negative one | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_a_every_rule_is_two_sided`, `test_c_the_scanner_fires_on_a_planted_absolute_home_path`, `test_e_the_scanner_stays_silent_on_ordinary_prose` |
| A word-boundary rule is fail-open for a name that appears inside identifiers | The product rule is bounded by letters rather than word characters, because an underscore is a word character and `\b` was silent on the module and filename spellings this tree actually used; the camel-case half is narrowed so it cannot fire on the AWS access-key-id prefix that really occurs here | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_d2_the_scanner_fires_on_the_internal_agent_product_name`, `test_d3_the_product_rule_respects_word_boundaries`, `test_d3b_the_product_rule_fires_on_the_identifier_form`, `test_d3c_the_product_rule_stays_silent_on_the_aws_key_prefix` |
| The whole-tree claim must be checkable by hand | A case-insensitive search over every tracked path and body returns zero occurrences of the product name and of the sampled title, using a pattern that is a strict superset of the word-boundary search a reviewer would run | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_d6_no_tracked_content_names_the_internal_product_or_sampled_title`, `test_d7_that_search_really_would_find_something` |
| No exemptions may accumulate quietly | The allowance mechanism exists, is printed in every report, and is empty | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_f_allowances_are_empty` |
| Fixtures must be synthetic and deterministic | The committed fixtures match their builder byte for byte, and no fixture matches any scanner rule | `examples/build_fixtures.py` | `test_a_committed_fixtures_match_the_builder_byte_for_byte`, `test_e_no_fixture_contains_a_banned_literal` |
| A public flip must need no cleanup | MIT licensed, no unpublishable classifier, no absolute home paths, empty runtime dependencies; a published clone may have remotes, but every fetch/push URL must be credential-free HTTPS or git-only SSH to a public host | `pyproject.toml`, `LICENSE`, `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_a_no_unpublishable_classifier`, `test_e_no_absolute_home_path_in_packaging_or_config`, `test_c_runtime_dependencies_are_empty`, `test_a_configured_remotes_are_public_safe`, `test_a2_remote_policy_accepts_credential_free_https_and_ssh`, `test_a3_remote_policy_refuses_credentials_and_local_paths` |
| Documented commands must be real | Every `asi` command line in the docs is parsed against the real argument parser | `cli.py` | `test_b_every_fenced_command_line_parses_completely`, `test_a_inline_flags_in_docs_are_real` |
| Documented refusals must be the ones the CLI prints | Every operator-facing refusal class (`SafetyRefusal` descendants and `LockHeld`) that the code raises is explained in `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, every refusal name the document cites exists in `errors.py`, and the lock path in the docs is the one the code opens; six raised names were missing when this was first measured | `errors.py`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md` | `test_every_raised_operator_facing_refusal_is_documented`, `test_every_documented_refusal_name_is_a_real_error_class`, `test_the_lock_path_in_the_docs_is_the_one_the_code_uses`, `test_controls_for_the_extractors` |
| A crash must not borrow the exit code of a negative answer | An exception no command anticipated is caught last in `main`, named on stderr with its traceback, and exits 70 (`EX_SOFTWARE`), so a scheduler reading 1 always reads a negative answer the tool gave; every declared exit code is distinct and every one, plus argparse's 2, has a row in the OPERATIONS table | `cli.py` | `test_an_unanticipated_exception_is_not_reported_as_a_negative_answer`, `test_the_control_a_refusal_still_exits_3_without_a_traceback`, `test_every_declared_code_is_distinct`, `test_the_exit_code_table_documents_every_declared_code_and_argparse` |
| Every state directory the docs name must exist, and every content-bearing one must have a privacy row | every state subdirectory path cited anywhere in the docs is checked against `StateStore.SUBDIRS`, and every subdirectory except the lock directory (exempted with its reason: the lock holds only a pid) must appear in the asset table of the privacy document, so a reader deciding what is safe to paste has a row for candidates, diagnostics, checkpoints, backups and sandbox scratch, not only for transcripts; the two scheduler log files the packaging templates redirect into the state tree have their own asset row and a mention in the scheduling doc, with the log names read off the templates and pinned to the two expected, so a renamed or added log fails the test | `app/state.py`, `docs/PRIVACY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md`, `packaging/` | `test_every_documented_state_directory_exists`, `test_every_content_bearing_directory_has_a_privacy_row`, `test_the_extractor_sees_the_known_rows`, `test_every_template_log_has_a_privacy_row_and_a_scheduling_mention` |

## The map is checked against the suite

A table of claims about tests is not evidence, and it decays silently: a renamed
test leaves the row reading exactly as it did when it was true. So the map is
verified mechanically, and the verifier is two-sided, because a checker that
found no references at all would also report that every reference resolved.

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A documented test name may be stale | Every test name cited above is required to exist in the suite | `tests/test_acceptance_map.py` | `test_every_test_named_in_the_acceptance_map_exists_in_the_suite`, `test_the_map_references_enough_tests_that_this_check_is_not_vacuous` |
| A cited name must identify one test | No test name may be declared by two modules, or a row points at two different things | `tests/test_acceptance_map.py` | `test_no_test_name_is_declared_by_two_modules` |
| The map may cover only a corner of the suite | Every test module must be cited by at least one row | `tests/test_acceptance_map.py` | `test_every_test_module_in_the_suite_is_cited_by_the_map` |
| A cited source path may not exist | Every backticked artifact in the map is resolved on disk, as a file, a glob or a directory. A path on the host (one that begins with a slash, such as `/var`) names no file in the repository, and whether it exists is a fact about the machine running the suite, so it is not taken for an artifact: measured before, the map's `/private/var/root` resolved on macOS and was reported missing on the Linux CI jobs (3.11, 3.12 and 3.13), so the suite passed on one and failed on the other | `tests/test_acceptance_map.py` | `test_every_artifact_named_in_the_acceptance_map_resolves`, `test_the_artifact_resolver_refuses_a_path_that_does_not_exist`, `test_the_artifact_extractor_leaves_out_a_path_on_the_host` |
| The checker itself may be vacuous | The extractor has a positive control, a prose negative control, and a mutation control that plants a name which does not exist | `tests/test_acceptance_map.py` | `test_the_test_name_extractor_finds_names_that_are_there`, `test_the_test_name_extractor_collects_nothing_from_prose`, `test_a_cited_test_that_does_not_exist_is_reported_missing`, `test_a_cited_test_that_does_exist_is_not_reported_missing` |
| A test may be written and never run | Every test method the loader finds on a `TestCase` under `tests/` must appear in the suite that discovery builds, and the uncollected ones are named; this was measured, not imagined: a hand-listed `load_tests` hook had dropped a whole class | `tests/test_suite_integrity.py` | `test_no_test_method_is_defined_but_uncollected`, `test_nothing_collected_was_not_defined`, `test_discovery_is_not_vacuous` |
| A test may be written on a class the loader ignores | Every class under `tests/` whose body defines a `test_*` method must be collected under its own name | `tests/test_suite_integrity.py` | `test_every_class_that_writes_a_test_is_collected_under_its_name` |
| The suite checker itself may be vacuous | Positive and negative controls for both extractors, and a mutation control that builds a module with a hand-listed hook and confirms the dropped class is reported | `tests/test_suite_integrity.py` | `test_ast_extractor_finds_a_class_with_a_test_and_skips_one_without`, `test_defined_extractor_uses_the_loaders_rules`, `test_mutation_control_an_uncollected_test_is_reported` |
| The oldest type checker the project declares may fail on the source | `pyproject.toml` declares the type checker a contributor installs as a floor (`mypy>=1.8` in the `dev` extra), and CI installs the newest. Measured before (the tree at 16c7a1f, 3.11): mypy 1.8.0, 1.9.0, 1.10.0, 1.11.0 and 1.11.2 each found one error, at `app/wiring.py`, where a conditional expression's two arms join to `object` before 1.12; 1.12.0 and every later one measured (1.13.0 to 1.18.1, 2.3.1, 2.4.1) passed, so the floor was false and nothing checked it. The wiring now assigns the isolation in an `if`, which mypy 1.8.0, 1.11.2 and 2.4.1 pass as on Linux and as on macOS (3.11; 1.8.0 under 3.13 too), and CI checks the source with the declared floor as well (the step "Type gate at the declared floor" in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: `mypy==1.8.0`, then `mypy` and `mypy --platform darwin`). The tests hold that step's pin to the declared floor, so neither moves without the other; both its checks (`mypy`, then `mypy --platform darwin`) after the install; and the step to its name and a `run: |` block of those three lines and nothing else (a line starting with `#` aside: between keys YAML reads it as a comment, and inside the block, indented as its lines, bash passes it over), so it has no `if:`, no `continue-on-error` and no `shell:` however spelled, and no line that lets a check fail (`set +e`, `|| true`): it runs, in the runner's own shell (`bash -e`), and a check that fails fails the job. Its install replaces the newest mypy for every later step of its job, so they hold too that every other step of the job that runs mypy comes before it, and that those steps run both checks: moved before the install, the step passed its other tests, the install kept 1.8.0 (it satisfies `mypy>=1.8`), and CI would never have checked with the newest (measured by audit). A step running mypy is one with a line `mypy` or `mypy ...` in its `run` block, or a key `run: mypy ...` (its first line too, `- run: mypy`). They read `ci.yml` as text in the form it is written, not as YAML or as bash: a step is a line `- <key>:` at the steps' indent, and the text of a `|` or `>` block is passed over when they find the steps, so a step written inside another step's script (a heredoc holding a `steps:` list with the floor's lines, the real step gone) is not taken for one. 22 mutants (the floor in `pyproject.toml` raised to 1.9; the pin raised to 1.12.0; the Linux check dropped, or moved before the install; the macOS check dropped, or moved before the install; the step skipped by an `if:`, by a quoted `"if":`, by an `if:` after the `run` block, or by one after a comment indented as the step's `-`; the step let fail by `continue-on-error`, or by a quoted `'continue-on-error':`; a shell without `-e`; `set +e` at the top of the block; `|| true` after a check; the step moved before the install, or between the newest's two checks; a step running `mypy` after it, or one whose first line is `- run: mypy`; the newest's macOS step dropped; the step gone and its lines written in another step's heredoc, at two indents) are each killed on both Pythons by the test named for it, and four controls pass on both (a comment inside the step's `run` block, and one between this step and the next, neither taken for the step's end; the next step skipped by an `if:`; the pin written `mypy==1.8`); the two checks swapped is refused too, by the test that holds the step to its lines; the wiring put back to a conditional expression is caught by mypy 1.8.0 (one error, as before), not by a test. Not covered by this row: the meaning of the workflow, as against its form: the same step in another of YAML's forms (a flow mapping, a merge key) fails the tests, and a check spelled other than a line `mypy` or `mypy ...` (`python -m mypy`) is not seen by them, while a line `mypy` that bash does not run (one of a heredoc's) is taken for one, so a change to `ci.yml` is still read by hand; a mypy between the floor and the newest (CI runs those two only); a key set for the whole job or workflow (a job's `if:` or `continue-on-error`, `defaults`, `env`), which the workflow has none of and no test holds; and every other step of CI, which no test holds this way beyond the order of the steps that run mypy. | `app/wiring.py`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `pyproject.toml` | `test_ci_installs_the_declared_floor_exactly`, `test_the_floor_checks_the_source_as_on_linux_and_as_on_macos`, `test_the_step_is_its_run_block_and_nothing_else`, `test_the_newest_checks_both_platforms_before_the_floor_replaces_it` |

## Provenance of the review, and one correction to it

The review that set this design was written against a proposal, not against a
tree. One of its statements is therefore stale rather than wrong: it recorded
that the repository did not exist at review time. It does now -- this repository
is the answer to that review -- and every row above is a claim about code that
is present here and a test that runs offline.

Nothing else in the review is treated as stale. Its findings are the acceptance
criteria, including the ones this repository answers by refusing to build the
feature at all; those are listed in the closing section rather than dropped.

## What this repository deliberately does not do

Stated here because a missing feature is otherwise indistinguishable from an
oversight.

- **It does not apply anything on a schedule.** There is no code path from a
  timer to a file write, and `packaging/` prints templates rather than
  installing them.
- **It does not edit rubrics.** Not on the daily path, not on the apply path.
  Changing a rubric is a human edit to a protected file, on purpose.
- **It does not score continuous quality.** The verdict space is three discrete
  values, because a 0-to-1 score with no inter-rater reliability was one of the
  review's named risks.
- **It does not read raw tool-output logs**, which the review identified as the
  only place the missing evidence exists and simultaneously as the least
  curated, highest-sensitivity artifact on the machine. That trade is left to a
  human decision rather than made silently by a daily job.
- **It does not claim the mirror problem is solved.** It refuses to write into an
  ambiguous tree and reports the divergence. Choosing a canonical source is a
  human act.
